It runs in a second field.worker, spawned for the check and terminated by any newer change or by its own answer, and not in the preview's own field.worker as first planned: a worker's synchronous job can only be stopped by ending the worker, and ending that one would throw away its stage cache, so the next change would parse and orient the model again. `idle()` does not wait for it. On x8 3 x 3 it takes 1.50 to 1.55 s in Node, 1.38 s in Chromium and 1.62 s in Firefox, and finds a thinnest wall of 2.50 mm. Goddess 2 x 1 at spacing 10 merges and at 11 is too thin, as M2 measured, and VIS-35 2 x 1 at spacing 10 now warns before export.
- **G2.6 before T2:** the two-level warning needs the footprint, which needs Manifold, so solid.worker reports the levels with the footprint as an output of S2. It rides in the import's job, so a load still takes two solid jobs, and the pipeline counts it as part of what shows: the badge stays up until it is in. "Use upper level" is offered only from a report for the plane on screen, and the plane choice carries the offset of the level it offered, which S2 stands on. Only that report knows which level silicone can reach: S2 first took the level with the most area, which on the barrel's +Z face is a sealed recess at 0.20 mm (129 mm², none of it exposed) and not the 0.50 mm level offered (40 mm² exposed). A named level that a changed tolerance no longer finds leaves the model on the plane itself, with a warning. Choosing the plane already chosen is no change, so it keeps the pins and removed vents.
- **Candidates' triangles on main:** the hover highlight needs the triangles of every candidate, not only the chosen one, so S2 sends them all. A pick that lands inside a ranked plane lights that whole plane, as `candidateFromPick` takes it; anywhere else the highlight is the triangles within the plane tolerance of the hovered one's plane and facing its way. Picking runs on a BVH of the mesh the pipeline holds, built on the first hover, never on the display proxy, so a picked index is a real triangle.
- **Display proxy:** made with the import in solid.worker, which has meshoptimizer. No example passes 300k triangles (Benchy has 225,154), so a unit test covers it on a closed box of 307,200.
- **Silicone figure:** S9 arrives with M7, so the sidebar and status strip show the plan's volume on the preview field: the integral of H with the preview's TIN raise, less the model above the plane per tile, plus the bores, and no sealed air yet. S2 now also gives the model's volume above the plane. At defaults it reads, in ml, Benchy 43.32, Goddess 118.39, VIS-35 530.02, jersey 11.53, x8 89.52, barrel 10.52, wolf 207.41, labelled as from the preview. Against M3's export-cell figures with M4's bores added that is +0.45%, +0.37%, -2.07%, +1.71%, +2.39%, +2.53% and +2.02%; M6a first wrote "within 2.5%", which the barrel misses. The gap is the preview's own cell and TIN raise (0.5 mm against 0.125, and 0.1 against 0.05): at the export's, the same sum gives the barrel 10.269 against 10.256 and jersey 11.359 against 11.340, within 0.2%. `test/unit/derived.test.ts` pins the seven figures at 0.1%, holds them within 3% of M3's, and the barrel and jersey within 0.5% at the export's cell and raise.
- **Box:** the status strip's box is the cover's outer box O by the height from the base's underside to the flat top, `Zt + Tb`, and the browser test compares it with `deriveRim(moldBounds(...))` run in Node. T1 shows the cover and base as plain boxes from the rim (O from `-Tb + proud` to `Zt`, B from `-Tb` to 0) until a cover or base built on that same rim arrives; the pipeline now keeps the rim key each part was built on.
- **Clearance** reads "≥ t, planned" until an export measures it, which is M6b's. The plan's word was "nominal"; the review found it, "export cell" and "minGap" opaque to a maker, so the strip, the Grid section and the Diagnostics card now say "planned" and "full export detail".
- **Diagnostics' places:** core's diagnostics carry no location, and M6a leaves core as it is, so the Show toggle reads the place from the "at (x, y)" that every message about an opening, a peak or a cavity already names, on the first tile.
- **Advice on t:** the parameter table's warning under 3 mm and advice of 4 to 5 mm above 100 mm of cavity had no code, so the app gives them under the t slider. VIS-35, at 134 mm, gets the advice.
- **Typed entries:** a parameter with no minimum in the schema took a typed 0, and a preview cell of 0 crashed field.worker, which then counted as an engine crash and doubled the cell until the next load; a layer of 0 made the pocket that follows it 0 deep. The controls now refuse 0 where it means nothing, with a floor of their own in `src/ui/layout.ts` (cells 0.05, layer 0.05, the pocket's own minimum, nozzle 0.1, wall 0.4, TIN tolerances 0.01), the schema is unchanged, field.worker answers a cell that is not above 0 with an inline error and no retry, and a pocket following a layer outside the pocket's range of 0.05 to 2 mm is a cross-parameter error.
- **Wheel:** a row's slider or number box steps on the wheel only once it has focus. Over any box the pointer passed, it ate the wheel, so scrolling the sidebar down to the lower sections stepped every box that scrolled under the pointer.
- **Clicks and drops:** only a press and release on the canvas pick a face, so a click on a card over the model, such as a part's eye, never picks what lies behind it. A drop anywhere on the page loads the file, where the browser would otherwise open or save it. A file that does not parse says why under Open STL, with no parts card and no badge. Leaving the page with a model loaded asks first, through the browser's own prompt (not `alert()`), since nothing is saved until M9, and the developer pages at the foot of Advanced open in a tab of their own. Advanced's summary counts the rows inside with a note, as it is closed by default.
- **Test time:** `scripts/ci.sh` takes 5.9 minutes and `--full` 17.6, against about 8 and 18. The app's 14 smoke tests run in all three projects; the drop of all seven and the timed wall check run only with `CFG_FULL=1`, on the build in Chromium and in Firefox, as the dev server serves the same code. The review screenshots run only with `CFG_SHOTS=1`.
- **Overflow check:** "no element with scrollWidth > clientWidth" measures boxes that have a client width. By the CSSOM spec an inline box has a clientWidth of 0, and Firefox still gives such a span its text's scrollWidth, so 57 unit labels failed a check over every element there and none in Chromium.
- **M6b, one path for every button:** Export ZIP, Cover, Base, Funnel and Fit test all run T3 in a fresh solid worker, as M5 built it, and differ only in what they leave out. The cover alone builds no base. The base alone still builds and measures the cover, since a raise of the margin moves the rim of both. The funnel and the fit test need no model, so they send none. A part that the last export of the same geometry already holds is saved from it with no worker, so Cover after Export ZIP is instant and byte for byte the ZIP's file, which a unit test and the browser test both hold to, the browser's by the solid workers the page spawned. Only the last export is kept: after a ZIP at 3 mm and a cover at 3.5, the cover at 3 mm is built again, and the same as the ZIP's.
- **M6b, waiting for the preview:** the export places no openings of its own, it takes the preview's, so a click while the preview catches up waits for it, with "Waiting for the preview to finish" in the stage bar and Cancel already live. A cancel during that wait starts no worker. A new file during the wait ends the export with no file and says why, as the model it was asked for is gone; the name it would have been saved under was that model's. Once its worker runs, the export goes on with the model it was given, as M5 has it. After the wait, a mold export checks the buttons' rule again, since the rim's errors (such as a ridge that comes to nothing, from Ridge width 0.8 and height 4, both within the sliders) come with the field the wait was for, and the buttons went by what showed at the click.
- **M6b, the export's own worker:** it gets the bootstrap rule's 20 s too: a worker that has not said ready or reported a step by then is terminated, and the export ends with an inline error and no retry, as the stage bar would otherwise read "Starting the geometry engine" for good. A crash on the retry at the coarser cell is said in a maker's words, with what to do, and the engine's own message in brackets.
- **M6b, Cancel:** the browser test checks the worker itself, through a record of every solid worker the page spawns with `?test=1`: Cancel must call its terminate(), and it sends nothing after. A second hold of 1.5 s is cancelled, and nothing arrives in the 3.5 s after, which a worker left running would have filled. The stage bar takes Export ZIP's place with Cancel at its right end, so Cancel takes no click in its first 400 ms, which is the second click of a double-click; a browser test clicks Cancel 80 ms after Export ZIP and sees the export finish, and fails without the guard. The focus goes to Cancel when the bar comes, as the button pressed has gone, and back to Export ZIP when it goes.
- **M6b, "current":** the measured clearance, the files the single parts reuse and what only the export found belong to the geometry they were made from, which is the export tier's keys of S13, S15 and S16 with the openings the export was given. A change of the mold leaves them behind, and changing it back brings them back, as the geometry is then the same. The status strip says "clearance measured 3.21 mm" while they hold, "≥ t, unverified" after the export of an open mesh, which has no solid to measure, as the risk table asks, and "≥ t, planned" otherwise. The saved file's outcome line follows the same keys: after a change, including one made while the export ran, it reads "Saved X, made with the settings before your last change. Export again for the current ones." A file saved although the export found an error of its own says "Saved X, but the export found an error", in the error style.
- **M6b, what only the export finds:** a failure, and every warning or error of the export whose code the preview does not show itself, such as `margin-raised`, `clearance-short`, `export-coarser` or `export-opening-invalid`. They show in the Export box and in Diagnostics, marked "Found by the last export", and never turn the buttons off, as exporting again is how to see them change; only the preview's own errors do. M5 gave `margin-raised` the severity info, and M6b makes it a warning, since the mold it describes is larger than the preview shows. No example raises the margin at defaults, so a unit test makes it raise on jersey with Print margin -0.6 mm, which the number box takes: the first build measures 2.535, the margin goes up by 0.5150 to 3.0506, and the box from 76.32 x 41.71 x 32.38 to 77.37 x 42.76 x 32.90 mm. The rebuild shows in the stage bar as one step, "Building again with a wider margin", where the clearance stands, so the bar never runs backwards, and decimating the model for minGap counts as measuring. `clearance-short`, under t after the raise, no input reaches, so only its display is tested, by an injected reply. The clippedNodes fault ends the export with its message, as M5 has it, now in a maker's words. A test injects an engine crash at export and sees the coarser retry's warning in both places and no dialog.
- **M6b, the buttons when off:** the M6a style faded the accent fill to 45%, which still read as the button to press in the dark theme: 2.37 to 1 against the panel, measured by the browser test. A disabled button now has the panel's second fill, a dashed border and muted text, about 1.12 to 1 in the dark theme and 1.19 in the light one, computed from the palette, where an enabled Export ZIP stands at about 6 to 7. The test holds disabled under 1.5 and enabled over 3 in both themes, and fails on the M6a style. Each button's reason is its tooltip and a line under the buttons, the funnel's own reason included. With no model, all five are off, the fit test too, as M6b asks, although it needs no model; diag.html still offers the fit test without one.
- **M6b, model_oriented.stl:** it keeps the file's own triangles, so it keeps the file's own edges too. The barrel's file has 12 non-manifold edges, and so has its model_oriented.stl; open edges are 0 on all 7, as the check asks. Every part the app builds reads back with 0 open, 0 non-manifold and 0 misoriented edges. Its triangle count equals the input less the collapsed triangles on all 7 (Benchy 225,154 of 225,706).
- **M6b, the browser test's reassembly:** it measures the full model, not the export's decimated copy, with `minGap(model, cover, t + 0.5)`. That reads the true gap whenever it is under t + 0.5, which is all a check of ">= t" needs, and takes 2.9 s on Benchy where a search of 8 takes 29 s. Mode A uses the model_oriented.stl from the ZIP itself, Mode B the model oriented in Node. Cover and base overlap by under 0.1 mm³ on all 9 molds, and touch at the shelf (minGap under 0.01 with a search of 0.05). As minGap returns no more than its search, the gap must also be under t + 0.5, and within a float step of the export's own figure, widened by the decimation's error, so a cover put back far from the model fails. The browser's report.json, all but its timings, and README.txt equal Node's on every mold, grids with the wall check, which the Node build then waits for as the app does.
- **M6b, the molds checked:** all 7 at defaults, the barrel in Mode B, which fills its void, and jersey on a 2 x 3 grid, in Chromium on the build; jersey and wolf, the heaviest field, in Firefox. The plan names no model for the grid. Benchy 2 x 3, which M3 and M4 used, would add about two minutes of minGap in the browser and again in Node, and M3 already covers that grid in Node.
- **M6b, measured at export (clearance after the decimation error, silicone in ml):** Benchy 3.2136 and 43.2, Goddess 3.2231 and 118.0, VIS-35 3.2232 and 541.4, jersey 3.2406 and 11.4, x8 3.2262 and 87.6, barrel 3.2189 and 10.3, wolf 3.2104 and 203.5; the barrel in Mode B 3.2189, jersey 2 x 3 3.2262 and 68.2 ml. Only Benchy passes 60,000 triangles, and its decimated copy's error is 0.0066 mm, against M3's full-model gap of 3.2178. No margin was raised. The silicone here is the plan's figure at the export cell, `moldSilicone` with the bores, so it takes off sealed air, which the sidebar's preview figure does not yet. To four places, as the browser test pins them at 0.1%: Benchy 43.1771, Goddess 118.0047, VIS-35 541.4064, jersey 11.3591, x8 87.5913, barrel 10.2682 (also in Mode B) and jersey 2 x 3 68.1545 ml, of which the bores are 0.3610, 0.3341, 0.2743, 0.1904, 1.5700, 0.2054, 1.0454 and 1.1422. Against M4's bores at the export cell those are within 0.4%, but Benchy's is 7% under its 0.389: the export cuts the openings where the preview placed them on its own field, as M5 has it, and on Benchy's chimney that is not M4's spot. The unit test pins jersey's to M4's within 1%.
- **M6b, README.txt:** plain ASCII, CRLF line endings and lines of at most 76 characters, which a unit test holds on long names, Polish letters and a name longer than a line, and the browser test on every mold. A model's name loses its accents, takes the nearest plain letter where there is one (ł to l) and '?' elsewhere, and a word longer than a line breaks where the line ends. The plan's "parameters" are a SETTINGS section at the end: every setting by its sidebar label, under the Advanced section's group names, and each auto one with what it came to in this export; only the preview's own cell says "for the preview only". A Mode A grid says how many models to print and glue. The warnings are the export's own and what main knows that the export does not find again (the file's, the plane's and its levels', the openings', the walls checked at the export cell, and the advice on t), without the card's marks, so a crash of the preview, which doubles its cell, is not written into the mold's README. The fit-test pieces keep the README core writes for them, with LF endings, which Notepad on Windows 10 and 11 shows as well; it now says to set the taper clearance under Cover & joint and save the fit test again with the Fit test button, with diag.html as the other way.
- **M6b, the funnel in the view:** hidden at first, as a separate print; its eye shows one on every pour, and the eye's test now expects it to start off.
- **M6b, settings that wait for M7:** Bed X, Y and Z, Plastic density, Tape strip holds and Underside persistence feed nothing but S9's key until M7's reports read them, and Infill only README.txt's advice, so Advanced says so under Printer and Reports, and a box larger than the bed (x8 3 x 3, 393.2 x 290.9 mm, on 220 x 220) gets its warning with M7's printability checks. Infill stays a fraction in its row, as the schema has it, and the note says so: showing it in percent would need a scale in every control's slider, box, wheel and reset.
- **M6b, export time** in the browser's worker, from report.json's own total, in Chromium on the build: Benchy 11.5 s, Goddess 5.4, VIS-35 2.6, jersey 0.38, x8 3.1, barrel 2.4, wolf 1.9, the barrel in Mode B 2.4 and jersey 2 x 3 1.8 s; in Firefox jersey 0.34 and wolf 2.1 s. minGap takes most of it: Benchy's decimated copy against its cover takes about 11 s with the search of 8 that M3 set.
- **M6b, test time:** `scripts/ci.sh` takes 7.6 minutes (455 s) and `--full` 21.2 (1272 s), against about 8 and 24, both with a display for Firefox (`DISPLAY` set), so its WebGL tests ran too. The three Export box tests run in all three projects, Firefox only with a display: without xvfb or `DISPLAY` they skip there, as every WebGL test does, and `scripts/ci.sh` says so at the end. The nine molds run in Chromium on the build only with `CFG_FULL=1`, and jersey and wolf in Firefox, with a display too. Each mold's Node build runs while the browser builds its own, and the browser test measures the reassembly in Node. Swap stayed at 635 MB throughout.

### M7: Analyses and diagnostics

Checks:
- Synthetic: ring and block with hole `cut-required` with two mouths; mushroom `jacket-out` with hint at the stem; arch has loop lock and a hint between the legs; inverted cup sealed domain 130.5 within 3%.
- Real: sealed domains barrel 3.43 within 3%, others 0; Benchy underside 0.53 ml within 25%; tiny hits filtered; loop flag wolf and Benchy yes, x8 no.
- Verdicts: jersey and x8 `plausible`; wolf `cut-required`; Goddess and VIS-35 not plausible. Any non-plausible verdict carries a hint.
- Lift on an analytic box within 2%. Clamp advice at least 10 N, with strip count shown. VIS-35 warns on default bed. Pocket 0.1 at 0.2 layers warns.
- UI: wolf shows `cut-required`, and its Show toggle changes pixels at the hint. Screenshots reviewed.

Measured at M7, where a rule above reads differently on the real models:
- **Where S8 and S9 run:** in a third worker, a field.worker of their own with a JobGate of its own, asked for only once the whole preview for the current keys is on screen and the solid worker is idle, so a drag or a burst of changes asks once, at its end. Asked for as soon as placement landed, as first built, S8 ran beside the first T2 of a model and slowed it in Chromium by 60 to 100 ms on this 4-core server (wolf 447 to 462 ms with the reports off, 538 to 565 with them on; Firefox by up to 20 ms). Started after the solids, the first T2 reads 454 to 477 ms on wolf, 546 to 581 on Benchy and 2.57 to 2.68 s on x8 3 x 3 in Chromium, against 447 to 462, 547 to 590 and 2.61 to 2.64 s with them off (`?reports=0`, a switch for this measurement), and T1 and the T2 after a change of t are the same either way. It keeps S8 under its key, which is the oriented mesh and the analysis cell, so a change of t or of a filter runs S9 alone, and the mesh goes to it once per model and plane. Run in the preview's own field.worker, as first planned, a change made while S8 ran would wait for it; a browser test holds a reports job for 6 s and sees the next change's field and solids land meanwhile, in Chromium and in Firefox, with T1 under 150 ms. `idle()` waits for the reports, and the export's wait for the preview does not: the export works out its own S8 and S9 on its own field, in its zip step, for README.txt and report.json, and a unit test saves a ZIP while the reports job is held back. A failed report is a warning in Diagnostics and never blocks an export. The export's own S8 and S9 are guarded the same way, by the same cell check and a catch: a failure leaves README.txt a note in place of the estimate, the steps that suit any model, the tape still counted from the field, `reports: null` with the reason in report.json, and no sealed air taken off the silicone; an S8 that reads no shell from a mesh with triangles counts as a failure, so no verdict comes from nothing (a negative cell read 0 shells and gave "pull out in the cover" before).
- **What S9 reads:** besides the plan's column, the island filter (`base.minIsland`), which keeps footprint crumbs from making a loop lock, `funnel.generate`, `funnel.height` and `seat.enabled` for the lift's level, and the preview's TIN tolerance, its raise of the ceiling. The printability checks are arithmetic on S10's rim, so main works them out whenever it draws, from the first field on, with the reports on, off (`?reports=0`) or failed; the export works them out on its own rim. So Bed X, Y and Z, Infill and Plastic density are read by no worker stage: `MAIN_ONLY` in `stages.ts` lists them, the stage test checks they change no stage's output, and Diagnostics rows main works out are never shown again from an older export. The stage test's real changes now also run Benchy with the smallest air pocket depth at 3 mm, past its pocket's 2.5, and wolf with an island filter of 200 mm2, and check each change against the table, not only against the outputs.
- **Intervals:** free stretches thinner than a cell are left out, as M2 did, and a gap that thin between two solids joins them. Solid stretches connect by any overlap, not a cell, so a sheet of the model thinner than a cell still holds its shell together; free ones by a cell, as the plan says. A footprint column is one whose lowest solid starts on the plane, or within a cell of it, and footprints are those columns joined by a side. A sandwiched body's mouths are the columns where it meets other silicone, joined by their 8 neighbours, as the rim of a round hole steps diagonally. S8 reads the whole mesh, voids included, and a sealed domain whose stretches are bounded by a void shell is marked as inside the model. Columns where the model stands over air the plate seals, such as an engraving in its underside, join the footprint patches round them, as G2.6 treats a level inside the footprint's holes: no silicone reaches between such patches. A sandwiched body's walls are its columns where, on a side, the model closes it off instead of more free space, in groups joined by their 8 neighbours and at least 2 mm long, as the closed end of a blind hole or the ring round a post; shorter groups are where a wedge of free space thins to under a cell, 1 to 5 columns at 0.25 mm and 1 to 8 at 0.125 mm on the seven, where the shortest group counted is Benchy's, 8 columns at 0.25 mm and 17 at 0.125 mm. Each body has a point of its own, the one nearest its centroid on one of its columns, at least half a cell inside its stretch there, which the hints use: the centroid of a ring or of silicone wrapped round a post lies in the model (the barrel's under-plug's in its sealed hollow, Goddess's largest sandwiched body's in her solid), and a test checks every hint of the seven by the parity of a ray against the model less its voids.
- **Verdicts:** per body, which is an interval shell past the smallest reported volume, and the model's verdict is the worst of its bodies; a grid's copies are alike, so the report says so and its hints are on the first tile. The plan's "both classes under 1 ml" totals the silicone bodies past the 0.05 ml filter, each class on its own (0.6 ml of each is plausible, exactly 1 ml is not), so every verdict other than plausible carries a hint by construction, and the tests check it on every model. The plan's "sandwiched silicone touching 2 or more mouths" misses silicone that runs round a part of the model joined to itself on both sides of it, such as Benchy's cabin, entered by the door and left by the windows round its corner posts: its roof's overhang makes all its contact one ring, so 1 mouth. So 2 walls or more hold in a loop too, as 2 mouths do, since on a single edge mouths and walls alternate; a roof on one post, a table, has 1 wall and stays jacket-out, as the mushroom does. A loop lock's hint goes to the largest under-plug body under its shell, which gets no hint of its own (the arch's, between its legs), and its seam, like every hint's, runs from the nearest edge of the cavity straight in to the hint at its height. Hints that hold in a loop come first and are marked Cut, and only they say to cut; the rest say to stretch the silicone off there, or to cut only if it would tear, and on a plausible model to flex it there if the model holds. The steps under the report and in README.txt follow the verdict: on plausible the base comes out with the model and the silicone stays in the cover unless the model holds; otherwise the silicone slides out with the model, to stretch off or to cut at the places marked Cut. With no funnel seat, the sprues are cut flush with the top. The words measure from the middle of the model, with the front at -y, the side the app's first view faces, and the air under Benchy's cabin roof says where in the same words.
- **Measured at defaults** (silicone in ml): Benchy `cut-required`, one shell on one patch of 464.6 mm2, under-plug 6.94, sandwiched 8.48, of which 8.40 in its cabin with 1 mouth and 6 walls; Goddess `cut-required`, 24.9 and 16.7, its two largest sandwiched bodies held in a loop, 16.2 ml with 7 walls and 0.51 ml with 2; VIS-35 `cut-required`, 262.4 and 29.2, with 4 sandwiched bodies held in a loop, the larger two 22.5 ml with 5 walls and 5.5 ml with 2 mouths; jersey `plausible`, 0.010 ml in 4 slivers under the filter; x8 `plausible`, 8 bodies on one footprint each, 0.078 and 0.073 ml in slivers; the barrel `jacket-out`, 1.035 ml under its bulge, just over the 1 ml line, a model the plan gives no verdict; wolf `cut-required`, one shell on 2533.5, 160.5 and 152.6 mm2, its 6 crumbs under 5 mm2 now joined to the largest patch by the recesses the plate seals, 66.2 and 14.7 ml, 2 sandwiched bodies held in a loop, with 16 hints. `test/regression/golden.ts` freezes these as REPORT_GOLDEN, and a unit test reads them through the app's own path too, S9 in its worker on the preview.
- **The plan's "loop flag Benchy yes" does not hold:** Benchy's second footprint patch, 8.31 mm2 at (-5.9, 0.0), is the inside of a letter of the engraving under its hull, ringed by the 0.30 mm recess that S8 itself finds sealed (4.5 mm3 at x -8.1 to -3.8, y -2.9 to 2.9). No silicone gets between the two patches, so there is no base loop lock; M1's footprint count of 2 is right, and the lock read off it was an artefact, which also flipped with the island filter (cut-required at 5 mm2, jacket-out at 9). Benchy is `cut-required` all the same, for the silicone round its cabin's posts, which the plan's rule did not see. A synthetic block with an engraved ring round an island is one patch and no lock, and with a groove out to its side two patches and a lock.
- **Air:** Benchy's one pocket is 0.523 ml, 2.50 mm deep, at (9.4, -6.6), 36.6 mm up, against the plan's 0.53, under its cabin roof: the report says "under an overhang of the model", with the height and the side from the middle, and not "under the model", which reads as the face on the base. The tiny hits S8 finds and the filters leave out: Benchy's 8 sealed letters (3 to 5 mm3) and its second pocket; wolf's 18 sealed recesses, up to 13 mm3, and 71 pockets, none past both filters; Goddess's 20 pockets, the deepest 0.62 mm but under 0.001 ml, single columns at points of its surface. The barrel's void reads 3.432 ml, inside the model, and no other model seals anything past the filter. Its row says it is a sealed hollow, in ml with a Show, without the rising-level note, which has nothing to do with a void; the import's own row on that void, in mm3, goes once S9 has found a hollow inside the model, in the card and in README.txt. The inverted cup reads 130.47 ml, and the cup with a 6 mm hole in its wall traps 54.95 ml over the hole, against the closed form's 54.97.
- **Silicone:** the figure takes off S8's sealed air that is not inside the model, as the model's volume already holds a void. No example seals air past the filter, so no figure moves: the preview's M6 figures stand to 0.1% (a unit test now reads them with the reports on), and the export's to four places (Benchy 43.1771, Goddess 118.0047, VIS-35 541.4064, jersey 11.3591, x8 87.5913, barrel 10.2682 ml). `sealedDomains` and `moldSilicone` now come from S8; an open box standing on the plate shows the preview's figure falling by its 19.65 ml of air.
- **Lift and tape:** the plan does not define Zf. The app takes the rim of a full funnel, Zt plus the funnel's height, when the mold has funnel seats and funnels, as a maker may fill one to the brim, and Zt otherwise. Lift on the preview at defaults: Benchy 1.28 N, Goddess 1.56, VIS-35 4.26, jersey 0.55, x8 4.57, barrel 0.29 and wolf 10.98 N, so the tape holds 10 N, 1 strip, on all but wolf, which needs 22.0 N, 3 strips; x8 3 x 3 lifts with 41.1 N, 82.2 N to hold, 9 strips. The cover's weight is shown beside the lift, from its printed mass, and the advice does not count it, as tape also has to load the seal. **Added at review:** one strip cannot press the seal all round a box, nor one line of it VIS-35's 230.6 mm, so the strips also go no more than 100 mm apart along any wall, at least one each way across the base, an estimate, and the count is the larger of the two: Benchy, Goddess, jersey and the barrel 2, VIS-35 4 (1 front to back, 3 left to right), x8 4, wolf 4 (3 for its lift), x8 3 x 3 9 (7 for the seal).
- **Printability:** each part is checked in its print pose, turned on the bed by any angle about the vertical axis, searched in quarter degrees, which agrees with Carver's closed form for a rectangle in a rectangle. VIS-35's cover, 63.6 x 230.6 x 143.1 mm, and its base, 57.3 x 224.3 x 5.2, do not fit the default 220 x 220 x 250 straight, which is the plan's "about 230 mm", but both fit turned about 45 degrees ((230.6 + 63.6) / sqrt 2 = 208.0 mm, and 199.1), so each warns to turn it in the slicer, and README.txt now forbids flipping a part, not turning it. x8 3 x 3's cover is 393.2 x 290.9 mm, which fits no way; one copy would, so the warning says to use fewer copies, and shows under Columns and Rows as well as Bed X. A part that fits no way says what would help. In Mode B the base prints with the model on it, so its height is Tb plus the model's. The plastic warns once the print takes more than a 1 kg spool, which at defaults only wolf does (1.27 kg); the plan lists the mass among the warnings without a threshold. The plastic is the parts' volume at the plastic's density, and at the infill with a solid skin of three nozzle lines, which README.txt asks for. **The plan's "wolf about 2 kg solid" reads 3.10 kg:** its cover alone is 2.43 l, M3's 121.2 x 142.4 x 171.4 mm box less the cavity, the base 0.06 l and the funnel 2.8 ml; at 35% it is 1.27 kg. The plan's figure came from the rough code, and the box it should come from is M3's golden, so the test pins 3.1 kg. Benchy takes 328 g solid and 165 g printed. The layer checks and Mode B's model overhang are M3's, unchanged; the ridge check is new.
- **Times:** S8 in Node takes 19 ms on jersey, 33 the barrel, 74 x8, 75 Goddess, 139 Benchy, 145 wolf and 173 VIS-35, and 82 ms on x8 3 x 3, as S8 is per tile; S9 takes 0.1 to 1.2 ms on one tile and 2.2 ms on x8 3 x 3. The export's own reports add 15 to 229 ms to its zip step. In the browser, from the `CFG_FULL` run of all seven, in Chromium and then Firefox: S8 66 and 41 ms on jersey, 73 and 47 the barrel, 157 and 111 Goddess, 169 and 163 x8, 196 and 185 Benchy, 281 and 202 wolf, 261 and 194 VIS-35, and 254 and 210 ms on x8 3 x 3, beside its wall check; S9 1 to 16 ms. Each runs once per model and plane, after the preview, in its own worker.
- **Test time:** `scripts/ci.sh` takes 8.5 minutes (508 s, from 7.6) and `--full` 21.2 to 22.3 (1272 to 1336 s, from 21.2), against about 8.5 and 24. The first `--full` run lost one click on Cancel in M6b's Export smoke test, in Chromium (the stage bar stayed up for 30 s); it passed in the quick run before it, in 8 runs on its own in Chromium and Firefox, and in the next `--full` run, and its cause is not found. The real-model sweeps of the reports run in Node on all seven in the quick suite, as S8 takes under 0.2 s a model; in the browser they ride on the `CFG_FULL` runs of all seven, which now also hold the reports to Node's. The new app tests run in all three projects. Swap stayed at 649 MB.
- **README.txt:** a DEMOLDING estimate with the verdict, its meaning, the places to cut and then those where the silicone only holds, before the steps for that verdict; an AIR section with the rising-level note; the tape as strips with the lift it answers; the plastic under Printing. When funnels collide, the pour step says to print the smaller top diameter the warning gives, or to pour through one funnel at a time, instead of a funnel in every seat. Advanced's notes under Printer and Reports now say what those settings feed.
- **UI at review:** the Diagnostics card keeps its height of half the viewport, and a line under a list longer than the card counts the rows below and scrolls to them, as Windows' overlay scrollbars may not show at all; the layout test checks that every row cut off is counted. "Underside persistence" and "Vent persistence" read "Smallest air pocket depth" and "Smallest peak to vent". The tape advice has a review shot of its own (`wolf-cover`).

### M8: Section, explode, opening drag, undo

Checks:
- Section snaps and Flip change pixels. Patch sampling by hue at a known cut shows cover, silicone and model caps. Soup-mode fixture shows a no-cap note.
- Explode runs no worker job.
- Drag a pour and a vent in 3D using `worldToScreen`: store updates, invalid drop snaps, pin survives a t change, T2b result arrives, and all grid copies move.
- 20-edit undo and redo returns identical params and geometry hash. Grouping holds. Keyboard shortcuts work. Esc leaves pick mode.

User: drag feel with a real mouse and GPU.

Measured at M8, where a rule above reads differently in the app:
- **Caps:** each closed part's count runs and its cap is drawn before the next part's count starts, all after every other opaque draw and before the see-through cover and silicone, and each cap leaves the stencil at 0 behind it (`src/view/section.ts`, written from the idea of the MIT three.js example, not its code). Caps are unlit, in the part's own colour, with a screen-space hatch of our own at an angle per part, so a cut reads the same from any distance. The silicone has no mesh of its own, so its solid is the preview's height field closed from below by the lattice's rectangle at z = 0, with the model counted as taken away, at every tile, and its cap is drawn where the count is exactly 1: the cut shows the silicone's thickness round the model, and a hole where the model is when the model is hidden. A plane keeps the side away from the first view's camera (x below the plane, y above it, z below it), so the cut faces the viewer; Flip keeps the other side. The position slider and the handle move the plane across the mold's outer box, and the handle drags along its axis, which looking along the axis cannot do, so the test drags it from an angle.
- **Caps, at review:** the model the silicone's count takes away is its mesh less its void shells (`exteriorMesh`, as S2's volume uses), so a hollow sealed inside the model is not silicone. Main works that mesh out the first time a cut needs it, once per model: 21 to 28 ms on Benchy and 1.4 to 2.2 ms on the barrel in Node, which drops the barrel's 1,262 void triangles. Counted from the whole mesh, as M8 first did, the barrel's 3.43 ml hollow read as a teal block of about 16 x 23 mm inside a ring of model on all three default cuts. The model's own cap keeps the whole mesh, so the hollow shows as a hole in it. Air the plate seals under the model is still read as silicone, as only S8's columns know its shape: Benchy's 8 letters (3 to 5 mm³, 0.3 mm deep), wolf's 13 recesses (up to 13 mm³, 0.917 mm deep), and an inverted cup's air, which no example has. Seen from the side kept, as after Flip in the first view, a ray reaches the plane through a part's front faces and every count comes out negated, so the silicone's cap asks for -1 (255 in the 8-bit stencil) there; asking for 1 left the silicone out from that side. While a box stands in for the cover until T2 builds it on the rim on screen, as during a drag of t, wall or the base's thickness, its cut is the box less the field's solid, which goes up with the box when exploded; as a plain box, drawn last at the same depth, it painted the whole cut in the cover's colour. Caps now draw with a strict depth test, so if two parts' counts ever cover one pixel the one drawn first keeps it. The handle turns yellow under the pointer before a press, as its own note said, and has a dark rim, as near-white on the light cover cap read at about 1.3 to 1. Pick a face goes through the cut-away half to the first hit on the side kept, and its highlights are cut too, where M8 first picked and lit the half the cut had taken away.
- **Open meshes:** a mesh the import could not make a solid of (soup mode) gets no model cap, and no silicone cap either, since the silicone's inner surface is that same open mesh; the section says so in one line. The cover, base and funnel are Manifold's and always closed. A shown funnel gets its cap.
- **The model on every tile:** M6 drew the model on the first tile only. A cut through any other tile would then have shown silicone where the model is, so the model is now drawn on every tile from one geometry; picking and the base highlights stay on the first. The copies and the rings stand on the export's pitch, as G6 says, while the preview's own tiles sit up to a preview cell per tile wider (G6's x8 125.0 against 124.75), so on a grid a cut through a far tile shows the model off-centre in its silicone by that drift: 0.25 mm at jersey 2 x 1's second tile, 0.50 x 0.25 mm at x8 3 x 3's last, none on Benchy 2 x 3, and 5.10 mm at jersey 20 x 1's twentieth (cell 0.952 mm), past r_eff of 3.366, where the model reaches into the preview cover's wall. The preview's bores drift against its cavity the same way. Compositing each preview tile at the export offset's nearest node would bound it to half a cell, but changes S5 for every grid preview and the tests that pin G6's wider preview field, so it is left open here.
- **Explode:** the cover with its rings goes up by the distance, the base down by it, and the funnels up by twice it, so they come off the cover as the cover comes off the cavity. The model and the silicone stay. It starts at a quarter of the box's height and goes to one and a half times it. A press finds a ring at its exploded place, and the caps move with their parts. At review: the distance follows the box until the slider moves, goes back to it on a double-click of the slider and on every load, and is held to the slider's range; M8 first kept a distance from one model to the next, past the next one's slider. In Mode B the base carries the model, so exploded the drawn model hides and the base's own copy goes down with it, and the silicone keeps a hole where it stood; M8 first showed the model twice.
- **Drag:** a press on a ring, on any tile, is taken on its way down to the canvas, so the orbit controls never see it, and they are off until the release. The ring moves once the pointer has moved 5 px, so a click pins nothing. Pointer moves apply once a frame. On main a move takes 1.10 ms in Chromium and 2.00 ms in Firefox on x8 3 x 3 (median of 21: the hard rules on all 9 copies, the drag's state, the stage keys of the T2b request), within the plan's one frame, and a browser test holds it under 16 ms. The T2b cuts during that drag took 1152 then 266 and 236 ms in Chromium and 1412 then 242 ms in Firefox, as M5 measured. The reason shown is core's own wording of the first hard rule broken. Esc during a drag puts the opening back and records nothing. At review: each move also runs the drop's own call (`dropOpening`, its snap only while the spot is invalid), so the tip says where a drop lands and a faint ring marks that place on every tile; the plan's soft rules, a long bore, a first pour off its body's peak and its own funnel colliding, run on the spot it lands on and show in the tip, and after the drop in its row under Pour & funnel and in a note at the top of the view, which also says when it snapped or found no place. The drop now passes the body's peak, which M8 first left out, so the off-peak rule never ran at a drop. The funnel rule counts only pairs with the moved pour in them. With all that, a move on x8 3 x 3 takes 1.10 to 2.60 ms in Chromium and 2.00 to 4.00 ms in Firefox on a valid spot, and 2.40 and 3.00 ms on one that snaps (median of 21, two runs, the timers' resolution 0.1 and 1 ms), and the browser test holds both under 16 ms. Top view folds the Diagnostics card to its title, with its counts, as it covered about a third of the top on Benchy and hid rings on x8 3 x 3; its title folds and opens it.
- **Undo:** a step is the project before a change: parameters, with the pocket's edited flag as its null, plane, pins and removed vents. Changes to one control join a step while each comes within 400 ms of the one before it, and for as long as its slider is held down, so a slider drag of any length, pauses and all, is one step, and a new press starts a new one; an undo or redo always ends a step, a change that ends where it began is none, and so is a step of changes that ends where it began, such as t from 3 to 3.5 and back in one drag (M8 first compared each change only with its own before, and kept a step that undid nothing), and 200 steps are kept. A drop is the drag's one step. Undo is ignored during a drag. In a number box (a text input) Ctrl+Z is the browser's only while the box holds typing not yet committed; once Enter or a wheel step has committed it, the box shows the project's value and Ctrl+Z is the app's, as on a slider or checkbox. M8 first gave every number box to the browser, so after Enter Ctrl+Z put the old text back in the box with the mold left as it was, and a click elsewhere then committed that text as a new step. The buttons' titles name the step, such as "Undo moving Pour 1 (Ctrl+Z)". A native title does not show in a screenshot, so the review shot draws that title under the button itself.
- **Tests:** `e2e/view.spec.ts` runs its four smoke tests on the build in Chromium and in Firefox, not on the dev server, which serves the same UI code, to keep `scripts/ci.sh` near its time; with CFG_FULL it adds the drag on x8 3 x 3, all 9 copies, against a fresh build and Node. The plan's "x8 or jersey on a grid" is jersey 2 x 1 in the quick suite. The cap test's colour check fails if the count's parity is broken (checked by flipping one stencil operation: the model's spot read as cover). The second cap test, added at review, reads the caps on the second tile of jersey 2 x 1, the hole a hidden model leaves, the cut under a box standing in for the cover, the silicone seen from the side kept, Mode B exploded, and the barrel's hollow; each of its checks was seen to fail with the fix it guards taken out. `test/unit/history.test.ts` runs the 20 edits on the store in Node as well, a change of base plane undone with the pins and removed vents it held, undo during a drag, a held slider and a drag back to its start; `test/unit/pipeline.test.ts` holds the drag's provisional cover to one cut of every copy on jersey 1 x 1 and 2 x 1, which fails if the moved opening is cut on the first tile only. After the review `scripts/ci.sh` takes 9.4 minutes (563 s, Vitest 147 s), with swap at 649 MB throughout.

### M9: Project files

Checks: save, reload and load give identical params and identical STL hashes. The pocket edited flag survives reload and undo. Schema migration works from a fixture. Corrupt project gives an inline error.

User: print the fit-test piece, tune clearances, leak test, and check pocket depth in the slicer.

Measured at M9, where a rule above reads differently in the app:
- **Where Save sits:** beside Open in the Model section, as the pair every program's File menu has, and Open is where a maker opened the file. The header holds only what acts on the whole view (undo, redo, theme), and the Export box is the mold's own output. Open STL… is now "Open STL or project…", and the drop hint says "Drop an STL or a project here". The row under the buttons names an opened project in bold above its STL. A save is named after the project it was opened from, or `<STL name>.moldproj`, and saved as `application/octet-stream`, so no browser renames it after the ZIP inside. At review: Ctrl+S saves the project and Ctrl+O opens the chooser, wherever the focus is, as the browser's own would save the page's HTML or open the file outside the app, and the buttons' titles name them. Save project stays off until the STL has been read, as a project whose STL cannot be read would be refused when opened (M9 first wrote one and said "Saved"). Its title says what a project keeps and what it leaves out: the view (camera, section, explode, parts shown) and the Export box's options.
- **The file:** a ZIP of `project.json` and `model.stl`, the STL byte for byte as it was loaded, at a fixed date, so saving one project twice gives the same bytes. The plan's State section lists schemaVersion, params, plane and pins; the file also holds the removed vents, as undo's snapshot does, the STL's name, the app's version, the STL's hash and the plane itself, below. Camera, theme, section and explode are not in it: a project loads as any new file does, framed afresh with explode back at a quarter of the box.
- **Schema version 2:** the hash and the plane are new fields, so SCHEMA_VERSION goes from 1 to 2, with one migration step, 1 to 2, which sets both to null (unknown). `schemaVersion` in report.json follows, as it is the same number; no golden reads it. `test/fixtures/projects/v1-jersey-export.json` is M8's own project.json, taken from an export ZIP that M8's code made in Node before any M9 change (jersey with t 3.5, pocket 0.4, wall 3.5, the pour pinned and the vent beside it removed). No version 0 ever shipped, so the app has no step from it and a version 0 file is "older than any this app can read". The chain is proven by `v0-synthetic.json` (`stl` and `app` for model and appVersion, `grid.cols` for `grid.nx`, no pins, no removed vents, no Reports group) through a 0-to-1 step given to the reader by the test, which sees the steps run 0 then 1. A newer version names its format, the app version that wrote it, and what to do. At review the app's version goes from 0.1.0 to 0.2.0 with the format, as files of format 1 and 2 both said 0.1.0, so the notes on an earlier or newer version contradicted the running app.
- **Reading a file:** a wrong type is refused with the field and the reason, as no value can be guessed from it; an unknown setting or top-level field is left out, a missing one takes its default, and a number out of range is brought to the range its control takes, each with a note. That range is the schema's, with M6's floors where the schema has none (a layer of 0 becomes 0.05), as a file must not set what the control itself refuses, and a whole number such as Pours per body is rounded. Notes list under the file row, in the warning colour where the project differs from the file. At review: M9 first refused every missing top-level field, so a file without `pins` or `removed` could not be opened, and the version 0 test's own step added them, which hid it. Now a missing plane is candidate 1, missing pins and removed vents are none (a warning note), a missing base plane and app version are unknown (an info note), and a missing hash is told by whoever matches the model by it; only the model's name and the settings must be there. Every reason names the file the maker chose, says in plain words what is wrong and what to do next ("Open an earlier copy, or open the STL and set it up again", or what to open instead), and keeps the field path and the reader's own words under a folded Details line; the notes say "the model's contents" where M9 first wrote SHA-256, and settings saved for another model of the same name say so rather than that their SHA-256 differ.
- **The hash:** SHA-256, written from FIPS 180-4 in plain JS in `src/app/hash.ts`, as Web Crypto needs a secure context. A maker can check it with `sha256sum`. The tests hold it to FIPS's own examples and to `node:crypto` at every padding boundary. It runs at 134 MB/s in Node (Benchy 84 ms, where `node:crypto` takes 22), so it runs off main, in the save and open jobs, and in the export's worker for the ZIP's project.json while main does not know it yet; main keeps it for the model once any of them has. A .moldproj whose model.stl does not match its hash is refused as damaged or changed.
- **Saving off main:** each save and open runs in a `project.worker` of its own, terminated when it answers, as hashing and zipping take 352 ms on Benchy and 4.0 s on 150 MB in Node, past the plan's 200 ms. In Node on this server: SHA-256 84 ms on Benchy and 1.06 s on 150 MB; storing the STL uncompressed 32 ms and 389 ms, which is its CRC; deflate level 1 268 ms (to 41%) and 2.97 s (to 21%); level 6 398 ms (40%) and 7.75 s (21%). So the STL is deflated at level 1: under 2% larger than level 6 on Benchy and no larger on the big file, in 67% and 38% of its time. In the browser, from the click to the download: Benchy 771 ms in Chromium and 743 ms in Firefox, a 4.65 MB file, with the main thread's longest gap between 4 ms timer ticks 14 and 13 ms. A synthetic sheet of 3,001,250 triangles (150,062,584 bytes), through the same worker and the same Blob and anchor, as loading it would preview it too: 4.44 s in Chromium and 6.16 s in Firefox, to 31.7 MB, with the longest gap 89 and 86 ms, which is the copy of the STL into the job and the Blob. `saveBytes` no longer copies bytes it can hand to the Blob as they are, which took one more 150 MB copy (about 85 ms) off main. The browser test holds both gaps under 200 ms.
- **Plane robustness:** project.json keeps the plane the choice found, `basePlane`: the outward normal, the offset along it of the plane the model stands on, upper level included, and the level choice's own offset. When a project loads, its first S2 compares the plane its choice finds with that one, as the largest distance between the two over the model's bounding box, against 0.02 mm, well inside the plane tolerance's least 0.05. If they differ, the ranked candidate, or upper level of one, that is the saved plane is chosen instead; failing that, the largest triangle on the plane that faces out along it is picked; failing both, the nearest candidate facing within 1.5 degrees and within 1 mm (the plane tolerance slider's reach), and otherwise the saved choice as this version reads it, with a warning under Plane tolerance and in Diagnostics that names the face and how far off it is. A choice changed this way keeps the project's pins and removed vents, says so in a note, and is written into every undo step and into what counts as saved, so undo never brings back the old one. Choosing a face oneself ends the check and its notes. A unit test makes jersey's file say rank 1 for rank 0's plane, as a later version ranking the other way round would, and sees rank 0 chosen with the same geometry hash; a plane tilted 20 degrees warns and keeps the choice. Saving while a project's check is still waiting keeps the plane the file brought. At review: a pick that names no triangle of this mesh, past its end or a sliver, as another version's weld can leave it, is refused by S2 as an inline error and no longer taken for an engine crash, which retried at twice the cell, showed "could not be built", and kept the coarser cell for the next face chosen. The model then stands on candidate 1 and the saved plane is looked for from there, as for any project, or, with none saved, a warning names the face. A choice changed this way is written only into the undo steps recorded since the file was opened (steps carry a sequence number), so settings opened on the model on screen leave the user's own older steps, and the open's own "before", on the faces they named; M9 first rewrote every step on the file's choice. The search is one pure function, `findStoredPlane`, which unit tests run through each branch (candidate, level, triangle, a guess within 1.5 degrees and 1 mm, and none past either), and a store test opens such a project and finds nothing unsaved.
- **Corrupt input:** a file that is no ZIP, a ZIP with no project.json, a .moldproj with no model.stl, malformed JSON, wrong types, a version newer than the app, a damaged archive, a model.stl that is not what its hash says and one that is no readable STL each give their reason in the Model section, with a dismiss button, never a dialog, and leave the model, its settings and its history as they were. fflate allocates the size an entry's directory declares, and cuts an entry that inflates to more at that size, so the declared size bounds memory: project.json may declare at most 4 MB and model.stl 1 GiB (about 21 million triangles, seven times the plan's heavy input), and an entry declaring more than deflate can make from the whole file's bytes (1100 to 1), or more packed bytes than the file has, is refused as damaged, all before anything is inflated; one that then inflates to another length than it declares is refused as damaged too, so none is kept in a buffer larger than itself. M9 first held the ratio against the entry's own declared packed size, which a crafted file can raise as well: jersey's .moldproj declaring 512 kB packed and 512 MB unpacked opened, with its 67 kB STL kept in a 512 MB buffer for as long as the model was loaded. The STL inside is parsed in the worker before it replaces the model on screen. A newer open or load supersedes one still being read, so the UI is never held by one; a unit test holds a project's job until a load has come. A ZIP of STLs says to unzip it and open one, and a 3MF to export an STL from the slicer. A file that is neither, nor named as an STL nor shaped like one (a binary STL of the size its count gives, or text starting with "solid"), is refused the same way and leaves the model; an STL that cannot be read still replaces the model with its reason, as before M9. The model on screen is copied into an open job only for settings without a model, a ZIP's directory saying first whether it holds model.stl (on a 150 MB STL that copy and its hash are about 85 ms on main and 1.1 s in the job).
- **README.txt's claim:** "so the same mold can be made again" was untrue, as the app opened nothing. Open now takes the export ZIP or its project.json as settings for the model already loaded, and README.txt says to open the STL, then the ZIP. Saved for that model, by its hash, or by its name where a version 1 file has no hash, with a note saying so, everything applies, as one step of the history; saved for another model, only the parameters, with a warning, as another model's base face and openings mean nothing here; with no model loaded, a note says to open the STL first. A project file clears the history, as a new file does; settings are a change to the model on screen, so they are one step undo takes back. Settings applied in full count as saved, as the file opens again to them (M9 first asked on leaving right after such an open). The browser test exports jersey, changes it, opens the ZIP and gets the export's project and geometry hash back.
- **Leaving the page:** M6 asked on every leave with a model loaded, as nothing could be saved. Now the browser's own prompt comes only when the project differs from the last save, open or Export ZIP (whose project.json opens again), so it never asks after a load, a save, or an undo back to what was saved, and still guards an afternoon of tuning. Asking on every leave would nag after every save. At review: opening an STL or a .moldproj over a model with changes no file holds asks first, under the file row: "Save, then open", "Open without saving", or "Cancel", never a dialog; settings alone never ask, being one step undo takes back, and a newer file supersedes the question. The "Saved X." line reads "Changed since you saved X." once the project differs from it, and an opened project's name says "(changed)". An error, a warning or the question opens a folded sidebar and scrolls the Model section into sight, as a file dropped on the view otherwise answered out of sight with nothing else on screen changing. Export ZIP still counts as saved, as this section says.
- **Measured round trips:** save, reload the page and open (M9 first loaded another model in the same page instead, with a second Store standing in for a reload in Node), on jersey with t 3.5, the pocket edited to 0.4, a pin and a removed vent, in Node and as a smoke test in Chromium and Firefox; on x8 with a pour dragged in the view and pinned and a vent removed, and on VIS-35 on its upper level, with `CFG_FULL` in both browsers: params deep equal, edited flags included, the plane, pins and removed vents, the STL's SHA-256 and the geometry hash all come back, with no plane note, and the history starts empty. The pocket's edited flag: reset by a double-click after the load, then undone, it is 0.4 again with the geometry of the save.
- **Tests and time:** `test/unit/project.test.ts` holds the hash, packing and unpacking, the size limits, migration from both fixtures, every rule of validation, the worker's jobs, the plane's search, and the store's save, open, settings and corrupt cases on jersey with the real pipeline; three of its checks were seen to fail with the clamp, the hash check and the plane's substitution taken out. At review it gained the fixed date read from both ZIP headers and a pack a year later, missing top-level fields, the plane's coordinates checked against values worked out by hand (a base at z = 5, jersey raised by 5 mm, and VIS-35's level against its lowest point read from its bytes), each branch of the search, the history and saved state through a resolution, the unusable pick, a newer load during an open, an unreadable STL's save, and the question before a replace; each of thirteen of these checks was seen to fail with the fix it guards taken out, and the browser's in-sight check with the scroll taken out. The browser corrupt list adds a ZIP of STLs, the hash mismatch, the truncated archive and a photo, through a change the history holds, and the smoke test closes the page for real with a change unsaved and gets the browser's own prompt in both browsers. The 150 MB save goes through `saveBytes` itself: 4.48 s in Chromium and 6.17 s in Firefox, main held at most 87 and 91 ms; Benchy 818 and 720 ms, 15 and 13 ms. `e2e/project.spec.ts` runs its two smoke tests on the build in Chromium and in Firefox, 4 to 5 s each, and with `CFG_FULL` the x8 and VIS-35 round trips and the save timings; the review shots add `jersey-save-open`, `jersey-project-opened`, `jersey-project-corrupt` and `jersey-project-migrated` in the export's sets, and at review `jersey-replace-prompt`. `scripts/ci.sh` takes 9.8 minutes (586 s, from 9.4; Vitest 150 s) and `--full` 24.4 (1462 s, from 23.4; Vitest 450 s, Playwright 16.8 minutes), both with a display, with swap at 651 to 652 MB throughout. After the review `scripts/ci.sh` takes 9.85 minutes (591 s; Vitest 151 s, Playwright 7.3 minutes, of which the four project smoke tests take 21 s), with swap at 652 MB. That is past the 8 minutes this section's opening sets for the quick suite, as M8's 9.4 already was: app.spec's smoke tests take 210 s, export.spec's and pipeline.spec's 67 s each, view.spec's 47 s, so bringing it back to 8 means moving other milestones' smoke tests to `--full`, which is left to the user's call; `--full` was not run again at review, only `project.spec.ts` with `CFG_FULL` (10 tests in 1.2 minutes). The `--full` run measured the saves again at 791 and 750 ms for Benchy and 4.45 and 6.06 s for the 150 MB STL, with main held at most 14 and 86 ms.
- **The one failure of the final `--full` run:** the corrupt-file smoke test, in Firefox, found the file row still in sight after scrolling the sidebar to its foot. A probe confirmed the cause in both browsers: a notice brings the Model section into sight two to three frames after it shows (the effect runs after the next paint and scrolls in a frame of its own), so a scroll made at once is undone by it. The app does what it should; the test's setup now scrolls again until the foot holds. That run otherwise passed: 1585 unit tests, and 175 browser tests in 17.0 minutes, with swap at 652 MB.

### M10: Nuts and bolts (optional toggle)

Geometry: countersunk bolts up through the base into hex nuts slid into side slots in the cover's corners. Parameters: size (M2.5, M3, M4; default M3), length, count fixed at 4, hole and nut clearances, minimum wall. Base countersink at 45 degrees on the bed side. Cover slot enters from the nearer side wall, with a short bridge ceiling in print pose and a one-layer membrane over the bolt hole that the bolt pokes through. Hole depth comes from bolt length and may never reach `Zt`. The seal stays inside the bolts, so the outer land grows automatically. Parameters appear in the schema only from this milestone.

Checks: required edge distance computed; bolt axes coincide within 1e-3; hole and slot at least `minWall` from the cavity, or a collision warning (fixture); membrane equals one layer; bridge length warning; nothing reaches `Zt`; no collision with pry notches; overhang audit passes except listed bridge faces; all M3 and M4 joint checks still pass with bolts on.

User: print, fasten, leak test.

Measured at M10, where a rule above reads differently in the app:
- **The fasteners** (`src/core/parts/fasteners.ts`), each value the standard's largest, so any screw or nut made to it fits what is cut for it:

  | Size | Pitch | Head dk (theoretical, 90 degree) | Head k | Screw standard | Nut s across flats | Nut m | Nut standard |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | M2.5 | 0.45 | 5.5 | 1.5 | ISO 14581 (as ISO 7046) | 5.0 | 2.0 | ISO 4032 |
  | M3 | 0.5 | 6.72 | 1.86 | ISO 10642 | 5.5 | 2.4 | ISO 4032 |
  | M4 | 0.7 | 8.96 | 2.48 | ISO 10642 | 7.0 | 3.2 | ISO 4032 |

  ISO 10642 and DIN 7991 start at M3, so M2.5's head is ISO 14581's. k is (dk - d) / 2 for a 90 degree head in every row, which a test holds. DIN 7991 heads (M3 dk 6, M4 dk 8) are smaller and sit a little deeper in the same countersink. Lengths are ISO 888's preferred series from 6 to 40 mm (6, 8, 10, 12, 16, 20, 25, 30, 35, 40), measured over the head as countersunk screws are; a project file's length off the list is brought to the nearest one, with a note.
- **Defaults for a 0.4 mm nozzle:** hole clearance 0.2 mm per side (3.4 mm for M3, ISO 273's normal clearance hole), as a 0.4 mm nozzle prints an upright hole about 0.1 mm small per side, which still leaves a free fit; the countersink gets the same clearance, so its cone is the head's moved 0.2 mm down the axis and a head sits up to 0.2 mm recessed, as the printed cone comes out between that and flush. The hole over the nut is placed for a head that deep and the length the nut needs for a head flush, so each is on the safe side (an M3 x 10 at a hole clearance of 0.3 passes the nut by 1.015 mm when fully recessed, by 0.715 flush, and is warned of). Nut clearance 0.15 mm per side (a 5.8 mm slot for M3), so a nut slides in by hand and stays; the slot is the nut's height, that clearance above and below, and one layer for the bridge to sag into (2.9 mm for M3). Least wall 1.2 mm, three lines of the nozzle. Thread past the nut: 2 pitches. The hole over the nut runs 1 mm past the screw's end, more than the length tolerance of a screw up to 40 mm, so a screw never bottoms out before it clamps. The plate keeps the larger of 1 mm and 4 layers over the countersink (4 top layers is what README.txt asks for): at Tb 3 with M4 the countersink is 2.48 deep, 0.52 is left, and the warning asks for 3.6 mm, 3.48 in whole layers. A bridge longer than 10 mm warns (common FDM guidance for a flat unsupported bridge); at the schema's nut clearances up to 1 mm no size reaches it (M4 at 1 mm is 9.0), so the range goes to 2 mm, and M4 at 1.6 mm (10.2) is the warning's fixture.
- **Where they go (G8):** the axis sits `edge` in from both of B's sides at each corner, the largest of the hole's wall at the plate's top (rh + w), the countersink's at the underside, where the taper's spread less the bed relief's run takes the plate (Tb - reliefH) tan(tau) - reliefRun past B (rk + w minus that), the countersink's rim square to the plate's side over the relief, which leans out at the taper (rk + w / cos(tau) - Tb tan(tau); it sets the edge only at a steep taper with a wide wall, such as M3 at a 30 degree taper and a 5 mm wall, 7.024 against 6.782 along the underside, which the review found as a plate wall of 4.79 under the 5 asked), and the slot's to the side wall it does not open through (s/2 + c + w - toBox). The groove's outer wall can have a sharp corner once the land is wider than the corner radius, so the hole keeps its wall to that corner on the diagonal, and the outer land becomes `land = edge + (rh + w) / sqrt 2`, unless the joint's own outer land or a wider edge already gives more. At M3 defaults the countersink sets `edge` = 4.095 (the hole alone needs 2.9), `land` = 6.146, B grows by 4.146 a side and the box by 8.292 each way, on all 7; M2.5 needs a land of 5.36 and M4 7.62 (7.89 on a 3 mm base). `edge.minimum` grows by the same, so "edge raised" and the auto edge include the bolts. The plate's corner radius gives way to `edge` as it gives way to the room round the cavity, so a corner never comes nearer a bolt than its sides, with the info message naming the bolt holes. The walls the placement sets are exact on all 7 at defaults: 1.2 mm to the groove's corner and 1.2 mm from the countersink to the plate's side at the underside. Over the schema's tapers (5 to 30 degrees), least walls (0.4 to 5), sizes, bases and reliefs no plate wall comes under the least wall, swept on jersey in `bolts.test.ts`. The land's note gives the growth the box actually takes: a hand-set edge that already holds the bolts grows it by less or not at all, and then the note says so or is left out (jersey at edge 16: 5.3 mm left to right and 4.6 front to back; at 20 and 30, nothing). "Edge raised" names the bolts with the joint when they set the minimum.
- **The slot opens through the short side:** the plan says the nearer side wall, and with the axis `edge` from both of B's sides both walls are equally near, by construction. The tie goes to the short sides, so the slots stay away from the pry notches at the middle of the long sides. The slot is a hex pocket round the axis, flats along the slot, opening into a channel of the same width out through the wall; a nut pushed in seats on the pocket's far faces within 1.155 c (0.17 mm) of the axis.
- **Heights:** the slot's floor is the lowest of a wall over the groove's roof (ridge height + extra depth + w, 2.7 mm at defaults), raised by under a layer so that it lies a whole number of layers under Zt. In print pose the cover's layers count from the flat top, so the bridge starts on a layer boundary and the membrane, the hole stopping one layer under the slot's floor, is exactly one printed layer (measured on all 7: the slot's floor at 2.70 to 2.88 mm). The auto length is the shortest standard one that passes the nut by 2 threads with the floor at that highest, so it never needs the cover's Zt: 12 mm for M3 and M4 and 10 mm for M2.5 at Tb 4, 16 mm for M3 at Tb 6. The plan did not say what happens on a model too low for the bolt: Zt is raised to keep the shortest bolt's hole and its slot a wall under the flat top. On none of the 7 does that move Zt; a 1 mm block with a 3 mm cap is the test (Zt 10.4 for M3 at Tb 4, the cap 7.4 over the block), built as solids with every check, and an info note under Top cap and Bolt size says the top was raised and that a smaller size lowers it. A chosen screw too long for the cover gets its hole stopped a wall under Zt and a warning with the longest that fits; one too short to pass the nut, a warning with the shortest that does. Nothing reaches Zt, which the solids check: plastic over each hole's end, a wall under the top, on jersey at defaults and with an M3 x 40, whose hole is cut 1.2 mm under jersey's top with its flat top whole, where a cutter run on to the screw's end fails both the cut and the box check.
- **The walls, checked in 3D:** every piece of a bolt's cut is a prism (an outline in plan between two heights), and the countersink a cone checked in the vertical section towards each side, against the plate's taper and bed relief. To the groove: a box out to its outer wall over the shelf, which only shrinks upward (and the ridge's, for the base). Outside: the box's sides but the one a slot opens through, the rebate's mouth on the shelf, and the flat top. The notches: their boxes in 3D, which the slots clear by height alone (the notch's top is under the shelf), 14.2 mm (barrel) to 102.1 mm (VIS-35) at defaults. The cavity: the field, in 3D, each node with H above 0 a column from 0 to H plus the TIN's raise, less a cell in plan for the surface between nodes, cropped to D as the tool is; 7.96 mm (jersey, x8) to 21.6 mm (wolf) at defaults. The preview checks the cavity at its own cell with no raise (the raise is S11's, which S6's output does not read), and the export again at its cell and raise; G6's rule that wall warnings come from the export holds there. Since the placement keeps the cuts outside the groove's outer wall and D, a real model never clashes: the collision warnings are proven on fixtures built to collide, one per wall (the groove moved out, the box shrunk, the plate shrunk, a notch under a hole and one beside a slot's channel, a cavity column under a hole and one under a slot's edge), each naming the cut, what it comes near, and the control to change, shown under that control too. The plate's names a smaller bolt or hole clearance, as the axes keep `edge` from B whatever the grid's edge. report.json's cavity wall is the whole field's least, the search widening until it finds the cavity (wolf 21.64, where a search capped at 20 mm read 20), and the notches' wall is null with them off.
- **Overhang:** the cover's only overhangs besides the seat cones are the slots' floors, flat bridges in print pose, listed by `boltBridges` and left out of the audit, which passes; what is left out is the slots' outlines inside the box and no more, 248.8 mm2 per cover at M3 on all 7, at 90 degrees, 5.8 mm across. The base's countersinks are 48-gons whose flats lean atan(cos(pi / 48)) = 44.94 degrees from vertical, inside 45, so the base passes with nothing listed, and its worst face is the countersink's, not the relief's.
- **The checks with bolts on:** M3's whole list (closed solids, intersection about 0, the joint's gaps, the box, the land, nesting, overhang, round trip, pocket, notches, plate, closed volume with only the groove besides the cavity, Mode B's filled voids, minGap) and the bolts' own (the cuts at their heights with a membrane of one layer, the walls, and the axes of base and cover from both print poses within 1e-3) pass on jersey in the quick suite and on all 7 and jersey 2 x 3 with `CFG_FULL`; M4's list after the openings (only the groove as a void, the top the box less the seats, the bore lengths, only the seat cone and the bridges overhanging, clearance, the removed volume, round trip) the same, jersey 2 x 3 included. The bolt coupon is read off its solids as the mold is: the hole and its one-layer membrane, the slot empty out through +x, the hole over the nut with plastic over its end, and the countersink and hole in the base, at all three sizes, with a coupon left uncut and one whose slot stops 2 mm short of the side as negative controls. `checkPlate` takes the bolts' holes off its section, and both overhang checks leave the listed bridges out and check they are the slots' outlines. Two negative controls: a cover with one bolt moved 0.01 mm fails the axis check, and one with no membrane fails the cut check. The browser test and a Node test reassemble the export ZIP's own cover.stl and base.stl, both print poses undone, and find each pair of holes within 1e-3 of each other and of the layout. A handle test sees every Manifold and CrossSection of a bolted mold freed.
- **Goldens with bolts** (`BOLT_GOLDEN`, 1%): the box is M3's grown by 8.292 each way and Zt is M3's on all 7. The plastic is the price: the cover grows by 15.5% (wolf) to 41.1% (barrel), jersey 80,885 to 111,534 mm3, and jersey's base from 10,256 to 13,817 mm3.
- **Bolts off is M9:** `test/fixtures/m9-parts.json` holds the SHA-256 of cover.stl and base.stl that M9 (e5256e0) exported in Node for jersey, jersey in Mode B, jersey 2 x 1 and the barrel, taken before any M10 change, and of the fit test's 16 pieces; the export with the bolts off gives the same bytes for all of them. The fit test's joint pieces never carry bolts.
- **The tape with bolts:** Cover & joint lists the bolts in place of the tape count, and says whether four are enough for the box. Each snug bolt is counted at 50 N, an estimate kept far under what an M2.5 screw and a printed slot carry, since printed plastic creeps; four hold 200 N against the max(2 x lift, 10 N) the tape is held to, which no example comes near (wolf needs 22 N). Bolts at the corners press the seal where they stand, as a strip of tape does where it crosses the joint, so between bolts the tape's spacing of 100 mm holds: past it a printed plate bends and the seal opens, and tape goes across the middle. VIS-35's bolts stand 55 by 222 mm apart, so it gets 2 strips left to right; x8's stand 135.6 by 101.0 mm apart and wolf's 113.1 by 134.3, so each gets one strip each way; benchy, goddess, jersey and the barrel need none, which a test holds on all 7. (The plan said earlier that only VIS-35 needed tape, which was wrong for x8 and wolf.) A screw length that does not fit is counted as holding nothing: the bolt list says so in place of "Four bolts are enough", and README.txt's parts list says not to buy that length but to set the one the warning gives and export again. README.txt has a Hardware section with the parts list (4 x M3 x 12 mm countersunk screws, ISO 10642 or DIN 7991, and 4 M3 hex nuts, ISO 4032, with s and m), when to fit the nuts (before assembly, pushed into the slots corner first, two flats against the slot's sides, as the slot is the nut's flats wide and ends in a corner), the screw step (snug, in turn, no more, as the plate bends), the membrane's place and purpose (between the hole and the slot, under the nut, so the screw pierces it before it meets the nut), and the demold steps unscrew instead of untape, in the Demold report too. report.json has `bolts`: the cut's sizes, the axes, the walls and the advice.
- **Fit test:** with the bolts on, the fit test adds `bolt_coupon_base.stl` and `bolt_coupon_cover.stl`: a 12 mm square of base (14 mm for M4) with one countersunk hole, and of cover with one nut slot opening at +x, its membrane and the hole over the nut, from the mold's own cutters at the clearances set, its top a whole number of layers over the slot's floor; the README says how to read them. The fit test is built without a model (the Fit test button and diag.html), so the coupon's slot floor is the lowest the mold's takes, a wall over the groove's roof, where the plan said the mold's heights: the mold's is up to a layer higher (2.70 to 2.88 on the 7, jersey 2.8846), so a screw passes its nut in the mold by up to a layer less than in the coupon, which its README says. It is one pair at the clearances set, not a step either side, to keep it small. With them off the fit test is M9's.
- **Project format 3:** the bolts are a schema group of their own, so SCHEMA_VERSION goes to 3, with one step, 2 to 3, which adds the bolts off at the values format 3 started with, written out in the step so a later change of defaults never changes what an old file opens to. The app goes to 0.3.0 with it. `test/fixtures/projects/v2-jersey-export.json` is M9's project.json from an export M9's code made in Node before any M10 change (jersey with t 3.5, the pocket at 0.4, wall 3.5, the pour pinned and the vent beside it removed); it and M8's version 1 fixture both migrate, and a test opens a .moldproj saved with M4 x 16 bolts and gets the bolts and the geometry back, and undo walks the four changes back to the taped mold. The stage table puts all six in S10, which S12, S13, S14 and S15 build on, so the T2 preview cuts them; a real-change test sees S10, S13 and S15 change on jersey.
- **UI:** the Bolts group closes Cover & joint, its toggle always shown and its five rows only when on, so the every-parameter browser test counts them with the bolts off and on. Bolt length is an auto setting that takes only listed values (`options` on the schema's auto kind), shown as a list: "Auto, 12 mm" and the lengths. Choice rows showed no notes, as none had any before; the plate warning now shows under Bolt size as well as Base thickness. Drawing the screws and nuts was left out: the section through a corner and the exploded view show the holes, the countersinks, the slots and the membranes, which is what there is to check.
- **Firefox's Math.hypot** differs from V8's in the last bit, which report.json's walls showed in the browser test (7.9621200676966755 against 7.962120067696673 in Node). The bolts' checks use a square root, which every engine rounds exactly, so the browser's report.json equals Node's in Chromium and Firefox again.
- **Tests and time:** `e2e/bolts.spec.ts` runs its two smoke tests on the build in Chromium and in Firefox (6 and 3 s each), and skips cleanly in Firefox without a display. The review shots add `jersey-bolts-group`, `jersey-bolts-section`, `jersey-bolts-exploded` and `jersey-bolts-warning` in the export's sets; M9's `jersey-save-open` and `benchy-section-x`, taken again, differ from M9's PNGs only in the status strip's rebuild time and, in Firefox, the sidebar's overlay scrollbar, which is shorter by the Bolts heading and toggle. `scripts/ci.sh` takes 10.2 minutes (613 s, from 591; Vitest 156 s, Playwright 7.6 minutes) and `--full` 25.7 (1544 s, from 1462; Vitest 509 s, Playwright 17.2 minutes), both with a display, with swap at 652 MB throughout.
- **The one failure of the final `--full` run,** after the review's fixes: M9's Save and Open smoke test, in Chromium on the build, waited out its 240 s for the chooser Ctrl+O opens; Vitest's 1824 tests and the other 179 browser tests passed (Vitest 500 s, Playwright 21.2 minutes with the 4 minutes waited, swap 665 MB).
