The page shows the app had saved and then undone the wall's change as the test asked, and a bare page of a button, a hidden file input and a keydown handler shows the cause is Playwright's and not the app's: its handler ran once each time, with user activation, and clicked the input, yet with the `filechooser` listener added just before the press the chooser was lost 10 times in 546, and with it added 200 ms or more before, or when the page opened, 0 in 870. Playwright switches its interception on only when a listener asks, beside the next action rather than ahead of it, and a chooser that comes first is not reported. The test itself failed once in 25 repeats the same way. It now listens from its start and polls for the one chooser; 40 repeats in Chromium, and `e2e/project.spec.ts` with `CFG_FULL` in all three projects, pass. No other test waits for a chooser.

### M11: Hardening and docs

Checks:
- Synthetic 3M-triangle STL imports, previews and exports. Peak RSS sampled from `/proc` under 3 GB. (M11a: not met as written. Summed RSS passed 3 GB in both browsers; the test holds PSS under 3 GB and reports summed RSS, until the user decides on the measure. See Measured at M11a.)
- Open-mesh fixture exports a Mode A base in soup mode with correct pocket volume. Mode B simplified geometry shows its error warning.
- 5 MB ASCII, wrong count header and non-STL file give inline errors.
- 300-rebuild soak on two models. Parameter fuzz with cross-rule checks. Full suite on all 7.
- `WORKFLOW.md` (the D2 sequence, sprue cut, sealing, vacuum note, cure inhibition, recommending Mode A for multi-footprint models), `DEPLOY.md`, `THIRD_PARTY.md` written.
- Licence scan with `npm ls --all --long --json`: no GPL or AGPL in shipped code.
- Every R-id maps to a test or a named user check.

User: full end-to-end pour, from model print to recast.

M11 is built in two runs: M11a, the hardening, and M11b, the docs, the licence scan and the map of R-ids to checks. M11c, a third, fixes what the user's multi-colour model showed, and adds it as the 8th example.

Measured at M11a, where a check above reads differently on the real app:
- **The heavy input** (`test/fixtures/heavy.ts`, never a file in the repo): a closed block 100 x 100 mm whose top is a 1224 x 1224 height field rippled 1 mm high every 2 mm, 3,011,040 triangles, 150,552,084 bytes, made in 0.2 s. In Node: parse 0.21 s, weld 0.42, edge audit 0.80, base detection 3.2, import 6.4 (one Manifold of it holds 609 MB of WASM heap, and building it took the heap to 1.0 to 1.3 GB), display proxy 0.43, the simplified copy 3.8 s; the process peaked at 1.9 GB. In the browser on the build, from the file chosen to the file row (the STL read in a worker first, then parse, weld, audit and base detection): 13.7 to 14.1 s in Chromium and 10.3 in Firefox; the preview after it 7.9 to 10.7 and 11.8 to 12.1 s; merged mode 2.8 and 3.0; back to glue 0.5; Export ZIP 22.0 to 22.2 and 23.7 to 24.0 s to the download (11.4 to 11.5 and 13.3 to 13.5 s in the export's worker), a 60.5 MB ZIP whose model_oriented.stl is the whole 150 MB file (two runs each). The test runs with `CFG_SOAK=1`, in Chromium and then in Firefox, one at a time.
- **Memory of the heavy input**, sampled from /proc every 200 ms over the browser's process tree (every process below Playwright's worker, 9 in Chromium and 7 in Firefox): the first run peaked at 4,512 MB, summed RSS, in the export, as the preview's solid worker (its import, then merged mode's union of a 2 million triangle copy, 2.1 GB of heap) and the export's own (its import again) stood at once. What brought it down: (1) G1.8's copy has 1 million triangles, not 2 million: the plan sets the threshold, not the copy's size, and merged mode's heap went from 2.1 to 1.4 GB, its error from 0.010 to 0.020 mm; (2) the copy is what every Manifold step after the import uses, the glue-in pocket's slice as well as merged mode's union, as G1.8 says of "the Manifold path", and the whole solid is freed once the import has checked it; (3) the preview's solid worker is recycled straight after a heavy import, and the fresh one, told the import's report, builds the copy alone (`importFromCopy`); (4) the export does the same from the preview's report, unless a trim needs the whole solid for model_oriented.stl; (5) a heavy preview worker is recycled before an export, so two heaps never stand at once; (6) a large STL's SHA-256 is worked out where it is read first, so the export is not sent a second copy of 150 MB to hash. Measured then, over two runs: Chromium peaks at 2,789 and 3,010 MB summed RSS and 2,158 and 2,375 MB summed PSS, in the preview, where the first solid worker checks the whole mesh while field.worker parses it; Firefox at 3,106 MB summed RSS both times and 2,776 to 2,777 MB PSS, in merged mode. Swap moved by under 3 MB. Every figure here is in MiB, as /proc's kB are KiB: 3 GB, 3e9 bytes, is 2,861 MiB, and 3 GiB 3,072. **The plan's "peak RSS under 3 GB" is not met as written:** summed RSS passes 2,861 MiB in Chromium's worse run (3,010) and in Firefox (3,106), and Firefox's passes 3 GiB as well. M11a first read 3 GB as 3 GiB, held only Chromium's RSS to it, and called the check met; the review corrected that. Summed RSS counts every page the processes share once per process: the empty app reads 1,111 MiB of it in Chromium and 890 in Firefox, against 502 and 571 of PSS, which splits a shared page between its sharers, so the tree's own memory is the PSS sum, and PSS peaks under 3 GB in both (2,375 and 2,777 MiB). Firefox also keeps about 0.5 GB more than Chromium once all is done (1,734 against 1,182 MiB PSS). The test holds PSS under 3 GB (2,861 MiB) in both browsers, checks that it found the browser's tree and read it whole (at least 3 processes, no failed read, PSS over 300 MiB), prints each process's share at the peak, and reports summed RSS over 3 GB as a deviation, an annotation and a warning, rather than failing. Whether PSS may stand for the plan's RSS, or the memory must come down, is the user's to decide (REQUIREMENTS.md, open items). On the final code, two runs each (the test alone, then `scripts/ci.sh --soak`): Chromium peaks at 2,807 and 3,005 MiB summed RSS and 2,174 and 2,373 PSS, its renderer, which runs the page and every worker, holding 1,798 and 1,965 of the RSS; Firefox at 3,088 and 3,171 RSS and 2,759 and 2,843 PSS, both in merged mode, its content process holding 1,885 and 1,913 and its parent process 964 and 1,021 (870 and 927 PSS), which holds the chosen file and the saved ZIP and draws the page, and which the page has no hold on. So summed RSS passes 3 GB in Firefox on every run and in Chromium on one of two, as the test reports; and Firefox's PSS stays under 3 GB by only 18 MiB on the second run, so the test may fail there on a run that reads a little higher. The first frame with the section on takes 499 ms in Chromium and 1,840 ms in Firefox on software WebGL: counting the 3 million triangle mesh in the cut, with no geometry worked out on main.
- **The heavy export, after the review:** report.json's model volume past the limit was the bodies' sum, which leaves a sealed void in (a heavy barrel would read 7,489 mm3 for a model_oriented.stl of 4,057); it is now the whole solid's, voids out, read before that solid is freed, or from the preview's report as bodies less voids. An export that must trim made a second whole-size trimmed solid and kept it, with the whole import, through the build; now it writes the trimmed model_oriented.stl and reads its volume in an arena of its own before the copy is made, then frees the whole solid, so the build holds only the copy. Where minGap measures the copy itself, as when its 60,000 triangle decimation is no solid, the gap gives up the copy's error (it took 0, as the whole model's had). In the preview, a copy that is no closed solid gave a warning the base never showed ("...the base uses the whole mesh, which may run out of memory"); it shows under Mode now. The limits (`HeavyLimits`) are passed to SolidStages and the export, so `test/regression/heavyPath.test.ts` runs these paths in the quick suite at 4,000 and 2,000 triangles on a 7,920 triangle block: the preview's whole import, its worker recycled and the next told that import; the copy's warning under Mode in both modes; the preview's worker recycled before an export told the import; report.json's volume less a 12,000 mm3 void; the gap less the copy's error; and an export that trims. Past 300,000 triangles Diagnostics says the view draws a simplified copy: "The view draws this model simplified to N of its M triangles, to keep it quick to turn; the mold is built from the whole mesh."
- **Base detection on heavy meshes:** a rough surface spreads its normals over hundreds of 1.5 degree bins, and each of the 128 examined costs a pass over the mesh: 10.0 s on the 3 million triangles. Past 300,000 triangles the bins examined go down in proportion, to no fewer than 16 (3.2 s there, the same five candidates). No example has more than 225,154 triangles, so none changes. The quick suite holds the 400 x 400 block at 16 bins to its candidates at all 118 (the same five; at 1 bin it loses the sides, so the comparison can fail), and the soak test checks the five on the 3 million themselves.
- **meshoptimizer's error** is worked out in float32 on the model scaled to 1. The first heavy fixture, a ripple 3 mm high every 25 mm, lost under a micron to the copy, and the simplifier read it as exactly 0, which the warning showed as "0.000 mm". The warning now says "with less error than the simplifier can measure" for a 0, and the fixture is rough enough to read: 0.020 mm.
- **Soup mode:** `patchedL` (an L of 700 mm2 underneath, refined to 11,200 faces, with the 24 of one side within 3 mm of a point taken out, 14 open edges) and a box with its top missing (4 open edges) export a glue-in base whose pocket, read off the exported base.stl against the plate built on the same rim in Node, is 146.423 and 124.025 mm3, Steiner's closed form for the outline offset by 0.2 mm (less e2 at the L's reflex corner) times one layer, to 0.001% (measured +2.9e-7 and -4.1e-7 of it: 146.42346 and 124.02508 against 146.42342 and 124.02513). Node and the browser hold it to that 0.001%, where they first held 2%, which a 0.12 mm enlargement passed; a square corner, 0.007 mm3 on the box, and an enlargement 0.01 mm off now fail. Merged mode is off for an open mesh: its button under Mode is disabled with the reason as its title, the soup-mode note shows under Mode as well as in Diagnostics, and the status strip reads "clearance ≥ t, unverified" from the load on, as no export can measure it; merged mode set from a project file still shows its error and blocks the export. The note's words, since the review: "...so merged mode cannot be used with it, and the cover and the glue-mode base are built from its surface as it is. Without a closed model the silicone thickness cannot be measured: it stays as planned, and no export can check it. To use merged mode and a measured thickness, repair the mesh, for example with your slicer's repair, and open it again." It said "merged mode is off", which read wrong beside Merged (B) still pressed from an earlier model. The hole's note says what to do ("The surface has 1 hole of 30.0 mm2. Close it in the program that made the model, or with a mesh repair, and open the file again.") and its Show marks the middle of each hole's rim; "missing-top-surface" marks the holes facing up and ends "Repair the mesh to close them, and open it again." What the chosen face implies (below-plane, below-plane-soup, missing-top-surface, base-from-hole) shows in the Model section under Plane tolerance, beside the face list, as well as in Diagnostics: an open block on a face with a spike past it says "...an open mesh cannot be trimmed, so please choose another base face, or repair the mesh and open it again." there, and blocks the export. Node and the browser both export it, the browser in Chromium and Firefox.
- **Bad files:** an STL is now read before it replaces the model on screen, as M9 did for the STL inside a .moldproj: on main under 4 MB, which takes milliseconds, and in a project.worker of its own above, as 150 MB takes 1 to 2 s (`readStl`); M9 let an unreadable STL replace the model with its reason. The reason is the parser's own words after "X.stl cannot be opened:", then "The model on screen stays as it was." while a model is on screen, and "Export the STL again from the program that made it.", under the file row with its Dismiss, never a dialog. Three parser messages were made plain: an empty file said "it is too small" and now "The file is empty."; a text STL with no facet said "too small" too and now "The STL file contains no usable triangles."; a file cut off inside a vertex line said 'a vertex value that is not a number: ""' and now "The ASCII STL ends in the middle of a triangle, so it looks cut off." Since the review that holds wherever the file stops inside a facet: in a number ("1.5e", "-", "1e+"), after the facet's endloop, in its facet normal line, or in its last value, which "1.25" cut to "1.2" still reads as; a file that stops between facets with no endsolid loads what it holds with "The ASCII STL has no endsolid line at its end, so it may be cut off: N triangles were read. If the model looks incomplete, export the STL again from the program that made it." A binary header count is clamped to min(declared, what the size holds), and loads with "Opened X.stl, with a warning." and the reason under it: a count past the size says "The file header says N triangles but the file holds only M, so it looks cut off: the M were read. Export the STL again from the program that made it."; a count short of the records, that the first N were read and the rest left out; a count of 0, that the records the size holds were read. Under 50 bytes after the records the header counts is padding, and loads with nothing to say: M11a first said "The file header says 225706 triangles but its size fits 225706, so 225706 were read and the file may be damaged." of Benchy with 2 bytes more. An image or a text file named as one is M9's "is not an STL or a project file" unchanged; an image named .stl gets the parser's "does not look like an STL file". From 32 MB the check also works out the SHA-256 (1.1 s more in the worker on 150 MB), so the export is not sent a copy of the file to hash; a smaller file is hashed by the export or a save as before, as on every load of Benchy it would add 84 ms for nothing. A 5 MB ASCII STL (the heavy block at 104 x 104 cells, 22,880 triangles, 5.5 MB) loads whole, in Node in the quick suite and in both browsers with `CFG_FULL`. The bad files run in Firefox, the user's browser, in the quick suite, and in Chromium too with `CFG_FULL`.
- **The preview grid drift (M8's open item):** each preview tile is composited at the export offset rounded to its own nearest preview node, so the drift is at most half a preview cell on every tile: jersey 20 x 1's last tile is 0.338 mm off at its 0.952 mm cell, where M8 measured 5.10 mm, and x8 3 x 3's last 0 x 0.25 mm at 0.5, where M8 had 0.50 x 0.25. The export does not change: at the export cell every rounding is whole, and the M9 SHA-256s of the export's STLs, all goldens and the hashes Node and the browsers share are unchanged. Updated: gridField's two tests on the wider preview field (now: the preview keeps the export pitch, each tile within half a cell, and 20 tiles at a pitch a quarter cell past a whole number), and solid.test's x8 2 x 1 and 8 x 1 (now within half a cell and inside D with no clip); M8's 8 x 1 test of the clip moved to jersey 20 x 1 at its own 0.952 mm cell, which still reaches 0.24 mm past D from the coarse cell alone. Added: jersey 20 x 1's and x8 3 x 3's last tiles, by the centroid of their cavities on the composite, against half a cell. The parameter fuzz found one more: past the export's node cap the export plan raises its own cell and snaps its pitch to that, while a preview snapped to the cell it was given, so x8 on a large grid drifted by 1.9 mm a tile (31.8 mm at the 18th). A preview now takes its pitch from the export plan itself.
- **Sealed air in a cut (M8's open item):** air the plate closes off under the model is taken out of the silicone's count by an exact closed surface of its own (`plateSealedAir`, in core): the model's faces, less those on the plane, fall into pieces joined across edges off the plane; a recess's walls and ceiling make a piece bounded only by edges on the plane whose volume, about a point of the plane, is negative, minus its air; turned inside out and closed by a fan to a point under the plane, it encloses that air and nothing above the plane. No cell is involved, so it is exact where S8's columns are not. Benchy: 8 recesses, 23.43 mm3, which is G2.6's 78.1 mm2 times its 0.30 mm; wolf: 13, 99.53 mm3 (G2.6's 108.5 mm2 at 0.917 mm), the largest 12.53; none on the other five; an inverted cup's air to 0.02% of the closed form. 83 ms on Benchy in Node. Since the review it is worked out in field.worker at S2 and comes with the plane, as does the mesh's list of triangles less its void shells (M8's exterior mesh), so turning the section on works out no geometry on main: on 3 million triangles those took 0.65 s and 0.3 s there. Past the display proxy's 300,000 triangles the sealed air is not worked out at all, as it would add 0.65 s and about 150 MB to field.worker on 3 million while the solid worker's import holds the most memory, so a heavy model's cut counts a sealed recess as silicone; the heavy browser test turns the section on and times its first frame. In Mode B the plate fills those recesses, and the cut now shows them as base. Checked by colour, with `CFG_FULL` in Chromium and Firefox, at a close cut through one of Benchy's letters (no silicone cap, and model over it), its point found from Benchy's faces alone since the review (under a face of the 0.30 mm level facing down, with the model on four sides within 4 mm by ray casts), not from `plateSealedAir`, with the silicone outside the hull at the same height as the control, and on a block with a ring engraved round an island (no silicone) beside the same block with a channel out to the side (silicone); both fail with the new count taken out. The quick suite holds `plateSealedAir` to its winding numbers in Node. **The silicone figures do not change**, and were not wrong: S8 finds the same recesses, and the plan's filter (`minVolumeMl`, 0.05 ml) leaves every one out, Benchy's 0.023 ml in all being 0.05% of its 43.18 ml.
- **Soak:** on the final code after the review, `scripts/ci.sh --soak` took 34.8 minutes (2,086 s; Vitest at the soak sizes 224 s, 1,520 tests; Playwright 31.0 minutes, 8 tests), all passing: Benchy in 136 s (Chromium) and 112 s (Firefox), used heap -192 KB; x8 3 x 3 in 700 and 745 s, +64 KB; held entries 5 and 5, handles 19 and 19, workers 2 and 2; main +1.16 and +1.00 MB in Chromium; the leak control +1.57 and +1.77 MB. Before the review: 300 rebuilds by t each of Benchy and of x8 3 x 3 (153 openings cut on every rebuild), on pipeline.html on the build, with M5's method: the readings after 10 rebuilds of warm-up and at the end. Chromium: Benchy in 137 s, solid.worker's used heap -192 KB, x8 3 x 3 in 705 s, +64 KB, against M5's 512 KB; held entries 5 and 5; live handles 19 and 19 on both; workers spawned 2 and live 2 at both readings; main's JS heap and buffers +1.21 and +1.06 MB against 2 MB. Firefox: 113 and 741 s, the same heap readings, handles and workers (main's heap cannot be read there). The leak control (90 rebuilds of jersey, one 1,000-point section kept each) grew the heap by 1.57 and 1.77 MB, so 290 rebuilds would show a leak of about 2 KB a rebuild. The handle count does not see that control, by design: its section is released from its arena, as a forgotten handle would be; the arena counts what it owns. `scripts/ci.sh --soak` took 34.5 minutes (2,071 s; Vitest at the soak sizes 214 s, Playwright 30.9 minutes). The soak runs with `CFG_SOAK=1`, which `scripts/ci.sh --soak` sets, and no longer with `--full`: the plan runs soak tests at M5 and M11 only. The heap reading also returns the arena's live handles (`Arena.live`: every Manifold and CrossSection tracked and not yet freed), and pipeline.html records every worker it spawns, so the test holds both to their warm-up values; Firefox runs it without the 3D view, as M5's did headless.
- **Parameter fuzz** (`test/unit/fuzz.test.ts`): each set draws every schema parameter where its control takes it (the schema's range, the control's floor, the slider's travel, and one in ten up to twice the slider's end), autos on their rule half the time. Through the pure parts (the rules, resolveAuto, both plans and G6's half cell, the bounds and rim, placement settings and sizes, the export pitch, the stage keys and a project file's round trip, on jersey, x8 and the barrel) 300 sets in the quick suite and 3,000 with `CFG_SOAK`: finite numbers (no NaN and, since the review, no infinity), the preview's r_eff equal to the export's, a nested rim where it has no error, plain inline errors. Through real preview builds of jersey (grids up to 3 x 2, one set in three over the whole schema and the rest over a tenth of it, as most whole-schema sets break a rule) 8 sets in the quick suite and 120 with `CFG_SOAK`: every crash, failure of the engine and hard rule left unblocked is a failure, and every set that builds has a cover and base each one closed solid in one piece, inside the rim's box, and a funnel when it has one, with finite silicone and auto values; each sparse set among the first 4 (32) that builds is exported too, its report with no NaN or infinity, read off the report as built (JSON.stringify writes both as null, so the file itself could not show one) and no null in report.json where the report has a number, and no error of its own. 74 of the 120 built; the rest stop on the rules (funnel-no-seat 15, funnel-too-flat 13, ridge-too-thin 11, pour-shortfall 8, funnel-no-cone 3, seat-too-deep 3, funnel-top-small 2). Found and fixed: the drift past the node cap, above; its 5 seeds are kept in `test/fixtures/fuzzSeeds.ts` and run in the quick suite, and they now check the margin as well. The review's r_eff check found seed 1,102,998 on x8 17 x 19 with `CFG_SOAK`, where the cap raised the preview's own cell as well as the export's, and its rounding to 0.001 mm left the preview's a step above, so its margin came out 0.002 mm wider: a preview's margin is now always the export's.
- **Review shots** (`CFG_SHOTS=1`): `jersey-bad-file`, `soup-mode-note`, `heavy-merged-warning`, `benchy-lettering-section` and `jersey-20x1-last-tile`, at 1366 x 768 and 1920 x 1080 in both themes in Chromium and 1366 x 768 dark in Firefox. They showed "1 holes totalling", now "1 hole of" in both of its messages. The review found the Diagnostics card, which a warning moves to the top right, over the mold in `soup-mode-note`, `heavy-merged-warning` and `jersey-20x1-last-tile`, and over the letter's cut in `benchy-lettering-section`, so their view moves right and up to put the subject clear of it; and "The model itself has 0 mm2 of overhang past 45 degrees per copy, which needs supports" in `heavy-merged-warning`: an overhang under 0.5 mm2, which reads as 0, is no longer reported.
- **Test time:** `scripts/ci.sh` takes 10.4 minutes (624 s; Vitest 159 s, Playwright 7.7 minutes), against the 8 this section's opening first set and M10's 10.2 (613 s). The plan's figure is now 10.5 minutes: each smoke test guards a path a maker uses, and `--full` runs before every commit. The first measurement after M11a's additions read 10.8 minutes (650 s), so the quick suite runs the new bad-file and soup-mode tests in Firefox, the user's browser, and Chromium's with `CFG_FULL`, as it does the 5 MB ASCII STL in the browser and the engraved blocks' colours (Node reads both in the quick suite), and builds 8 fuzz sets for 12. An STL under 4 MB is read on main, where a worker of its own per load cost a few seconds over the suite. `--full` takes 22.9 minutes (1,372 s; Vitest 515 s, 1,849 tests; Playwright 14.2 minutes, 180 tests), down from 25.7, as the soak moved to `--soak` (34.5 minutes on its own). Swap stayed at 673 to 674 MB throughout, from 665 at the start. After the review: `scripts/ci.sh` takes 10.5 minutes (629 s; Vitest 164 s, 1,519 tests; Playwright 7.7 minutes, 117), 1 s under its figure, with the 308,880 triangle Node test of the display proxy run with `CFG_FULL` (6 s), as a first run with it read 634 s; `--full` 23.1 minutes (1,383 s; Vitest 515 s, 1,871 tests; Playwright 14.4 minutes, 180 tests). Where Firefox cannot draw (no Firefox, or no display), the bad-file and soup-mode tests run in Chromium in the quick suite instead of being skipped.

Measured at M11b, where a check above reads differently on the real app:
- **The docs:** `docs/WORKFLOW.md` is new, the maker's whole job in eleven steps: setting up the mold in the app (the STL opened, its base face checked, Mode A or B, with Mode A for a model on several footprints such as the wolf, and the printer's layer height, nozzle and bed), the fit test, which like every export button needs a model open (`diag.html`'s builds at the default settings but for the taper clearance, and has no bolt coupon), and the bolt coupon and how to read them, Export ZIP once the clearances are set and again after any change, print poses with no supports, whole layers and the base 0.2 mm proud, cure inhibition with a test patch of each print material, the glue (Mode A), the tape and the petroleum jelly, joining and sealing (petroleum jelly in the groove, which helps, as G9 says, and the leak test shows whether a joint needs more; tape, whose count rests on Tape strip holds, 10 N a strip by default, with a way to judge one's tape; or nuts corner first and screws snug in turn, with tape across the middle of long sides) with the leak test with water before the model is glued in, then the mold taken apart and dried, and why water pushes less than silicone up to a full funnel; gluing, and a first coat brushed under each overhang the air report lists while the model can still be reached, before the mold is closed; standing the mold level with its funnels before mixing, as the silicone's working time starts there, degassing in the cup if a vacuum chamber is at hand, and the vacuum note: degassing the filled mold is untested for printed molds, as air in the infill can come through the walls, so a filled fit-test pair goes under vacuum first; the pour, curing, the sprue rule, the D2 demold by the Demold report's verdict and its places marked Cut (the report shows six, each with a Show button, and README.txt lists them all), the finished mold, and what only a test pour can tell, the tape's strength and the vacuum among it. It names no brands, and no longer gives an unsourced maker's leak as fact. README.txt follows it: its cure-inhibition step names each print material, the glue in Mode A only, the tape where the bolts still need it, and the Vaseline; its leak test comes before the glue, with the mold taken apart, dried and sealed again; the Vaseline goes in the groove before the base is fitted, where it came after; a first-coat step comes before the mold is closed when the air report lists an underside pocket; it says "Mix the silicone, and degas it in the cup if you have a vacuum chamber", where it said "Mix and degas"; and its AIR section adds the vacuum note. The Demold report's air rows and their diagnostic say "before you close the mold" and that a vacuum is untested on printed molds, keeping G-Analyses' three remedies. `fit_test/README.txt` is wrapped at 76 columns in CRLF lines, as README.txt is, where it had LF endings and 15 lines over 76, the longest 520; the wrapping moved to `src/core/report/text.ts`, shared by both. `test/unit/requirements.test.ts` holds WORKFLOW.md to the plan's list above by 45 phrases, the three verdict names among them, holds the demold steps of all three verdicts to the phrases they share with it, and holds README.txt's sprue rule and vacuum note to its words, so a change to either side shows. `docs/DEPLOY.md` is rewritten for whoever runs the server: the heavy model's memory in GB and GiB as M11a measured it (PSS 2,158 to 2,375 MiB in Chromium, 2.3 to 2.5 GB, and 2,759 to 2,843 in Firefox, 2.9 to 3.0 GB; summed RSS up to 3.3 GB), so about 3.5 GB free, where it first read MiB as GB; updates, as this checkout has no git remote, so there is nothing to pull; the server, which reads `dist/` from disk on every request (Vite's preview serves it with sirv in its dev mode), so another server is fed a build into `../cfg-static` and nothing else builds into `dist/` while it runs; and, as the risks below promise, what to do if Chrome blocks a download: choose Keep, use Firefox, tunnel to localhost with `ssh -N -L 5173:127.0.0.1:5173`, or `chrome://flags/#unsafely-treat-insecure-origin-as-secure`. `scripts/install.sh` runs `npm ci` only when the lockfile's packages differ from those npm recorded at the last install, where it compared file times, so 1.0.0's version bump would have deleted node_modules and installed every package again. `docs/THIRD_PARTY.md` is new, `README.md` is the maker's, with the same memory figures and a look-and-feel check for U2, and REQUIREMENTS.md's section 7 now follows WORKFLOW.md step by step, its section 9 gains the project's own licence as the user's open item (the M11a memory deviation stays open), and its section 10 becomes the table of user checks U1 to U7. DEPLOY.md's advice on other static servers was tried on spare ports (5191 and 5192) with Python's `http.server` serving a scratch build: `.wasm` went as `application/wasm` and `.js` as `text/javascript`, `diag.html`'s worker loaded Manifold and answered its three jobs, and the app opened; with `.wasm` sent as `application/octet-stream` all of it worked the same, as `bootManifold` instantiates the WebAssembly from its bytes. The build's pages load `/assets/...`, so DEPLOY.md says to serve the build at the root.
- **Licence scan** (`test/unit/licences.test.ts`, about 2 s: npm ls 0.4 s twice, the build 0.5 s). The plan's check holds as it reads: the production tree of `npm ls` has 45 packages and no GPL or AGPL among them, which the scan now asserts over the whole tree. The only copyleft licence in it is `@img/sharp-libvips-linux-x64` 1.3.4, LGPL-3.0-or-later, one of the 36 packages manifold-3d's package lists for its manifoldCAD command-line tool and its glTF and 3MF export (gltf-transform, sharp, esbuild-wasm and theirs), of which no byte reaches a browser. On top of the plan's check, the scan reads what ships off an in-memory build with the app's own Vite config, writing nothing, in the pages' chunks and the workers': a package ships when the bundle holds any of its files, every such package must be in the production tree, and every dependency in `package.json` must be in the bundle, so a bundle read wrongly fails. It fails on any shipped licence that is GPL, AGPL, LGPL, SSPL, missing or not on its reviewed list; on a shipped package, version, licence or copyright line that THIRD_PARTY.md lacks or gives otherwise, rows matched by name and version, so two versions of a package each need theirs; on its tables of production packages outside the bundle and of the direct development tools differing from what is installed; on build tool code it does not know; on a shipped package with no licence file (LICENSE, LICENCE or COPYING, any suffix such as LICENSE-MIT); on notices that lack a shipped package's entry, its "Licence:" or copyright line, or any of its licence files; on code a shipped package takes from another project being in the bundle, found by a name it keeps there, without its notice; and on the WebAssembly's table differing from the test's own fixed list, an entry without its component's copyright lines and full licence text as the upstream project gives it, `manifold.wasm` without the names some components are seen by, or a library's name in it that no row covers. It runs npm ls with `--include=dev` and without NODE_ENV, as NODE_ENV=production dropped the tree to 45 packages and gave 11 false problems, and holds the notices to plain ASCII bytes. It reports the licences outside the bundle: production outside the bundle 36 (MIT 31, Apache-2.0 3, ISC 1, LGPL-3.0-or-later 1), development only 43 (MIT 32, Apache-2.0 7, MPL-2.0 2, ISC 1, BSD-3-Clause 1; the MPL-2.0 pair is lightningcss). Fixture trees, packages with real folders, and changed copies of the real doc, notices, bundle and binary make each of these fail with its own message, and seven of its branches taken out one at a time (the licence column, the development table, unknown build tool code, GPL outside the bundle, a missing licence file, the copyright column, the binary's names) each fail the suite. Vitest sets NODE_ENV to test, and Vite builds for production only under NODE_ENV production (the first run read the browser stand-in as `__vite-browser-external:node:module`), so the scan sets it around its build. Vite's module preload polyfill is turned off (`build.modulePreload.polyfill: false`): it is adapted from es-module-shims (MIT, Guy Bedford), whose notice Vite does not carry, and every browser the app supports preloads modules itself, so the bundle now holds only Vite's empty stand-in for Node modules and Rolldown's runtime helpers of the build tools' code.
- **Notices:** the minified build drops every `@license` comment, three.js's among them, so the notices ship as `public/third-party-notices.txt` (47,858 bytes), which Vite copies next to `index.html`. It holds each shipped package's licence files as the package gives them (Vite's LICENSE.md up to its list of what Vite bundles into its own Node code, and Rolldown's THIRD-PARTY-LICENSE for code from Rollup and esbuild); the code the shipped packages say they take from others and the bundle holds: UZIP.js in fflate's DEFLATE (MIT), MathGeoLib in three-mesh-bvh's closest points of segments (Apache-2.0), Geometric Tools in three.js's `Ray.distanceSqToSegment` (BSL-1.0) and gl-matrix in its `Matrix4.invert` (MIT), while three.js's port of earcut (ISC) is left out of the build, which the scan checks by its `earcutLinked`; and what manifold.wasm holds besides Manifold. That list was first read off the names the binary keeps, which header-only code compiled into Manifold's own namespace does not leave, so it missed four: Manifold v3.5.4's sources (its `src/` and `include/` headers and `cmake/manifoldDeps.cmake`, read at the tag) take PhysX's segment distance, which `minGap` uses (`src/tri_dist.h`, BSD-3-Clause, NVIDIA, whose 2024 release's notice the entry gives, as BSD-3-Clause asks for it with a binary), tbtSVD's decomposition in every transform (`src/svd.h`, MIT, wi-re), quickhull for `hull` (public domain, Antti Kuukka) and dset's disjoint sets for `decompose` and the booleans (zlib, Wenzel Jakob), and its trigonometry from Sun's fdlibm via musl (`include/manifold/math.h`, whose notice asks to be kept). With Clipper2 (BSL-1.0, the commit Manifold's build fetches), linalg.h (Unlicense), Emscripten's runtime and embind (MIT), musl (MIT) and LLVM's libc++ and libc++abi (Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception), that is ten. Clipper2, linalg.h, Emscripten and libc++ are also seen in the binary, by their namespaces, Clipper2's error message and libc++abi's source path; the package records none of their versions. `manifold.js` calls `initTBB`, but the binary has no `tbb` name and Manifold fetches oneTBB only for its parallel build, so no oneTBB code ships. The file is plain ASCII, the copyright sign written (c), as `vite preview` sends text/plain with no charset and a browser could read UTF-8 as another encoding; the scan compares texts the same way and fails on any byte past 0x7F. The app links it as "Third-party licences" at the foot of Advanced. The layout smoke test in `e2e/app.spec.ts`, which already checked the developer links there, now finds that link too and fetches it: 200, text/plain, the file's own bytes, in all three projects. That kept it out of a test of its own, which would have cost a page load per project.
- **The requirement map:** section 3's Check column names backticked test files with quoted titles, and user checks by their ids in section 10. `test/unit/requirements.test.ts` parses it (31 rows, 89 references to 32 test files, 108 titles, 22 user checks) and fails on a row with no check, a file that is no `.test.ts` or `.spec.ts` under `test/` or `e2e/`, a file that does not exist, a file named with no title, a title that is not in a test's or group's own title nor in a table its `.each` reads (a title left in a comment, an assertion's message or a fixture's text fails), an unreadable item, a user check section 10 lacks, and the two sections disagreeing on which user check covers which requirement; a fixture shows each failing. R18 and R31 now name the tests of `test/unit/bolts.test.ts` and `e2e/bolts.spec.ts` they rest on, and R27 no longer names `e2e/shots.spec.ts`, which runs only with `CFG_SHOTS=1`, never in `scripts/ci.sh`, and makes screenshots for a person to review. The old column named no file but in R31's row, whose files all exist, so no reference had gone stale by a rename; it named milestones instead, R17 a WORKFLOW.md that did not exist yet, and R22, R25, R29 and R30 none at all ("none needed", "present", "this repository", "decisions below"). Every row now has automated checks. R25 to R30 are new ones in requirements.test.ts: the decisions D1 to D9 recorded and the open items open, `bash -n` on the three scripts with install.sh's Node 24 guard as written (it must exit 1 under 24) and nothing that gains root outside its `WITH_SYSTEM_DEPS=1` block (`sudo`, `pkexec`, `doas`, `su -c`, `install-deps` or `--with-deps`), serve.sh's two commands, and no commit message naming Claude or Anthropic (D8). R22, dropped, maps to the schema having exactly the plan's rows, so no primitive, and to the nesting the never-narrowing cavity keeps.
- **Version 1.0.0** in `package.json`, `package-lock.json`'s two root entries and `src/app/version.ts`. SCHEMA_VERSION stays 3. In an export the version is only in README.txt's first paragraph and in report.json's and project.json's `appVersion`: STL headers carry none. A scratch test, deleted after, exported jersey, jersey in Mode B, jersey 2 x 1, jersey with the bolts and the barrel, each with the fit test, before and after the bump: all 121 files, every STL whole and past its 80-byte header, and every text file with the version string replaced and report.json's timings left out, gave the same SHA-256 (222 hashes). M9's hashes in `test/fixtures/m9-parts.json` and every golden pass unchanged. No expectation pinned 0.3.0 as the app's version; `e2e/project.spec.ts` writes files that say 0.3.0 as input, which 1.0.0 opens as format 3, so they stay.
- **Test time:** `scripts/ci.sh` took 10.8 minutes (647 s) once before the review. After it, on the final code, two runs with Firefox's display took 631 and 629 s (Vitest 164.1 and 164.6 s, 1,572 tests and 32 skipped in 49 files; Playwright 7.7 minutes, 117 tests and 12 skipped), against the plan's 10.5 minutes (630 s): at the figure, within a second either way. The first report put the 647 s down to the server's spread on "the same suite" at M11a, which was wrong: M11a's 650, 634 and 629 s were three different suites (650 s before the bad-file and soup-mode tests moved to Firefox and the fuzz to 8 sets, 634 s with the display proxy's 6 s Node test still in the quick suite), so they show no spread; the two runs here differ by 2 s. The review's changes added 21 Vitest tests and no browser test. A first run with no `DISPLAY` set took 537 s and failed: this server has no `xvfb-run`, so 28 of Firefox's WebGL tests skipped, and Firefox's two project-file smoke tests (`e2e/project.spec.ts`) failed on WebGL's console errors where they should skip as the others do. That predates M11b, and runs with `DISPLAY=:97`, a virtual display, as DEPLOY.md says, pass. `--full` takes 23.0 minutes (1,380 s; Vitest 516 s, 1,924 tests and 1 skipped; Playwright 14.4 minutes, 180 tests), against M11a's 1,383 s, all passing, with the display for Firefox. Swap stayed at 683 to 691 MB throughout.

Measured at M11c, a fix the user's file called for:
- **The bug:** `chick_mc.stl`, the user's modified copy of "Chirpy the Chick" (`examples/SOURCES.md`; R2 corrected the earlier "the user's own model") and now the 8th example (`chick`), is a closed binary STL of 146,186 triangles (73,105 vertices, 0 open edges, genus -5), 40.0 x 44.4 x 23.6 mm, made for multi-colour printing of 6 closed parts: 85,348 triangles (16,131.1 mm3), two of 14,136 (530.1 each), two of 11,122 (105.0 each) and one of 10,322 (376.1), the top of the head, which alone reaches 23.61 mm. The five smaller ones each pass into the largest, by 964.4 mm3 in all, so the parts sum to 17,777.6 mm3 and their union is 16,813.2 (Manifold; S8's winding reads one shell of 16,814.9). M4 took every connected piece of the mesh for a body, and each part with a peak of its own at the ceiling for one needing a pour, so placement made 6 bodies: pours on 3, a vent on 2, and 3 `pour-shortfall` errors, "Placed 0 of 1 pours on the body with its peak at (124.9, 104.0)", (118.8, 83.3) and (131.3, 83.3) at the export cell ((125.0, 104.0), (118.5, 83.5) and (131.5, 83.5) at the preview's), as the parts sunk in the body leave no room for a pour's disc on their own ceilings. An error turns Export ZIP off, in Chromium and Firefox alike, and a 6 mm pour still left one, so no setting got an export out of the app for this file. The import's overlap check saw the overlap, as a warning that the stated volume counts it twice, and report.json then gave the model's volume as 17,777.6, the parts' sum. S8 and the demold report, which work from the winding, already saw one body, and the section caps one model.
- **The rule now:** bodies are physical objects. Connected pieces whose surfaces come within 0.05 mm of each other (`TOUCH_MM`, the least plane tolerance the auto rule picks), which crossing surfaces do at 0, or of which one lies inside the other, are one body, and so is every chain of them (`touchingGroups`, `src/core/mesh/touching.ts`, pure, so soup mode and field.worker have it). Pieces whose boxes, grown by the tolerance, stay apart are never tested further, which is every pair of x8's barriers, 7.2 mm apart at the least; one piece is a group of its own with no test at all. The test is exact on the surfaces: the triangles of each piece in the boxes' common part, the smaller set in a grid of about one triangle's size, the larger looked up in it, each pair by the distance of two triangles (an edge of one through the other, a corner to a face, or an edge to an edge) and the first pair near enough ends it. A void's surface counts as that of the piece whose solid holds it (of the pieces whose box holds the void's and whose surface encloses it, the one with the smallest box), so a part resting on a sealed hollow's floor or passing through its wall touches that piece (M11c review: both were 2 bodies, the second one whichever corner came first). Only when no pair is near, and one piece's box lies in the other's, does one point of it, the centre of its first triangle, say whether it lies in the other's solid, hollows counted, so a part sitting in another's sealed hollow, touching nothing, stays apart: on a closed mesh by the faces the vertical line through the point crosses above it and below it, which must give the same winding number, and otherwise, or on an open mesh, by the generalised winding number of every triangle. The task offered joining by S8's column intervals instead; an exact test was chosen because the columns join solid stretches by any overlap between neighbouring columns, a cell (0.25 mm) apart sideways, and a gap thinner than a cell in a column, so their tolerance would be the analysis cell's and not one that could be stated, and a gap of 0.2 mm would join on one axis and not the other. An open mesh's winding number reads between 0 and 1 near a hole: with its underside taken away, each of Benchy's 8 lettering recesses reads 0.515 to 0.527 at a corner, so only a reading past 0.75 counts as inside, and those 8 stay buried fragments, as M4 records. A joined body's field is that of all its pieces' triangles together, the top envelope of their union, and one group, as the chick is, takes the tile field itself, as a one-piece model does; its centroid and extent come from its faces on the plane marked on a lattice at the field's cell, so the 33.5 mm2 of the chick's soles that lie on the body's underside count once. A body of one piece takes exactly the path it took before. The import counts bodies by the same rule on its mesh (report.json's `model.bodies`), as soup mode and `countBodies` do. The groups depend on the mesh alone, so field.worker works them out once per S2 and keeps them, a few numbers, while S2 stands; S7, which runs again on every opening moved or removed and every change of pours, t or tolerance, takes them as they are, passed to `findBodies` with the pieces, which it works out again as it did before M11c (25 ms on the chick, 0.25 to 0.33 s on 3 million triangles in one piece, whose lists of triangles would hold 35 MB if kept). Each piece's triangles go in a grid of their own the first time a small region of the piece is asked for, and a region over a quarter of its box is read in one pass instead. The review measured the first version on a sphere of 64,800 triangles with 500 cubes of 0.5 mm floating 0.5 mm off it, 5.5 s per S7 for the grouping alone (21.6 s with 2,000), as each pair whose boxes overlapped walked both pieces' triangles and summed the winding number over the sphere's; now 25 to 31 ms on the closed mesh, once per S2, held in `test/unit/placement.test.ts` under a second. On an open mesh, where the winding number is the rule, the same file still takes 3.9 s, once per S2: soup mode only, and no example is one. On the chick in Node `touchingGroups` takes 35 to 49 ms once warm (the first version 26 to 38 ms) besides `connectedPieces`' 25 ms, x8, Benchy and wolf under 0.1 ms, and S7 at the export cell 159 ms where it took 305 with six bodies' fields; nothing changes for a one-piece model, so the soak did not run again.
- **The chick at defaults:** one body, whose one significant leaf is the top part's peak, H 26.98 at (124.9, 104.0): one pour there, a vent beside it at (124.9, 94.2), bores of 5.05 and 9.99 mm, no other leaf past the persistence to vent, and no diagnostic from placement. Pours per body 2 and 3 give 2 and 3 pours on the one body, the extras spread round its centroid, with the soft funnel-collision warning, as 40 mm funnels do not fit side by side over a 40 mm model. The export in Node: 11.807 ml of silicone, clearance measured 3.255 mm (minGap 3.260 less the decimation's 0.005), box 68.5 x 72.9 x 36.0 mm, report.json's model volume 16,813.2 mm3 for both `model.volumeMm3` and `model_oriented.stl`, the closed-volume check one negative shell for the cavity and the groove residual in both modes, the Mode A pocket from the footprint's union (1,211.9 mm2), Mode B the union of the parts, and the demold verdict S8 gives, plausible, its largest under-plug body 0.026 ml, under the filter.
- **The note:** the overlap is no fault of the file, so `shell-overlap` is now a note, not a warning: "The file is made of 6 parts that overlap by 964.4 mm3, as models split for multi-colour printing often are. The mold treats them as one model, of 16813 mm3." Parts that only touch get `shell-touch`, "...6 parts that touch...", and a file whose parts make several bodies, "...some of which overlap by V mm3... so it makes N models of them, U mm3 in all." Parts that all stand apart, as x8's, get nothing. Wherever the app or the export states the model's volume, it is the union: the note, and report.json's `model.volumeMm3` and its `model_oriented.stl` entry, in every path: the whole solid, the heavy copy told the preview's import, and a trimmed model. A trim keeps the parts apart too, and takes away what they share below the plane, so a trimmed model's overlap is measured on the trimmed solid again (`sharedVolume`: its parts, each less its hollows, less their union), past G1.8's limit on the whole trimmed solid before it is freed; the first version took the whole model's overlap off a trimmed one, which the review found reads short by what the parts share under the plane, and went negative on planes picked through the chick's head (-651.3 mm3 where the union is 266.1). The status strip states no model volume. README.txt's model_oriented.stl entry adds, only when parts were joined: "It keeps the 6 parts your file is made of, which overlap or touch, as the file has them: slicers print overlapping parts as one." model_oriented.stl keeps the file's parts, so its own triangles sum to 17,777.6 mm3.
- **The silicone figure** takes the union: both the preview's and the export's take off the model's volume above the plane less what its parts share above it, which Manifold's union of the parts measures, 17,777.6 - 964.4 = 16,813.2 mm3 on the chick, and agrees with S8's winding (16,814.9) to 0.01%. Without that the chick's field figure would read 10.50 ml in place of 11.46. On a plane that trims the model, what the parts share above it is the trimmed parts' figure, as for report.json: the export's from its own trimmed solid, and the preview's from the solid worker's footprint stage (S2), which trims the model for the plane already, and says so with the levels; until that is in for the plane on screen, the preview takes the import's. Past G1.8's limit the preview's comes from the trimmed copy, the export's from the whole solid. The review's chick planes read 0.59 to 0.92 ml too high in the first version. An open mesh has no Manifold and so no overlap figure, and an open file of overlapping parts would count their overlap twice in its silicone, as before M11c; no example is one.
- **Other places that read pieces:** grid replication, the vent beside each first pour, `cavity-without-pour`, funnel collision and the off-peak rule all work per body of placement, and now see one on the chick; the Demold report and the fit test never read pieces. The README's counts are of openings and copies, unchanged. What the fix does not touch: the base face's listed area is its faces' sum, as G2 scores it, so the chick's -Z reads 1,245 mm2, counting its 33.5 mm2 of shared soles twice, where its footprint, pocket and stubs use the union, 1,211.9 mm2; the ranking is not affected here (the next face is 210 mm2).
- **Results that did not change:** every golden of the seven, x8's 8 bodies and 8 pours, and a scratch test, deleted after, that exported 12 cases on 87ff4fd and on this code in Node (the seven at defaults, x8 in merged mode, at 3 pours per body and on a 2 x 1 grid, jersey with the bolts and the barrel in merged mode) and hashed every file, STLs whole, README.txt and project.json with the version string masked, report.json without its timings, and the openings placement made: all 94 hashes are the same. M9's part hashes pass as they were.
- **Tests:** a table of synthetic files of two parts in `test/unit/placement.test.ts`, each read through the weld, the pure grouping, `countBodies`, the import with Manifold and placement: two boxes overlapping, sharing a face exactly, 0.049 mm apart, crossing as a plus sign with no corner of either in the other, a corner 0.049 mm over the middle of another's top, edges crossing 0.049 mm apart, a part buried inside a big one and a hat, one body each, and as controls two boxes 0.051 and 0.5 mm apart, edges 0.051 mm apart, and a part in another's sealed hollow, two (0.04 and 0.06 before the review). Each way of finding a contact (an edge through a face, a corner to a face, an edge to an edge, a corner inside) was taken out in turn, and each time a row failed. Run against 87ff4fd's rule, every row but the controls fails, as do the chick's tests. Beside them: a joined body vents its lower peak and pours at its higher one (two towers on a bridge, two pours before), stands where its parts' union does (centroid 20 where the sum gave 19), and takes 3 pours at 3 per body; the note's wording; and README.txt's sentence. Changed, with the rule: the import's overlapping cubes are now 1 body where they were 2; the placement test of a piece under another's ceiling had a box inside the bigger one, touching its base plane, which is now part of that body, so its piece now stands between an arch's legs, under its top and touching nothing; the M3 closed-volume check takes off the union of the model's parts in merged mode, as Mode B builds it, where it took off their sum (920 mm3 too much on the chick); M1's footprint is held to the faces' union sampled on a 0.05 mm lattice, and to their sum only where nothing overlaps. The heavy path's hollow block gains a part passing into its top, so the copy's report gives the union (190,250 mm3 where the sum would be 192,500). The plan has no figure of its own for the chick, so its golden numbers are its own, with these parameters, those of each table: M1 at defaults (above, base face 1,245.4 mm2 at z 0, footprint 1,211.9); M2 at plan defaults 11.46 ml, bare 10.16 and off the lattice 10.09; M3 at defaults silicone 11.41 ml, cover 134,573, glue 16,190 and merged 33,252 mm3, minGap 3.2600 and 3.2601 at draft 0, 12,344 TIN triangles, box 68.456 x 72.941, Zt 32.026; M4 1 body, 1 cavity, 1 leaf, 1 significant, no plateau, 1 pour, 1 vent, longest bore 9.99 mm, 0.344 ml in the bores, the cut cover 133,906 mm3; M7 plausible, 1 body on 1 footprint, nothing past the filter, 2 raw pockets and no sealed air; M10 cover 173,821, glue 20,432, merged 37,494, box 76.748 x 81.233, slot floor 2.826, walls 14.685 to the cavity and 24.088 to the notches; the preview 12.25 ml and the export 11.807. **One check reads differently:** the preview's silicone is 4.2% over M3's solids with the bores (12.248 against 11.754 ml) where `test/unit/derived.test.ts` holds the seven to 3% (the barrel's 2.5% the most): its cell, 0.5 mm against 0.125, makes 3.0% of it and its TIN raise, 0.1 against 0.05, the rest, as at the export's cell and raise it reads 0.45% over, held within 0.5% as the barrel and jersey are; a small, curved model has little silicone for the surface the coarse cell thickens. The chick is held to 4.5% there, an open item for the user to accept or not (`docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, section 9). Its timing budgets come from the M3 and M4 tables, which only `--record` measures, so the pipeline budget test leaves it out.
- **The browser:** `e2e/export.spec.ts` drops the chick, finds one pour within 1 mm of (125, 104) and a vent, no error and no shortfall in Diagnostics, the note there, every export button on, then saves the ZIP and checks its openings, its volume, README.txt and the clearance, and since the review cuts across x at 125 mm, as the review shot looks at it, and finds no pixel of silicone's colour with the model's cap on both sides of it: 14.3 s in Firefox in the quick suite and 14.2 s in Chromium with `CFG_FULL`, as M11a's hardening tests run. With `CFG_FULL` the chick also runs every check of M3, M4 and M10 in Node, and the app and export sweeps of all eight in the browser.
- **Review shots** (`CFG_SHOTS=1`): `chick-mc`, the Pour & funnel rows with one pour and one vent, their rings over the head, the note in Diagnostics and Export ZIP on, with the view moved right and up, as M11a's warning shots move it the other way, so the bottom-left Diagnostics card covers none of the mold (the review found its front-left corner under the card); and `chick-mc-section-head`, a cut across x through the head's middle, where the top part passes into the body: one model cap, with silicone of even thickness round it and the pour's bore and seat over the peak; at 1366 x 768 in both themes in Chromium and dark in Firefox.
- **The review** found, besides the volume of a trimmed model and the grouping's cost (above):
  - *A hollow's wall.* Void pieces were left out of the surface test, so a part resting on a sealed hollow's floor, or passing through its wall, was a body of its own, or not, by which corner came first. A void's surface is now its holder's (above). Rows in the table: a part through the far wall of another's hollow, from inside it, and a part on the hollow's floor, one body each; `test/unit/shells.test.ts` takes the import's cube through the void's far wall as well as its near one.
  - *Controls the box test decided.* The 0.06 mm boxes never reached the test on the surfaces, as their boxes, grown by the tolerance, already stood apart. Four rows whose boxes overlap: a box 0.049 and 0.051 mm over a U channel's floor, 1 mm from its walls, which the boxes of the facing triangles tell; and a tetrahedron's corner 0.049 and 0.051 mm off the middle of another's face x + y + z = 30, slanted to every axis, where only the corner's distance to the face tells, so the squared distance compared with the tolerance unsquared, which makes it 0.22 mm, fails it (seen). The review's L was the first idea for that row, but its gap lies along z, so the triangles' boxes decided it too. The table also runs each row with the closed mesh's line test, which gives the same groups.
  - *Several bodies of several parts.* A file of two figures 20 mm apart, each a box with a taller part off its middle: 2 bodies, no fragment, each pour inside its own part's top at the tile's peak there, and the import's note, "The file is made of 4 parts, some of which overlap by 1000.0 mm3, ... so it makes 2 models of them, 20200 mm3 in all."; a body's field from its first part alone fails it (seen). Exported in Node (`test/unit/export.test.ts`), report.json has 2 bodies and 20,200 mm3, and README.txt "It keeps the 4 parts your file is made of, some of which overlap or touch".
  - *Soup mode.* An open file now gets the note too, with no figure, as an open surface has no union to measure: "The file is made of 2 parts that overlap or touch, as models split for multi-colour printing often are. The mold treats them as one model." `test/unit/shells.test.ts` holds two open boxes passing into each other to 1 body and that note, and the same apart to 2 and no note. README.md and WORKFLOW.md say the overlap is given for a closed mesh.
  - *The cut's seam.* The model's cap was counted from all six parts, and along the top part's walls inside the body single samples read the model as absent, so the silicone, drawn where its count is exactly 1, showed in a dotted U (42 to 50 pixels in the shots). The import now keeps the union of joined parts (the one it measures the overlap with) and the cut is counted from it, the silicone's from its triangles less its void shells, up to the display proxy's 300,000 triangles; past it the cut counts the parts as before, as a second whole mesh on main costs memory a heavy model has not to spare. The browser test above finds 31 such pixels in Chromium and 1 in Firefox with the union taken out, and none with it; the review shots none in all three.
  - *Mode B's loose parts.* A union fuses only what overlaps, so a hat 0.04 mm over a block stayed a shell of its own and drew "One part of this model does not touch the plate", beside the note that the parts are one model. The loose parts are now the groups of the merged base's shells by `touchingGroups`, less the plate's (`test/regression/solid.test.ts`: 0.04 mm over, two shells and no warning; 0.5 mm over, the warning). M10's control of a post 0.03 mm over the plate with no stub to tie it is two shells still, and now no loose part, as within the tolerance it prints fused.
  - *Pins of older projects.* Pins and removed vents name bodies by number ("b2:pour:0"), and 1.0.0 numbered each part as a body. A project saved before 1.0.1 (by its `appVersion`; one without it counts as older) whose model the import finds joining parts opens with its pins and removed vents reset and a warning, "This project was saved by version 1.0.0 of the app, which took each part of the file for a model of its own. Parts that overlap or touch are one model since version 1.0.1, so the openings it moved and the vents it removed were reset: move or remove them again.", which leaves it unsaved; one saved since, or of a model of one part, keeps them; settings applied from such a project.json the same (`test/unit/project.test.ts`).
  - *The chick's shot* is moved clear of the Diagnostics card (above).

Parts 0.051 mm or more apart are bodies of their own by the rule, and a small one with no room for a pour of its own gets `pour-shortfall`, an error that keeps Export ZIP off, while S8's columns, a cell apart, see one shell and a print would fuse a gap that thin; `test/unit/placement.test.ts` holds a 6 x 6 mm part 0.06 mm over a block to that, and 0.04 mm over to one body and no error. Tying the tolerance to the analysis cell or a share of the layer height would change the stated rule, so it waits for the user. The chick's preview held to 4.5% where the seven are held to 3% (above) and the quick suite's time (below) are open in the same way.
  - A one-piece model takes the path it took before, with no test and nothing more kept, so the soak was not run again.
