- **Version 1.0.1**, project format 3 still.

### R1: First public release, v0.1.0 on GitHub Pages

Not one of the plan's milestones: the user's decisions of 2026-11-09 for publishing the app (`docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, D10 to D15, R32 to R35). M0 to M11c stand as they were. The app is renamed Mold Form Generator (the user's "mold-from-generator", whose spelling the user confirms before the first push); the site is https://kajtomato.github.io/mold-form-generator/, whose root shows the newest tag vX.Y.Z on `main` and whose `/beta/` shows the newest commit of the branch `beta`; public versions start at 0.1.0 and the page shows its version. Whether the source is public, and what becomes of the third-party example models, is decided by the user before the first push, so nothing here depends on it: `examples/` and the history are untouched. The live memory meter the user asked for is future work (REQUIREMENTS.md, section 8).

Checks:
- Every text a user reads says Mold Form Generator: the header, the titles of index, diag, bench and pipeline, the export's README.txt, the STL header, the notices file and README.md and the docs about the app; `package.json` and both root entries of `package-lock.json` say `mold-form-generator`. Names no one reads keep the first name's `cfg`: the theme's storage key `cfg-theme`, the build's variables `CFG_CHANNEL`, `CFG_COMMIT` and `CFG_BUILD_DATE`, the temporary folders, so a theme chosen before R1 survives and no later rename needs a migration. The rename changes no geometry: every exported STL is the same past its 80-byte header, which the test of M9's hashes checks for good, M9's own hashes against R1's files with M9's header text put back.
- The version is written in one place, `package.json` (0.1.0), which `package-lock.json` repeats and the build reads, so the check of a release tag against `package.json` covers what the page shows. The first public version is 0.1.0: `package.json` must say so while no release is tagged, and the lowest release tag must be v0.1.0 once one is. Project format 4 says the pins number bodies as joined parts: a format 4 project keeps its pins, a format 3 one only if 1.0.1 saved it, and any other is reset with M11c's warning, by tests of 3 (1.0.0), 3 (1.0.1) and 4. No version numbers are compared anywhere: a scan of `src` lists every line that tests a version, and none orders two.
- One build works at `/`, `/mold-form-generator/` and `/mold-form-generator/beta/` (Vite's base `./`): every page (index, diag, bench and pipeline, each opened in the browser), the workers (field, solid, project and diag), the WebAssembly, the notices file and every link load under the sub-path in Chromium and Firefox, and the build served at the root by the preview project, as `scripts/serve.sh` serves it, still passes its tests.
- `src/app/buildInfo.ts`, fed by Vite's define: the version from `package.json`, the channel (`CFG_CHANNEL`: stable, beta or local, local by default), the short commit (`CFG_COMMIT`, else git with `-dirty` for changes, else unknown) and a date in UTC (`CFG_BUILD_DATE`, else the time of the build). On the two sites the date is the commit's, so building the same commit again gives the same files with the same names, and the release built again for a push to beta never takes away a worker a page already open still loads. The version string is the release's number on stable, `0.2.0-beta+a1b2c3d` on beta and `0.1.0-local+a1b2c3d` elsewhere, in project.json, README.txt and report.json; STL headers carry none. The header shows it after the name in the muted ink, and on beta an amber BETA chip in the warn tokens and a "Stable version" link to `../`, with a native tooltip that says in a maker's words what kind of build it is, the date of its code and its commit; still 48 px, nothing clipped at 1366 px, in both themes, with a model open on beta. Every page of a beta build carries `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">`.
- `scripts/build-pages.sh <stable-ref> <beta-ref or -> <out-dir>` builds each ref in a temporary git worktree outside the repository, removed even on failure, reusing `node_modules` when the ref's lockfile pins the same packages as the working tree's (the root entry's name and version aside) and saying which way it went, passes the commit's date, writes `.nojekyll`, never writes into `dist/`, `dist-ci/` or the working tree, and refuses a tag vX.Y.Z that its `package.json` does not say. It builds the release first and stops if it fails; when only the beta fails, the release is still built, `beta/` holds a `noindex` page saying the beta is not available, and a `::error::` line says why. `.github/workflows/pages.yml` runs it on a pushed tag `v*`, a push to `beta` or by hand, with Node 24, the newest `v*` tag on `origin/main` by version and `origin/beta` if it exists, fails a run whose pushed tag is not the one it publishes, naming why, and deploys with one concurrency group and the site's dotfiles; a unit test holds its triggers, permissions, Node version and the script's call, and runs its choice of refs. No tests run on GitHub yet.
- `docs/RELEASING.md` for the owner, with the exact commands: a release, merging `main` back into `beta`, a quick fix, taking a bad release down, what a failed build does, and the one-time setup in an order that works; "site only" (D15) is said to need work not built yet. README.md, DEPLOY.md and REQUIREMENTS.md updated.

User: confirm the name's spelling; the one-time GitHub setup and the first push, after deciding about the example models; then U8, both addresses checked.

Measured at R1:
- **The rename and the geometry:** a scratch test, deleted after, exported 7 cases in Node with the fit test, on an archive of d5a5750 and on R1's code: jersey, in Mode B, on a 2 x 1 grid and with the bolts, the barrel in both modes, and the chick. All 168 files match: the 140 STLs past their 80-byte header, the 28 text files with the app's name and version masked and whitespace folded (README.txt's first paragraph wraps elsewhere, as both have new lengths), report.json less `app`, `appVersion`, `schemaVersion` and its timings, project.json less `appVersion` and `schemaVersion`; the geometry hashes are the same too. M9's 24 hashes in `test/fixtures/m9-parts.json` (cover and base of four cases, and the fit test's 16 pieces) are M9's own, as M9 exported them; `test/unit/export.test.ts` checks that each exported STL's header reads `binary STL, mold-form-generator` and that, with M9's header `binary STL, casting-form-generator` put back in the first 80 bytes, it gives M9's hash, so that proof stays in the suite and holds through any later rename. `diag.html`'s test downloads are `download-test.stl` and `download-test.zip` (they were `cfg-test.*`). The folder `scripts/install.sh` unpacks Firefox's GTK into, `~/.cache/casting-form-generator`, lies outside the repository and keeps its name, which `e2e/firefox.ts` shares.
- **Format 4:** the migration 3 to 4 changes no field; the pipeline is told the format the file was written in (`ReadProject.from`) with its app version, and `bodiesByPart` decides by the format. Only internal builds wrote formats before 4, so the warning names the build as one, its number in brackets, where "version 1.0.0 of the app" beside a header reading v0.1.0 would read as a later version: "This project was saved by an early test build of the app (1.0.0), from before the public versions, which took each part of the file for a model of its own. Parts that overlap or touch are now one model, so...". A project of a newer format saved on beta says to open it on the beta site, as a visitor cannot update a web page; any other says to open it in the version that saved it.
- **Base `./`:** the pages load `./assets/...`, the workers come from `new URL('x.js', import.meta.url)` and the WebAssembly from `new URL('manifold-....wasm', import.meta.url)`, all relative to the file that asks. `diag.html` and `bench.html` gained `<link rel="icon" href="data:,">`, as index and pipeline had, since without one a browser asks for `/favicon.ico` at the host's root, outside the site, which the Pages test counts as a request leaving it.
- **The config:** `vite.config.ts` exports `buildConstants`, `buildDefines` and the `noindex` plugin and imports none of the app's code, as Vite 8 warned about extensionless imports in a config's own imports; `vitest.config.ts` imports it with its `.ts` extension (`allowImportingTsExtensions`, with `noEmit`) and defines the same constants, so the tests are a local build that knows its commit. The version comes into the build by the define rather than by importing `package.json`, as Playwright's test workers, which load the app's modules for their Node runs, refuse a JSON import without an import attribute.
- **The Pages build:** both sites in 1.5 s on this server, with `node_modules` reused through a link in the worktree's parent folder, where git does not see it. Vite runs with `--configLoader runner`, so it writes no bundled config into `node_modules/.vite-temp` (checked by file times). The workflow uses `actions/checkout@v7`, `actions/setup-node@v7`, `actions/upload-pages-artifact@v5` with `include-hidden-files: true` (v4 stopped packing dotfiles, `.nojekyll` among them) and `actions/deploy-pages@v5`, the newest majors on their release pages on 2026-11-09. The lockfile carries the app's version, which differs between the release and beta after every release, so the script compares the lockfiles without the root entry's name and version, as `scripts/install.sh` already did; before the review it compared bytes, and every real run would have installed one of the two sites with `npm ci`.
- **The same commit, the same files:** two builds in memory with the release's constants give the same 15 files byte for byte, and another `CFG_BUILD_DATE` renames the four workers, as the date is in their code (`test/unit/release.test.ts`). Before the review the date was the time of the build, so each push to beta, which rebuilds the release, renamed the release's workers and took the old ones off the site, where a page already open would look for them at its next export or save.
- **The Pages test** (`e2e/pages.spec.ts`): the script builds committed refs and this run's changes are not committed, so the test commits a snapshot of the working tree (tracked and new files, ignored ones and `examples/` left out) into a scratch repository in the system's temporary folder and builds its HEAD for both sites with the snapshot's own script; with a clean tree that is HEAD's content, with its own commit id. It checks that the build left the snapshot as committed, with no worktree, and wrote `.nojekyll`. A static server of its own, on a port the system picks, serves the site under `/mold-form-generator/` as GitHub Pages would (a folder to its `index.html`, a 404 for anything else), with `no-store` so its log holds every file each page loads, and the examples at `/examples/` for the test hooks. On each site it opens index (a model, its preview, Save project, which starts project.worker), diag (its worker boots Manifold), bench (its worker and the WebAssembly arrive; the minute-long benchmark is not waited for) and pipeline (`draw=0`, jersey through both workers to a cover). Seen to fail: with the base `/` (the header's version never shows, as the scripts 404) and with the `noindex` plugin left out (the robots list). `test/unit/release.test.ts` holds the build info and its tooltip, the names and the version, the theme key, the scan for version comparisons, the same files from the same commit, the config, the workflow's text, the workflow's own script that picks the refs (run in a scratch repository: the highest vX.Y.Z on main, with v0.10.0 on the middle commit of three tagged v0.1.0, v0.10.0 and v0.9.0, so a sort by name or by date either way picks another, a higher tag off main and tags not of that form left out, beta only once the branch exists, the stop with no release, and a pushed tag that is not published turning the run red with its reason), and the script in a scratch repository of one page and this checkout's packages: its refusals, a build of both sites with `node_modules` reused where only the version differs, a beta that fails with the release still built, a lockfile that pins another package going to `npm ci` (offline, so it fails at once) and the release's failure stopping it, each leaving no worktree, temporary folder or change behind.
- **One build per test run:** the browser tests compare the browser's export with Node's byte for byte, and the first quick suite failed 8 of them, as Node in the test workers said `0.1.0-local+unknown` where the build said `0.1.0-local+d5a5750-dirty`. `playwright.config.ts` now works out the build's constants once and hands them to the servers' builds (`CFG_CHANNEL`, `CFG_COMMIT` and `CFG_BUILD_DATE` in the environment) and to the test workers (the same, and as globals buildInfo.ts reads where no build defined them). Vitest's define is worked out once when its config loads, and a test that rewrites a tracked file during the run (`--record` rewrites `docs/BENCHMARKS.md`) makes a clean tree dirty, so the check of the tests' commit asks for HEAD exactly when the tree is clean and accepts HEAD with or without `-dirty` when it is not. The same run failed the Pages test once in Firefox, on the root's runtime chunk, which the test's next navigation (to beta's diag.html) had cut off while the root page still loaded it: the test now opens `about:blank` before each page it checks, and names the browser's reason for a failed load.
- **Review shots** (`CFG_SHOTS=1`, in `e2e/pages.spec.ts`): `r1-header-stable-*` and `r1-header-beta-*`, the whole window and the header's strip, at 1366 x 768 in both themes in Chromium and dark in Firefox, with jersey open.
- **Test time:** `scripts/ci.sh` takes 11.3 minutes (675.7 s; Vitest 178.4 s, 1,658 tests and 34 skipped in 50 files; Playwright 8.2 minutes, 124 tests and 17 skipped), with the display for Firefox, all passing, swap at 688 MB: 13 s over M11c's 662.7 s and 16 s over the plan's 11 minutes, which the Pages smoke tests and release.test.ts account for. That was before the review's changes, which add browser work to the Pages smoke tests (bench, pipeline, a save, a preview on beta, the header laid out in two themes) and five tests to release.test.ts. After them, `scripts/ci.sh` passed with Vitest at 180.7 s (1,665 tests and 34 skipped in 50 files) and Playwright at 8.3 minutes (124 tests and 17 skipped), with the display for Firefox, swap at 693 MB; the run's total was not timed, and these two parts add up to 11.3 minutes before the typecheck. The Pages smoke tests take 2.0 to 3.6 s each in Chromium and 1.5 to 2.8 s in Firefox.

### BL: the silicone base layer

The user's request of 2026-11-09, before the first release (`docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, R36, D16): silicone spreads over the base plate, so the copies of a grid and the bodies of a file are one mold and not a jacket each, with a depth the user sets that follows t and may be 0. Built first as 1.0.2 on a branch of d5a5750 and served on the LAN for the user's test pours, then joined to R1 with project format 5.

Checks:
- `src/core/field/baseLayer.ts` raises H to at least the layer's depth on every node the tool keeps inside D (one cell in from it, so `maskOverrun` stays 0 and the groove and ridge are untouched). It only raises H, so clearance, nesting and the never-narrowing rule hold as before. Its top is a cone of slope k (Base layer slope, 2 degrees) from the cavity cut off at depth + k R, where R is the layer's farthest reach from the cavity by chamfer distance: the farthest corner is the depth, the rest rises towards the models with no flat ring at a jacket's foot, and no new peak appears for air to gather under (the merge tree's significant leaves are unchanged). Its outer edge keeps the wall draft: a node stands no higher than its distance to the edge over tan(draft). Closing runs again over the result. At a depth of 0, `buildField` gives the field of before, value for value.
- S6 makes the layer from D, which follows from S5's own inputs, so S6 depends on no S10 parameter; its own two parameters are read fresh in field.worker, as S5's cached settings predate any change of them (the first test build missed a change of depth in the preview until this was fixed, and a test of the staged field now holds it). The layer goes into the field the tool is cut from and the reports read; placement and the bodies read the tile without it, and the walls between tiles are measured without it, as they stand above it, so a grid joined by the layer gives no merged-tiles warning.
- Placement: a cavity of the tile that the layer joins to an opened one on the whole mold fills through the layer, so it gets no pour; it gets a vent at a significant leaf of the whole mold inside it, if one is there (`cavity-vent`).
- The solids, at defaults with the layer on (x8, and jersey 2 x 2; all eight and benchy 2 x 2 with `CFG_FULL`): every check of M3 and M4, one cavity, and no plastic in the cover's slice just under the depth anywhere in D a mm in from its edge. The goldens of M2 to M7 are read with the layer off (`goldenParams` in `test/regression/golden.ts`), as all were measured before it.
- Project format 5 holds the layer's two settings; formats 4 and older open with the layer at 0, so they make the mold they made, and a file of format 4 that already holds them, as the 1.0.2 test build wrote, keeps them.

Measured at BL:
- **Silicone at defaults, preview, ml, with the layer (and without):** benchy 45.19 (43.32), goddess 122.45 (118.39), vis35 536.52 (530.02), jersey 11.62 (11.53), x8 92.97 (89.52), barrel 11.27 (10.52), wolf 226.47 (207.41), chick 14.60 (12.25): 0.8% to 19% more. A 2 x 2 grid of benchy exported at 182.9 ml, measured clearance 3.21 mm.
- **Lift:** the layer's wide, low ceiling takes the wolf's lift from 11.0 to 20.6 N, more than its cover's 11.8 N, so the tape advice goes from 3 strips to 5; the wolf's cover is 2.41 l, from 2.43.
- **The browser's export** at defaults (Chromium, from the button, checked against a Node build): benchy 45.1881 ml, goddess 122.3068, vis35 548.0961, jersey 11.6832, x8 91.7855, barrel 11.1166 (in Mode B too), wolf 223.2889, chick 14.336, jersey on a 2 x 3 grid 72.2966 (68.1545 before the layer); measured clearance 3.20 to 3.28 mm on all of them; Firefox gives jersey's and wolf's figures to the digit. The export takes 0.4 to 11.6 s in the browser's worker, as before.
- **Time:** the layer takes 69 to 139 ms at the export cell (x8, vis35 and wolf about 70 to 85 ms, benchy 2 x 2 139 ms on 783k nodes), beside 45 to 52 ms of closing.

### R2: the example models for a public repository

Not one of the plan's milestones: the user's decisions of 2026-11-11, before the first push (`docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, section 2, D15 answered, D17, R37). The source repository is public, with the example models whose source is known and whose licence allows sharing them; the files with no source are not published; the name Mold Form Generator, `mold-form-generator`, is confirmed. The chick is a modified copy of "Chirpy the Chick", not the user's own model. While the run was under way the user asked, too, that the repository name neither the console maker nor its characters (section 2, in other words, as the user's own named them). This run moves files and guards tests: no geometry and no golden number changes. The git history is rewritten by the orchestrator after it, to take the two unpublished files out.

Checks:
- **Layout.** The user's models from `examples/with-sources/` are in `examples/` under the names the user gave, but for one renamed `charge-and-play-stand.stl` as its name held the barred names (below), with the bytes the user gave, and the folder is gone; its `sources.txt` is now `examples/SOURCES.md`. Its copies of Benchy, the chick, Goddess, both jersey files and the wolf matched those in `examples/` by SHA-256 and were dropped. `wolfinal(1).stl` is `wolfinal.stl`, the same bytes (SHA-256 3b18dc13...), and its references follow: `test/fixtures/loadExamples.ts`, and the download-name test, which keeps a name with brackets as a case of its own.
- **The 3MF.** The app reads STL only, so `Factorio gear.3mf` became `examples/Factorio gear.stl`: a short script, run outside the repository and not kept, read `3D/3dmodel.model`, applied each component's and build item's transform (one item, no transform, no components here), turned the model's unit, `meter`, into millimetres, and wrote the triangles in their winding, reversed only under a transform that mirrors (none here), as a binary STL whose header names the model, its author and its page. The app's own import in Node reads it as closed (0 open, 0 non-manifold, 0 misoriented edges), on the `manifold` route, not flipped, one body of genus 1, 3,346.4 mm3, with the 3MF's 3,592 triangles and 1,796 vertices and its size, 39 x 39 x 5 mm. The 3MF is not kept in `examples/`.
- **`examples/SOURCES.md`.** A table of every model file git tracks in `examples/`: the file, the model's name linked to its Printables page by the model's number alone (`https://www.printables.com/model/<number>`, which Printables takes to the page; the full address ends in the title), the author as Printables names them, the licence by its full name linked to its deed, and whether and how it was changed. The licences were read from Printables' public GraphQL API on 2026-11-11 (its pages refuse scripts), as the page says, and match the orchestrator's table: each page links the 4.0 International deed, and CC0 1.0 for the public domain dedications. Two models go by short names of their own, the Charge and Play Stand and the Goddess Statue, as their titles name the console maker and its characters, and the Goddess Statue's uploader, whose user name is a character's, is given by the address of their profile, `https://www.printables.com/@<handle>` with the handle the API gives. Creative Commons 4.0 attribution asks for the creator, the licence and a link to the work, not its title. It says the models are not under the MIT licence and keep their authors' terms, what NonCommercial, NoDerivatives and ShareAlike mean here, that the modified chick is shared under its original's licence, that some models are fan works whose names, characters and items belong to their owners, naming none, that the Factorio gear's conversion is a change of format only (section 2(a)(4) of the 4.0 licences), and that two further test models with no known source stay on the maintainer's machine, with the tests that need them skipping. `README.md` (a new section, The example models, and its Licence section) and `docs/THIRD_PARTY.md` point to it. `LICENSE` is the plain MIT text and names no folder: the exclusion of `examples/` is said in `README.md`, `docs/THIRD_PARTY.md` and `examples/SOURCES.md`, and `LICENSE` is left as the standard text.
- **Tests without the two local models.** `test/fixtures/loadExamples.ts` marks `vis35` and `barrel` `LOCAL_ONLY`; `hasExample(id)` says whether a local-only file is in the checkout, and counts every published one as here, so a published model that goes missing fails the tests that read it instead of skipping them or dropping out of a loop; `CFG_WITHOUT_LOCAL_EXAMPLES=1` makes the local-only ones count as missing, so the suite can be run as a clone of the public repository runs it; `exampleBytes` refuses a missing one there too, so a test that reads one in Node without skipping fails. In the browser the page fetches `/examples/<file>` itself, from the development server or the harness's route, which read the disk, so the harnesses (`e2e/appHarness.ts`, `e2e/pipelineHarness.ts` and the Pages server of `e2e/pages.spec.ts`) answer a missing one with a 404 on every server, as a clone's would; a smoke test, "the servers answer an example not in this checkout with a 404", holds that in both modes. `EXAMPLE_IDS` is the examples in the checkout, so every loop over the whole set drops a missing local-only one. `scripts/ci.sh` names the two at its end when they were missing. Every test or group that needs VIS-35 or the barrel calls `skipWithout(test, id)`, which skips it with the note "not in this checkout: <file>, kept only on the maintainer's machine (examples/SOURCES.md)": in a test's body, from a group's `beforeEach` (the shared molds of `test/regression/solid.test.ts` and `openings.test.ts` also build nothing then), or per row, where an `.each` table holds a local-only model, which for that reason became `.for`, as `.each` gives no test context. A loop inside one test over a chosen few drops the missing ones, as the pure fuzz spreads its sets over the models it has. Where a test checks mostly published models and a local-only one in a line or two, only those lines are guarded with `hasExample`, so a clone runs the rest: the bolts in each corner (VIS-35 as the box deeper than wide), the four S8 tests over every example in `test/regression/reports.test.ts` (the barrel's void and filters, VIS-35's verdict), and the plane kept in the STL's coordinates in `test/unit/project.test.ts` (VIS-35's upper level). Test titles are as they were. With both files present, nothing more skips than at R1 and BL.
- **The catalogue check.** `test/unit/examples.test.ts`, in the quick suite: `examples/SOURCES.md` lists exactly the model files git tracks in `examples/`, each with a Printables page, an author, a licence of the known list by its full name with its own deed, and whether it was changed, a NoDerivatives model unchanged; the two local-only files are lines of `.gitignore` that git matches, not tracked and not rows of the table; `README.md` and `docs/THIRD_PARTY.md` point to the file, which says what the licences mean. Its negative controls make each rule fail on a small table. "Tracked" is git's index, which the next commit holds: a model not yet added fails as a row with no file, a local-only file still in the index fails, and a file dropped into `examples/` only to try the app is not the catalogue's concern. Before the changes are staged, as this run's are (the orchestrator stages and commits), `CFG_UNSTAGED=1` counts them as staged: the index and the new files, less those deleted and those `.gitignore` names, which `git rm --cached` takes out. Negative controls run the same function on lists made up for them, in both modes. So "not tracked" holds for real only once the orchestrator has staged the change with `git rm --cached` of the two files, and "not in the history" is not tested: the orchestrator's rewrite does that before the first push.
- **No barred names** (the user's request of 2026-11-11, section 2). A quick test reads every file `git add -A` would publish (`git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`, as on disk): a text file whole, a binary STL's 80-byte header, and nothing of another binary; their paths; and the commit messages. It fails on the console maker's name, its games' and characters' names, case aside and as whole words (no letter on either side, so `_` and digits part words), and on "switch" in a file name, where the console stood; in text "switch" is ordinary code, and after the maker's name it is caught by that name. The names are spelled backwards in the test, so that it does not hold them itself. Negative controls make each name fail in a line, a file name, a binary STL's header and an ASCII STL, and let `switch` in code and a longer word holding a name pass.
- **The new models through the app** (`CFG_FULL`, the same file): each of the seven goes through `Pipeline` in Node with the real stage code in workers of its own, as the app runs it with the reports on: parse, weld, import, base candidates, field, placement, the Demold report, and an export ZIP at defaults with the base layer (no fit test). No pipeline error, no diagnostic of severity error before the export, in the export's own findings, in report.json's or in the Diagnostics list once the export is current, and no failure of the reports in the export; Export not blocked, the ZIP's parts present, cover and base closed, `model_oriented.stl` with the file's triangles. Every model but the oil barrel is closed, so its clearance is measured, and at least t; the oil barrel's is not. The Factorio gear is held to what the conversion gave: 3,592 triangles, no open, non-manifold or misoriented edge, the `manifold` route, not flipped, one solid of genus 1, 3,346.4 mm3. A check that the seven are the tracked models outside the regression set keeps the list honest.
- **Docs.** `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`: the user's words of 2026-11-11 in section 2, and the request on names in other words, D15 answered, the name's spelling answered, R37 mapped to the tests above, D17, and section 9's two items answered. `docs/RELEASING.md` says the source is public and how an example is added, and no longer keeps "public without the models" or "site only" open. `docs/DEPLOY.md` says how the tests behave without the two models.

  | `charge-and-play-stand.stl` | 38,408 | 94.7 x 103.1 x 106.8 | 1 | -z, 2,235 mm2 | 289.74 | 3.23 mm | 4.8 | cut-required |

  The file took 55.6 s with the seven, on one Vitest worker. At review, with the export's own findings, report.json's and the reports' failure checked too, and the gear held to its conversion, a second run gave every figure the same but two export times, 14.1 and 3.5 s, in 55.3 s.
- **What the app says about them,** all as designed, none an error: the oil barrel has a 3.1 mm2 hole (24 open edges in one loop), so it runs in soup mode, with merged mode off and its clearance unverified, and a 70-triangle piece of about 1 mm3 counts as a part that touches; the war barricade, which the user modified, has 243 non-manifold edges and 17 parts that touch, 16 of them closed slivers of 4 triangles and no volume, an info "A sealed internal void of 0 mm3 was found", and the upper-level warning of a 608 mm2 level 0.76 mm up; the sci-fi barricade's bottom is rough (511 of 1,172 mm2 within the 0.05 mm plane tolerance); the shipping container is 2 parts that touch; the charge-and-play stand's cavity is 110 mm tall, so the 4 to 5 mm advice shows. The doomed marine, a sculpted figure with no flat base, stands on a candidate of 3.3 mm2, the only part of its 72 mm2 soles within the plane tolerance, with the warnings that the surface looks rough (raise the tolerance) and that every footprint island is under the 5 mm2 limit, so the Mode A pocket follows the model's section only; it exports with 3.21 mm measured. No code changed for any of them: this is the app's documented behaviour on such files.
- **Skips, with both local models present:** `DISPLAY=:97 scripts/ci.sh --full` passed in 27.8 minutes (1,669 s): Vitest 700.7 s, 2,109 passed and 1 skipped in 52 files, that is BL's 2,097 and 1 with the 12 tests of `test/unit/examples.test.ts`; Playwright 16.1 minutes, 194 passed and 520 skipped. The figure given for R1 and BL, 518 skipped, comes from a BL run that failed 25 tests, where 6 more did not run; this run's 520 skipped are exactly that run's 524 skipped or not run, less the 4 Pages beta tests that pass here, so the other 2 are the Pages review shots in Chromium and Firefox, which skip without `CFG_SHOTS` and did not run there. No test skips for a model here, and swap stayed at 618 MB. After the review's fixes, nothing staged yet, `DISPLAY=:97 CFG_UNSTAGED=1 scripts/ci.sh --full` passed in 28.2 minutes (1,694.6 s): Vitest 710.9 s, 2,112 passed and 1 skipped in 52 files (the 3 new tests of `examples.test.ts`: the index's negative controls, the name scan and its negative controls); Playwright 16.3 minutes, 197 passed and 520 skipped (the new 404 smoke test in the three projects); swap at 738 MB.
- **As a clone of the public repository sees it:** `DISPLAY=:97 CFG_WITHOUT_LOCAL_EXAMPLES=1 scripts/ci.sh` passed in 10.4 minutes (624 s). Vitest: 1,550 passed and 108 skipped in 52 files; 70 of the skips name a local file and `examples/SOURCES.md` (VIS-35 or the barrel, in 15 files), and the other 38 are the quick suite's own (`CFG_FULL` and `CFG_SOAK` tests, and the 8 full-only tests of `examples.test.ts`); the loops over every example ran on the other six. Playwright: 113 passed and 28 skipped, R1's 17 of the quick suite and 11 for the local models: VIS-35's smoke test in all three projects, and the two project-file and two view smoke tests that drop the barrel, on the build in Chromium and in Firefox (on the development server they skip anyway). After the review's fixes, `DISPLAY=:97 CFG_UNSTAGED=1 CFG_WITHOUT_LOCAL_EXAMPLES=1 scripts/ci.sh` passed in 10.5 minutes (631.2 s) and named the two files at its end: Vitest 174.9 s, 1,559 passed and 102 skipped, of which 64 name a local file, in 14 files, as the six tests that use one only in a line or two now run; Playwright 116 passed and 28 skipped, the same 11 for the local models, the 404 smoke test passing in all three projects. The full Vitest suite as a clone, `CFG_FULL=1 CFG_UNSTAGED=1 CFG_WITHOUT_LOCAL_EXAMPLES=1 npx vitest run`, which had not run before, passed in 652.2 s: 1,876 passed and 61 skipped, 60 of them naming a local file, in 14 files, and the 3 million triangle test, a soak test.

### FL: the funnel's flange

Not one of the plan's milestones: the user's report of 2026-11-12, after printing a mold and pouring (`docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, section 2, R38, D18): "Problem - funnel is not attached. It should have some "flat-contact" section with the box". The funnel stood on its spigot alone, a 30 degree cone about 2.8 mm deep and 13 mm across in a seat of the same cone 0.1 mm off it, under a cup 40 mm wide and 30 mm tall, so it rocked, tipped and lifted off. G12 now gives it a flange that rests on the cover's flat top (G12 above), and G11 and the drag validation keep every vent clear of it.

Checks:
- **The funnel** (`src/core/parts/funnelProfile.ts`, `funnel.ts`). Flange width (`funnel.flangeWidth`, 4 mm past the seat's edge as FL built it, 2 mm since VN (D21), 0 to 10, 0 for none) in Pour & funnel's main list after Height, and Flange thickness (`funnel.flangeThickness`, 1.2 mm, 0.6 to 5) under Advanced, Funnel, each with a hint. In use the flange's underside lies at z = 0, the top's plane, from the spigot out to the seat's radius at the top plus the width, the lowest point of the funnel outside the seat; its edge rises square to the thickness, and a 45 degree chamfer runs in to the neck's outer radius, so the print pose, rim down, keeps every wall within 45 degrees (the audit finds no steep face, and at a limit of 44 degrees finds the chamfer) and the underside is the funnel's last layers, facing up. Seated on a real seat at Zt (a block and each pour of jersey's cut cover, and x8's and the wolf's with `CFG_FULL`), it meets the cover nowhere, its flange touches the top, its spigot below the top keeps `seatClearance` to the seat's cone within 0.02 mm, and across the seat's mouth the gap is wider by 2 layers times tan 30 (0.23 mm at defaults): the relief, as the mouth prints in the cover's first layers. On a 10 degree seat 2 layers would leave 0.137 mm across at the foot of the cover's first layer, so the relief runs up to 0.76 mm, where the gap measured on the seated pair is 0.200 mm. Lowered by a layer, it cuts into both. Under the flange the spigot's wall is no thinner than the thinner of the tip wall and the wall (at a 45 degree seat the bore now widens to the neck 0.07 mm above the top, inside the flange, where it would otherwise have left 0.8 mm against a 0.86 mm tip wall). A new rule, an inline error with the others: `funnel-flange` when the chamfer would end above the neck's top, saying the neck it needs, rounded up, and the widest flange that fits. Where the seat cannot spare the relief and still leave 0.5 mm of spigot cone to centre the funnel, the relief shortens instead (0.3 mm in a 1 mm seat at 0.2 mm layers, and at layers of 2 mm), so the funnel builds wherever it built before FL; an error for it, as first built, blocked the export at the bottom of Seat depth's own range. The rule fuzz of `test/unit/funnel.test.ts` draws the flange's width (a sixth of the sets with none), its thickness and the layer height too, and builds 300 sets the rules pass, with and without a flange; at a flange width of 0 the funnel is the one of before FL, byte for byte, at defaults and at a 20 mm pour in a 45 degree seat (SHA-256 captured at 7a35b00).
- **The vents** (`src/core/analysis/validate.ts`, `placement.ts`). `OpeningSizes` holds the flange's radius, 0 with no funnel, no seat or no flange, and the top's chamfer. Between a pour and a vent the hard rule is now the larger of the land (seat, vent mouth and `minLand`) and the flange's radius, the vent's mouth and 0.5 mm, on every copy of a grid through `nearestCopy`; the 0.5 mm is the spigot's play in the seat, about 0.12 mm, and half a printed line at the flange's edge, about 0.2 mm, with room for the vent's mouth to show beside the flange. Between two pours, and a pour and its copies, the rule stays the seats' land, so x8's 22 mm pitch and the grids keep their pours; funnel collision takes the larger of the rim and the flange, sizes the flange in its advice where the rim alone would not fit, and says when a flange would cover another pour's seat. A pour's flange stays inside the top's chamfer, which moves no default pour: at every example's pours the top's edge is more than a mm further than the flange and the chamfer, as the cavity's own margins keep pours further in. The vent beside a first pour takes its closest circle from the same rule, 12.79 mm out at FL's 4 mm (10.79 at VN's 2); a vent dragged under a flange is refused with "a funnel's flange would rest on it and close it" and snaps out clear of it, and one dragged nearer still, inside the land too, is told both; a typed position that is moved says why, and so does the view's note after a drop. The 0.5 mm gap is fixed: with a 0.4 mm nozzle it stops covering the spigot's play and half a line past a seat clearance of 0.26 mm at 30 degrees (0.21 at 45), and the vent's bore, inside its 0.3 mm mouth chamfer, stays clear until 0.52 (0.42). (VN works the gap out from the settings, never under 0.5.)
- **More than one pour per body.** A flange no wider than the land less the gap, 1 mm at defaults, reaches no further than the land, so it moves no opening: jersey and Benchy at 2 pours and x8 at 3 give 7a35b00's placement to the bit at widths 0 and 1 (SHA-256 of the openings, captured from that commit's own code). Past that, a vent placed beside the first pour before the extra pours takes about 2 x 12.8 mm of a body's top: x8 at 3 pours then had room for 3 barriers' third pour only down a flank or not at all (21 pours), and at 2 pours it sent 3 barriers' second pour into the trench beside them, 22 mm bores at 6.8 mm of ceiling. So the pours go first and the vent beside the first after them, no more than t / 2 lower than it would stand without the body's other pours, or not at all, with a note, as those pours open its top: on x8 at 3 pours 7 of the 8 barriers have no vent beside, where the first build had put 7 in the trenches, 20 mm under the tops with 24 to 25 mm bores that vented nothing; at 2 pours x8's first barrier, and jersey at 3. A spread pour keeps its flange 0.5 mm off the other pours' seats where the body has room as high up, within t / 2 of the spot the land alone gives, and else takes that spot: jersey's second pour stands 18.2 mm from its first (19.7 before FL, 15.2 as first built), Benchy's 18.0 (18.6, 15.7), so neither flange covers the other's seat; the chick's and x8's have no such room as high up and stay about 15 mm apart, where the funnel warning now adds the flange width that lets one funnel at a time keep off the next seat (1 to 1.1 mm there).
- **The vent beside a pour on a peak.** On a peaked top the flange's ring takes that vent down the slope. Where it then stands more than t / 2 lower than with no flange, an info note (`vent-low`, under Flange width) gives the drop and says that the air in between leaves only up the pour, and that a width of 1 mm or less lets the vent stand 9.8 mm out (VN: the drop by how high each vent's disc reaches, and the widest width within t / 2); the pour at the peak still opens it, so no peak is left uncovered on any example. On every example, Benchy 2 x 3, jersey 3 x 3, x8 at 3 pours and the wolf with the base layer, a scan of every copy of every vent against every copy of every pour finds none within the flange's radius, worked out by hand, and 0.5 mm; every body gets its pours, x8 at 3 its 24. Where a small model leaves no room for the vent beside its pour clear of the flange, the warning names the widest flange, in half mm, that would leave room, and only where one would: the barrel, whose cavity is about 25 mm across, at t = 1 (2.5 mm, and at 3 mm placement indeed finds none), a 45 degree seat (3.5) or a 6 mm vent (1), its pour still covering its leaf; at a 20 mm pour, or on a 6 mm cube, where no flange would help, it says no more than before FL.
- **The fit test** (`src/core/parts/fitTest.ts`). Its vent, 0.48 mm clear of a 4 mm flange's edge where M3 put it, moves 0.02 mm out along its line to the 0.5 mm the mold's vents keep (at VN's default of 2 mm it stays where M3 put it, 2.48 mm clear), and for a wider flange as far as its bore still opens into the box tool (to a flange width of about 5.9 mm at the default pour and seat; past that a funnel seated on the 40 mm piece covers its vent, which the water test, filled through the pour opening, does not need). It never moves back in towards the pour: a vent of over 5.6 mm already runs past the box tool's edge where M3 put it, and stays there, under a default flange (as first built it moved 0.35 mm further under it). At a flange width of 0 it stands where it did, and every fit-test STL is M9's. Its README says how to check the funnel's fit in its seat (U3).
- **Projects.** Format 6 holds the two settings; `v5ToV6` opens a file of format 5 or older with a flange width of 0 and the thickness of 1.2, so a saved mold comes back with the funnel and the vents it had, and turning the flange on then moves the vent out (`test/unit/project.test.ts`). Where the file had a funnel, its notice says so, where Flange width is, and that a width of up to 1 mm (its own land less the gap) leaves every vent where it is, so a cover already printed takes the new funnel, while a wider one moves the vent beside each pour out and needs the cover printed again: a 4 mm flange on a v0.1.0 cover would rest on that vent's mouth and leave 0.31 mm of its 2.5 mm bore open.
- **M9's bytes.** The M9 byte check (`test/unit/export.test.ts`, "the export with bolts, in Node") runs with the flange at 0 added to its settings, as it runs with the base layer off, and matches M9's hashes, the fit test's too. An export of every example at defaults, jersey 2 x 1 with no base layer, jersey 3 x 3, Benchy 2 x 3 and x8 at 3 pours, at a flange width of 0, gives the cover, base, funnel, model and fit-test bytes of 7a35b00: 66 STLs, all the same, and so they did again after the review's fixes, with the openings and the silicone to the last digit. At defaults the review's fixes change only x8's cover at 3 pours.
- **Docs.** README.txt says the spigot drops into the seat and the flange rests flat on the cover's top, and to smear a thin film of Vaseline on the underside of each flange first, which the cure-inhibition patch already tests, then seat each funnel and press it down until its flange sits flat all round, keeping the Vaseline out of the seats and vents; `docs/WORKFLOW.md` the same in sections 2, 3 and 7, and section 3 and `README.md` what a project of before FL opens to.

Measured at FL:
- **The funnel:** 2,824.4 mm3 and 768 triangles before, 3,514.8 mm3 and 1,152 with the default flange: 3.50 g of PLA at 1.24 g/cm3 before, 4.36 g with it, 0.86 g more per funnel, which the plastic estimate counts once per pour.
- **The vent beside the pour:** 9.79 mm from the pour's centre before, 12.79 mm with the default flange, on every example, at the angle that maximises the ceiling on the new circle (the wolf's turns from (-8.70, -9.85) to (1.61, 6.24), its pour at (0.50, -6.50)).
- **Silicone at defaults, the export, ml (Node, with the base layer):** benchy 45.1881 to 45.1867, goddess 122.3068 to 122.3214, vis35 548.0961 to 548.1078, jersey 11.6832 unchanged (its vent stays on the flat top), x8 91.7855 to 91.8051, barrel 11.1166 to 11.1491 (its vent leaves the lid for its side, the longest bore 5.8 to 13.0 mm), wolf 223.2889 to 223.3299, chick 14.3360 to 14.3402; jersey 2 x 1 with no layer 22.7182 and jersey 3 x 3 108.6284 unchanged, Benchy 2 x 3 275.0538 to 275.0455, and x8 at 3 pours 97.5670 to 96.8963, with its 24 pours on the barriers' tops and the vent beside the first pour of 7 of its 8 barriers left out, as their other pours take the room on top (97.3846 as first built, which put those 7 vents in the trenches between rows, 24 to 25 mm bores). Every change is the vents' bores (the bores' share at defaults: 0.0000 to +0.0325 ml, and x8 at 3 pours the 7 vents fewer); the goldens of M4 (`OPENING_GOLDEN`), the preview figures of `test/unit/derived.test.ts` and the browser's table in `e2e/export.spec.ts` are measured again, with the old figures beside them, at their old tolerances. The browser's export from the button gives Node's figures to the digit, in Chromium on all eight, barrel in Mode B (11.1491) and jersey 2 x 3 (72.2966, unchanged), and in Firefox on jersey and the wolf, with measured clearance 3.20 to 3.28 mm as before.
- **The cut molds' checks:** at the goldens' settings (no base layer) the wolf's vent beside its pour now stands on an 88 degree cliff of its ceiling, 152.4 to 142.1 mm across a quarter of a mm (at the export cell; the app's export takes the preview's spot, (1.6, 6.2), off the cliff). There the bore-length check's column, 0.02 mm square, averaged the TIN across a crease and read the ceiling at the node 0.139 mm over the field against 0.1 allowed; at 0.002 mm it reads 0.059 (0.054 at 0.001: the raise of 0.05 and float noise), so the column is now 0.002 mm, a narrower measure of the same point, with the 0.1 kept. And the length placement reports, from the field's bilinear surface, differs there from the TIN's by 0.75 mm: a TIN through a cell's four nodes, split along either diagonal, parts from the bilinear surface by up to 0.80 mm at that very point (0.35 on the other diagonal), as the cell's corners are far from a plane. The check now allows 0.1 and the larger of 0.24 and that split's gap at each copy's own point, so 0.34 wherever a cell is near a plane, which is every other opening of the eight examples (the barrel's vent beside is next, at 0.01), and 0.90 at the wolf's vent: a looser bound at that one opening, which the first build had at 0.1 + 0.24 + a quarter of the cell's twist, 2.02 mm, on every opening.
- **Two pours per body, at the goldens' settings:** the second pour stands 19.7 mm from the first on jersey and 18.6 on Benchy before FL, 15.2 and 15.7 as first built, where each flange covered the other's seat, and 18.2 and 18.0 now; the chick's 15.0 throughout. At a flange of 0 or 1 mm every opening is 7a35b00's.
- **The vent beside the pour on a peak, at the goldens' settings:** its ceiling falls with the flange, from 9.79 to 12.79 mm out, by 8.6 mm on the wolf (156.78 to 148.19 mm), 7.1 on the barrel (28.07 to 20.94, off its lid), 3.0 on Goddess (101.44 to 98.42), 3.1 at x8's broken barrier (21.33 to 18.20) and 2.0 on VIS-35 (134.23 to 132.21), each past t / 2 and so with the `vent-low` note; on Benchy it rises (42.92 to 43.21), on the chick it falls 0.9, and on jersey and x8's whole barriers it stays on the flat top.
- **Time:** placement (S7, export cell, no base layer, median of the last 3 of 4, two rounds) before FL and now: benchy 86 and 75 against 84 and 77 ms, goddess 50 and 54 against 50 and 53, vis35 129 and 114 against 115 and 115, jersey 27 and 25 against 40 and 27, x8 417 and 433 against 422 and 422, barrel 15 against 15, wolf 136 and 145 against 140 and 152, chick 110 and 106 against 109 and 108; x8 at 3 pours 423 and 433 against 463 and 458, 6% more, for the vent beside's second search; the funnel builds in 2.0 ms against 1.4. All else within this server's noise.

### VF: the base flicker and the flange outline

Not one of the plan's milestones: two display-only items of 0.2.0 (`docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, R38, R39, D20). The user reported on 2026-11-12, in Firefox on Windows with a real GPU: "When I rotate model, part of the base "surface" flikkers" (section 9, U2). And run FL left the funnel flange's outline in the view, which must not catch a click meant for a vent near it (section 8). Both were designed first by a read-only designer; where a design and the code disagreed, the code was trusted, as said below.

Checks:
- **The cause of U2, from the code.** The plate's top lies at z = 0 (`base.ts`, plate top 0), and the cover's rebate is cut up to exactly z = 0 (`cover.ts`), so the cover's shelf faces down at z = 0 over the plate. The groove runs from -0.5 and the cavity crosses z = 0, as the tool sends nodes outside it to -1, so the two coincide on two bands round the groove: the inner land, from where the cavity meets z = 0 (0.4 mm inside D on a 40 mm block at the preview cell) to e1, and the outer land, from e4 to B, 2 mm each at defaults. Both are exactly coplanar and fight at any distance, the shelf see-through at 35% over the opaque base, so the lands switch between plain base grey and tinted pixel by pixel as the view turns, and only they do, which is "part of" the base. In Mode B the merged base holds the model's copies, which coincide with the model drawn on its own, and the base, whose material is newer, won most ties. The funnel's flange lies on the cover's top (hidden by default). Near-coplanar gaps: a ring 0.05 mm over the top, the plate's sides 0.1 x cos 15 = 0.097 mm inside the rebate, the ridge 0.25 x cos 10 = 0.246 mm off the groove's walls and 0.3 mm under its roof, a spigot 0.1 mm off its seat. The perspective camera's planes were fixed at 0.1 and 5000 mm, and a 24-bit depth buffer (D24S8, as `stencil: true` asks) tells apart at distance d about 2^-24 d² (f - n) / (f n), d² x 6e-7 mm: 0.065 mm at the farthest corner of a 60 mm cube's framed view, 0.47 mm at a 200 mm cube's, so on a large model the rebate, the ridge and the rings fought too, beyond about 400, 650 to 710 and 290 mm. The rest ruled out: the silicone writes no depth and is cut 0.75 mm above the plate; the model's base face in its pocket is hidden; transparent order is fixed by renderOrder.
- **The fix** (view only). `src/view/materials.ts`: `DEPTH_BIAS`, a polygon offset of (1, 2) on the base and the funnel, so a part that rests on another's face draws a hair behind it and that face wins every tie; it pushes only their depth back, so whatever lies in front or level wins, see-through or not, and the base's proxy takes it too. `src/view/depthPlanes.ts` (no three): near and far at 0.9 and 1.1 times the nearest and farthest eye depth of the 8 corners of what is drawn, never nearer than 0.1; with the camera inside the box or beside it, near at 1/100 of its distance to the target; with nothing drawn, 0.1 and 5000. `PreviewScene.fitDepth` (`src/view/scene.ts`) builds that box on every frame and at the start of `worldToScreen`: the mold's box, the model itself (not its group, whose highlights share the whole mesh), its copies, the cover's and the base's roots wherever the exploded view has them (parts, proxies, rings, flange circles), the funnels, the diagnostic pins and the section's handle; an object whose own `visible` is false is skipped, while a part hidden by its material, as the funnel is at defaults, still counts, which only widens the box; the height preview and the caps, whose vertices are lattice indices, are left out, and the silicone lies inside the mold's box. `depthOverride` gives tests fixed planes, 0.1.0's among them. Past 5 m the mold no longer vanishes. The orthographic camera keeps linear planes 10 m deep, 0.0006 mm a step, but `orthoPlanes` now centres them on what it orbits, not on the camera, so a view zoomed out past 5 m and switched to orthographic keeps the mold (before, the perspective view had lost it there too). The offset does not order the model against its copy in the merged base where the view draws a simplified copy of the model, above 300,000 triangles: the two then cross in a still pattern, older than VF.
- **The outline.** `src/app/derived.ts`: `openingRings`, viewSync's loop for the rings moved unchanged (a Node test holds it to the old list), and `flangeOutlines(sizes, O, rings)`, one spot per non-ghost pour ring at the flange's radius, none while `flangeRadius` is 0 (no funnel, no seat or no flange), refused where a non-ghost vent ring stands nearer than `flangeReach` (the flange, the vent's mouth and FL's 0.5 mm gap, the hard rule's own reach and its strict `<`), copy by copy over the copies drawn, or where the flange and the top's chamfer run past O, the flange's share of the top-edge rule. `src/view/dashes.ts` (no three): 24 dashes round a default flange, 0.4 mm across, a 2.8 mm period, 60% dash, dash k centred on 2 pi k / n. `FlangeOutlines` (`src/view/overlays.ts`) draws them in `FLANGE_OUTLINE_COLOUR` #475569, or the invalid red where refused, each on a light band of `FLANGE_CASING_COLOUR` #e2e8f0 0.8 mm across and 0.2 mm longer at each end (`dashCasing`), unlit, 0.03 mm over the top under the rings' 0.05, testing Less and writing no depth, after the cover (the band at order 2.4, the dashes at 2.5, set on the meshes only, as a Group's order would put them after the pins, the highlights and the section's handle, which are meant to stay on top), so a ring wins where they cross, the see-through cover never washes them out and a seated funnel hides them; under the cover's root, so they rise with it exploded and the section cuts them. It has no `hit`, and nothing adds it to the rings' list, so `markerAt` and the drag are as they were. Shown at every camera angle, on every copy of a grid, and not for the landing ghosts. The drag's tip adds a line when the flange is the problem ("The red dashed circle is that funnel's flange: keep the vent's ring wholly outside it, which leaves the vent's mouth 0.5 mm clear of the flange.", or "this pour's flange", "every vent's ring" and "each mouth" for a pour): a ring is drawn 0.3 mm each side of the mouth's edge and the dashes 0.2 mm each side of the flange's, so the two touch exactly where the rule starts to refuse; at FL's 4 mm a vent beside its pour stands 12.79 mm out against a reach of 12.782, 0.008 mm off the dashes, which looks like touching (at VN's default of 2 mm, 10.79 against 10.782). The tip is placed by its height as laid out, as its lines wrap, so near the foot of the view it stays whole. Pour & funnel's lead says what the circle is while there is a flange.
- **Tests.** `test/unit/viewDepth.test.ts`: 0.1.0's planes give 0.065 and 0.47 mm at the framed view of a 60 and a 200 mm cube; `depthPlanes` keeps every corner between the planes with a step under 0.001 mm from 195 views outside the box (zoomed out to 20 times, orbits all round from 30 degrees below to 85 above, Top view) and under 0.01 mm from 6 inside it, at 20, 60 and 200 mm, with what the camera orbits between the planes from every view and, inside the box, the near plane within 5% of the way to it, while the smallest display gap is 0.02 mm (a flange circle under a ring); its negative controls, a near plane at 1.1 times the nearest corner, 0.1.0's planes, and inside the box a near plane at twice or a fifth of the way to the target, each fail those rules; `orthoPlanes` keeps the mold from 50 times out, where planes fixed round the camera lose it past 5 m; only the base and the funnel have an offset. `e2e/view.spec.ts`, "U2: no flicker as the view turns" (a 40 x 40 x 20 mm block): a smoke test, the framed view's step under 0.001 mm (0.031 mm with 0.1.0's planes, the control), every corner of the exploded parts and each funnel's top between the planes in Top view and from below the plate, max explode, funnels shown (a near plane past the nearest corner, the control, loses one), and 8 m out every corner kept in perspective and in the orthographic view switched to there (0.1.0's orthographic planes, the control, lose all 16); the lands keep one colour from 36 views, every 30 degrees at 35, 70 and 89.9 up, 100 mm off: 5 x 5 pixels on the outer land, half way across, on the side facing the camera, and on the inner land, 0.3 mm out from D, on a side the camera looks along, against the same view exploded by 0.25 mm, and Mode B's model, at the spot of its top whose line of sight passes furthest from every ring, flange circle and bore, against the base hidden; every sample stands over 30 px from any ring or flange circle at its own radius; none may be more than 8 off; drawn as 0.1.0 drew them some must be; and with the cover pushed behind the base, or the model behind its copy, by (2, 16), over 90% are (all are). `test/unit/outline.test.ts`: one outline per copy of each pour at the hand-worked radius, none at a vent, a ghost, or with no funnel, seat or flange; refused copy by copy exactly where `checkOpening` refuses for the flange, either way round, on 1 x 1, 2 x 1 and 3 x 3, 400 seeded spots each (a vent clear of its own pour under the next tile's flange among them); outside the circle but within the reach is refused; past the chamfer as the top-edge rule; the dashes' geometry, with a solid band as the gap probe's control; the light band 0.2 mm round each dash with every gap open; a vent's ring touching the dashes exactly where `checkOpening` refuses it for the flange, from 0.5 to 20 mm out; and the draw order through three's own render list: the cover, the band, the dashes, then the pins, the handle and the ghosts. `test/unit/history.test.ts`, on jersey with the real store: the rings equal viewSync's old ones, a vent dragged under a flange refuses its pour's outline, half a mm past the reach it is clear, Esc changes nothing. `e2e/view.spec.ts`, smoke, jersey 2 x 1 in Top view: the outlines' data, the dash's slate at about 25 px per mm, nothing of it 1.2 mm inside or in the gap, its light band at about 75 px per mm; a real drag of the vent to 11.5 mm turns both copies red with the tip's line, 13.5 mm clears them, Esc leaves pins and undo as they were; the same drag 90 px above the view's foot keeps the whole tip inside the view; the vent pinned at 9.9 mm, inside the circle and refused for the flange alone: `markerAt` on its centre and on the circle beside it gives the vent, on the circle opposite and 9 mm out nothing, 7 mm out the pour, a real press on the vent drags it and one on the circle opposite orbits; at Flange width 0.2, whose dashes run over the pour's own ring, the ring's blue where they cross; with Flange width 0 no outline and no slate. The x8 3 x 3 drag test (CFG_FULL) also counts 72 outlines and moves the dragged pour's 9. `e2e/shots.spec.ts` adds the VF and OL review shots. `e2e/view.spec.ts` now runs six smoke tests.
- **Negative controls run once** (each file restored and its SHA-256 checked): no `DEPTH_BIAS` fails the offset unit test and the lands test (33 views flagged); fixed planes fail the planes unit tests and the e2e planes test (0.031 mm); the outlines added to the rings' list fail the smoke test's count, and the pour's ring hit out to the flange fails "the circle opposite"; the reach replaced by the radius fails the outline and history tests; the seat's radius for the flange's fails the first and chamfer tests; a solid band (a dash share of 1) fails the dashes' test. After review: a near plane inside the box at twice the way to the target fails the planes unit tests; `orthoPlanes` fixed round the camera fails its unit test and the smoke test's orthographic check (16 corners lost); the outlines' Group given order 2.5 again fails the draw-order test (the dashes after the pins); the light band not run past the dashes' ends fails its unit test, and no light band at all fails the smoke test's band probe (none of its colour); the tip placed by its first guess alone ends 21.6 px below the view; the outline drawn with no depth test paints its slate over the pour's ring (none of the ring's blue); and Mode B's sample back at 10 mm from the centre stands 8.4 px from a flange circle, against the 30 px the test asks.
- **Bytes.** No file under `src/core` or `src/workers` changes, nor anything the export path reads: `pipeline.ts` reads only `exportBlocked` and `siliconeAdvice` from `derived.ts`, which are unchanged, and `store.ts` only `view/layers.ts`. M9's byte check (`test/unit/export.test.ts`) and the export smoke tests, whose ZIP from the button checks out against Node, pass.

Measured at VF:
- **Depth step at the farthest corner, 0.1 and 5000 against fitted:** 40 mm block framed 0.031 mm against 1.3e-5 (1.7e-5 in the browser, whose aspect is wider); 60 mm cube framed 0.065 against 2.0e-5, zoomed out twice 0.21 against 2.6e-5; 200 mm cube framed 0.47 against 5.4e-5, zoomed out twice 1.50 against 6.9e-5, in Top view at its top 0.10 against 1.4e-5; inside the 200 mm box, 35 mm from the target, 0.029 against 0.0084. Every gap the view draws is then at least 20 steps apart from any view outside the box, and hundreds at the framed view.
- **Fight pixels**, the lands test's 1,200 and Mode B's 600, drawn as 0.1.0 drew them (no offset, 0.1 and 5000): 205 and 410 in Chromium, 183 and 565 in Firefox, so software WebGL here shows U2 too; now 0 and 0 in both.
- **The offset's size**, against a reference with the base pushed back by (8, 512), 12 azimuths at 5, 10, 20, 35, 70, 85 and 89.9 degrees, 3 samples each (6,300 pixels), Chromium and Firefox: 0.1.0 1,237 and 1,205; no offset with fitted planes 2,569 and 1,403; (0, 1) 2,338 and 352; (1, 1) 331 and 329; (1, 2) 331 and 329; (2, 4) 320 and 320; (1, 2) with 0.1.0's planes 484 and 496. The 320 to 331 left at every offset with a slope factor are the same samples at 5 to 20 degrees, where that reference also pushes the base behind the cover's rebate and groove faces: the reference's own error, as the 24 views at 35 and 70 degrees against the exploded one find none. Mode B: 1,597 and 1,771 of 2,100 as 0.1.0 drew it, 60 and 60 with (1, 2), all 5 pixels a view at 85 degrees and also in 0.1.0's run. So (1, 2) stays, as designed. The units alone do not decide: pushed back by (0, 16), the cover's shelf still won every sampled pixel over the base's (1, 2), whose slope term outweighs 16 units at these views, and (1, 16) lost on 63% of them and (1, 64) on 86%, as two coplanar triangles' slope terms differ by more than that in a software rasterizer; (2, 16) lost on all, so the tests' negative controls use (2, 16), where the design had (0, 16).
- **Frame cost:** fitting the planes on x8 3 x 3 with the funnels shown (9 models, 72 funnels, 153 rings, 72 flange circles, 270 objects) takes 0.10 to 0.13 ms in Chromium and 0.17 to 0.2 ms in Firefox, of which 0.04 ms is the scene's matrix update.
- **Nothing clipped:** with the section on and the camera inside jersey's box, 30 mm from the cut, near is 0.304 against 0.1, the caps the same and 0 of 20 sampled cap pixels differ, in both browsers; every section and cap test passes unchanged.
- **The outline's draws:** with a mesh per circle, x8 3 x 3 in Top view took 382 draw calls against 238 without (a see-through double-sided material draws twice) and render() 3.4 against 0.7 ms in Chromium; instanced, one mesh per colour and radius and one pass, 239 against 238, and 0.8 against 0.8 ms (Firefox 1 against 1 ms).
- **Drag (T0)** on x8 3 x 3, median of 21 moves on main, with the outline's scene work and with it stubbed out, in one page: 1.5 to 1.8 against 1.5 ms in Chromium, 2 against 2 ms in Firefox (6 ms on the first, cold round). The CFG_FULL drag test, on a fresh page, logs 2.10 ms on a valid spot and 4.60 on one that snaps in Chromium, 5.00 and 5.00 in Firefox, under its 16 ms (M8's review: 1.10 to 2.60 and 2.00 to 4.00, before FL).
- **Contrast** of a dash's centre against the top 1 mm outside it (WCAG ratio, median and worst), Chromium, Firefox within 0.03, with the plain dashes first built: in Top view, Benchy 2.96 and 2.34 (10 px per mm), jersey 2 x 1 2.28 and 1.80 (6.5), x8 at 3 pours 2.19 and 1.06 dark, 2.56 and 1.37 light (5.5), the wolf 1.75 and 1.00 dark, 2.59 and 1.00 light (4.3), x8 3 x 3 1.49 and 1.00 (2.1). The review found the framed view in the dark theme nearly blank: there the backdrop is the dark scene through the 35% cover, about rgb(95, 102, 108), not the pale top, and the slate stood at 1.08 to 1.18. So each dash now lies on a light band (#e2e8f0, 6.2:1 against the slate). Measured again as the line against the backdrop, the pixel across the dash, from 0.6 mm inside to 0.6 mm outside, that stands out most, median and worst, Chromium, before the band and with it: framed, dark theme, Benchy 1.18 and 1.08 against 2.56 and 1.40 (5.1 px per mm), jersey 2 x 1 1.18 and 1.03 against 2.41 and 1.13 (4.1), the wolf 1.12 and 1.05 against 2.40 and 1.17 (3.0); framed, light theme, Benchy 4.06 and 1.87 against 4.06 and 1.43, jersey 2.64 and 1.34 against 2.33 and 1.06, where at 4 px per mm the band greys the thin slate a little; Top view, either theme, Benchy 2.81 and 2.34 against 2.81 and 2.52, jersey 2.29 and 1.88 against 2.81 and 2.18, the wolf (dark) 2.00 and 1.23 against 2.80 and 1.96, x8 at 3 pours (dark) 2.78 and 2.22 with it; Firefox, on Benchy dark and jersey light, within 0.01. The design's bar, a median of 3 and a worst of 2, is still not met: the slate stays #475569 as decided, and a darker one (#334155) would lift Benchy's Top view median to about 3.9. Left to the user's look (U2).
- **Clutter:** on x8 at 3 pours, 15 mm apart, the circles cross the neighbouring pours' rings, and every ring is drawn whole over them.
- **Test time:** the outline's smoke test 7.6 s in Chromium and 4.4 s in Firefox (9.6 and 5.8 s with the review's checks); the U2 tests 1.8 and 49 s, and 1.3 and 19 s (the planes test, now smoke, 2.0 and 1.5 s; the lands test at three elevations 72 and 27 s).
