U2 asks the user to orbit slowly at defaults in Mode A at the framed distance and zoomed out 3 times, watching the lands round the cavity, the base's sides through the cover, the model in Mode B and, on a large model in Top view, the rings, and to say whether the flanges' circles read well.
- **After review,** not taken up here: the grab cursor never shows over a ring, as OrbitControls sets an inline `cursor` on the canvas that beats `.viewport.grab canvas` (older than VF, since M8; fixed in VN); and a pour dragged past the top's edge turns its circle red with no line in the tip saying the circle is the flange, while the tip's reason names the edge.

### VN: a 2 mm flange and the vent's note

Not one of the plan's milestones: the item of 0.2.0 left over from run FL (`docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, section 8, R38, D21), designed first by a read-only designer, who weighed six ways to keep the vent beside a pour high on a peaked top (D21), and the user's answer of 2026-11-16 to the question that design left: "2 mm" for the default Flange width, over keeping 4 (recommended) and 1 mm (section 2). And two small fixes VF's review found.

Checks:
- **The default** (`src/core/params/schema.ts`). Flange width defaults to 2 mm past the seat's edge, where FL set 4: the funnel stands 17.5 mm across on 97 mm2 of the cover's top outside the seat, where FL's stood 21.5 mm across on 219, and the vent beside each first pour stands 10.79 mm from it, between before FL's 9.79 and FL's 12.79. A project file holds its own width, so one saved at 4 mm opens at 4 and the format stays 6; format 5 and older still open at 0 (`v5ToV6` sets 0 as before), and their notice stays true: a width of up to 1 mm (the land less the gap) leaves every vent where it is, and a wider one, the default 2 among them, moves the vent beside each pour out.
- **The note** (`src/core/analysis/placement.ts`, `validate.ts`), as designed: no opening moves for it. `discMaximum` reads the highest node within a vent's radius, no further, the nodes `leavesCovered` reads, or the surface at the centre where no node is inside. `ventLowNote` measures each vent by it, the vent with the widest flange the land keeps clear against the one placed, as air reaches a vent anywhere under its disc, and shows when that drop is over t / 2, as before. `widestFlangeWithinDrop` then places the vent beside the pour again at each width in `FLANGE_TRY_STEP` (0.5 mm) above that widest flange and below the one set, upward, while its reach stays within t / 2, so every half mm up to the width it returns passes; where none does, the note keeps FL's sentence. The note then says: "A Flange width of at most W mm under Pour & funnel keeps the vent within 1.5 mm of that height, and one of at most 1 mm lets it stand 9.8 mm out" (or "only with no flange (Flange width 0 under Pour & funnel) does it stand X mm out" where the land keeps none clear); its "X mm lower" is the reach's drop, and its first "(x, y)" is still the vent's, for the Show button. One difference from the design: the widths tried are whole half mm, as the vent-refused warning's are (1.5, 2, 2.5 at defaults, 1.0, 1.5 at a widest-in-land of 0.9), where the design wrote "plain + 0.5", which is the same at defaults. With more than one body the width assumes the other bodies' openings stand where they do at the width set, as the note's "at most" says; with more than one pour per body, see the review's fixes below.
- **The gap** (`src/core/analysis/validate.ts`). `flangeGap(seatClearance, halfAngleDeg, nozzle)` is the larger of `FLANGE_GAP`, 0.5 mm, and the funnel's play in its seat, the clearance over the cosine of the half angle, with half a printed line, the nozzle over 2; at defaults 0.115 + 0.2 = 0.315, so `Math.max` gives the literal 0.5, to the bit, at every half angle from 10 to 45 degrees, at a 0.6 mm nozzle and at clearances up to 0.25 mm. It grows past a clearance of 0.26 mm in a 30 degree seat (0.21 at 45) or a nozzle of 0.77 mm. `OpeningSizes.flangeGap` carries it from `openingSizes`, and `flangeReach`, `flangeSpacing`, `widestFlangeInLand(sizes)` (called twice in placement), the one-funnel-at-a-time advice of `funnelCollision` and so the fit test's vent (`fitVent`, through `flangeReach`) and the view's red outline (`flangeOutlines`, through it too) all read it, each in its old order of sums. `v5ToV6` works it out from the file's own seat clearance, half angle and nozzle, each at its default where the file has none. S7 declares `print.nozzle` (`src/app/stages.ts`), which it already followed through S5 and S10, so no key changes. Not in the design, as VF came after it: the drag's tip (`flangeTip`, `src/app/derived.ts`) says the gap these settings need, and past 0.5 mm how far off the dashes to keep the ring, as only at 0.5 do the ring and the dashes touch where the rule starts to refuse.
- **The grab cursor** (VF's review, older than VF: since M8). OrbitControls' constructor connects through `disconnect()`, which sets the canvas's inline cursor to `auto`, and an inline style beats the stylesheet's `.viewport.grab canvas`, so the cursor stayed the arrow over a ring, and while picking a face too, whose crosshair the same rule sets. `PreviewScene` now clears that inline cursor once the controls are made (`src/view/scene.ts`), and the stylesheet decides.
- **The Pages test's flake in Firefox** (VF's review). `e2e/pages.spec.ts`, "/beta/ is the beta", failed here 1 run in 8 (`--repeat-each 8` in Firefox), as VF's review found it about 1 in 3, on "failed: .../mold-form-generator/assets/rolldown-runtime-....js (NS_BINDING_ABORTED)" in `checkDiag`'s own watch: a load of the root page that Stable version opened, cut off by `open()`'s step to the blank page and reported after that step had returned. Leaving out aborted loads the watch had not seen start was tried first, and still failed 2 runs in 8, as Firefox reported that load as started after the step too. So `open()` now waits, after the page's load event, until the page before has nothing loading and has started or ended no load for half a second, by a record of the page's loads kept from its first `open()`. It leaves no error out, so every failed load, console error and response over 400 still counts. With it the test passed 16 runs of 16 (`--repeat-each 8`, twice) in Firefox, and the whole spec passes in both browsers.
- **Tests.** `test/unit/validate.test.ts`: the gap is 0.5 mm, `toBe`, at defaults, at every half angle from 10 to 45 degrees, at a 0.6 mm nozzle and at 0.15 and 0.25 mm of clearance, and the worst case, to 12 places, at a clearance of 0.3 mm (0.54641), 0.5 in a 45 degree seat (0.90711) and a 1 mm nozzle (0.61547); at 0.5 in a 45 degree seat a vent at the old reach is refused for the flange and one at the new reach passes, and by hand the funnel pushed to that side covers 0.407 mm of the mouth of a vent placed by the fixed gap and none of one placed by the derived one; the widest flange the land keeps clear is 1, 0.9 and 0.5 mm there, where the fixed gap says 1 for all three; `discMaximum` on a ramp, between nodes, inside a cell, on a cliff and off the cavity. FL's tests of the flange's rules (`validate.test.ts`, `outline.test.ts`) set FL's 4 mm, as they are about a flange whose term decides the top's edge at a 3 mm wall, which 2 mm does not, and x8's 22 mm pitch now asks for a 21 mm top only. `test/unit/placement.test.ts`: on a ball of 11 mm (a dome about 14.5 mm round) the note at 4 mm names 2.5 mm, its own scan finds 1.27 mm of drop at 2.5 and 1.81 at 3, each at least 0.1 mm off t / 2, and planned at 2.5 there is no note and at 3 there is; on a ball of 22 mm, the negative control, 4 mm gives none. `test/regression/openings.test.ts`: OPENING_GOLDEN and the matrix's codes measured again (below); the wolf's note at 4 mm, its drop by the test's own scan of the nodes under each disc (3.63 mm, where its centre stands 8.59 lower), its whole message, and no note planned at the 2 mm it names, one at 2.5; at the default 2 mm no example has the note, with the base layer and at the preview cell too, each body's reach within t / 2 by the scan; at 4 mm VIS-35 no longer has it, while Goddess keeps it and names 2.5 (since the review the chick at 3 pours keeps it too, and VIS-35's check no longer skips Goddess's); at a seat clearance of 0.3 mm jersey, the wolf and x8 at 3 pours (24 pours) have no vent's mouth within reach of a funnel pushed to its side, `expectNoVentUnderAFlange` now working the gap out by hand, and jersey's vent stands within 0.02 mm of the new reach and its 0.01 of slack, where the default placement fails that check; with 4 mm set, every opening of the eight examples, Benchy 2 x 3, jersey 3 x 3, x8 at 3 pours, jersey and Benchy at 2 pours and the wolf with the base layer stands where 71aff82 put it by default, by SHA-256 of the openings captured from that commit's own code, and a clearance of 0.3 mm or the default 2 mm moves jersey's; FL's 7a35b00 hashes at 0 and 1 mm stand; the barrel at t = 1 is FL's 4 mm case, and at 2 mm it now has its vent, with the note naming 1.5 mm. `test/unit/parts.test.ts`: the fit test's vent stands where M3 put it at 2 mm, moves 0.023 mm out at 4, and 0.046 mm further at a clearance of 0.3. `test/unit/project.test.ts`: a format 5 file at a clearance of 0.3 says "up to 0.9 mm", at 0.5 in a 45 degree seat 0.5, with a 1 mm nozzle 0.8, at 0.15 and with none 1; the default is 2; a format 5 jersey turned on at the default stands 10.79 mm out. `test/unit/stages.test.ts`: a clearance of 0.3 changes S7, S13 and S16 and a 1 mm nozzle S7 and S13; S7 declares the nozzle. `test/unit/outline.test.ts`: the tip's two lines at 0.5 mm, and at 0.907 mm "0.41 mm or more outside it", where a vent is refused until its ring stands that far off the dashes, and so is the outline. `test/unit/history.test.ts` and `test/unit/funnel.test.ts` follow the default; `e2e/view.spec.ts` "the flange outline" sets 4 mm, so its 11.5 and 9.9 mm vents stay under the flange's reach and circle, and "the cursor: grab over an opening's ring, the default off every ring" is new; `e2e/shots.spec.ts` holds its vent at 10.2 mm. `test/unit/requirements.test.ts` records D21 and the user's answer.
- **Negative controls run once** (each file restored and its SHA-256 checked): a fixed 0.5 mm gap fails 12 tests (the three VN tests of the gap in `validate.test.ts`, the fit test's, the format 5 notice's, the tip's, two "sees real changes" rows, the three runs at 0.3 mm and the 0.3 mm hash control); the note measured at the vents' centres fails the dome test, the wolf's, the barrel at t = 1 and VIS-35's and the chick's; the note naming no width fails the dome test, the wolf's, the barrel's and Goddess's, and a width search that never stops at t / 2 the dome test; `discMaximum` read one cell diagonal further fails its test; the default back at 4 mm fails the schema's rows, the format 5 tests, the fit test's, the six changed goldens and the no-note test; S7 without the nozzle fails its declaration test; the format 5 notice by the default gap fails its test; the tip always at 0.5 fails its test; and with the inline cursor left on the canvas the cursor test fails, on that inline style, and with that check taken out, on "grab" (it reads "auto").
- **Bytes.** At a flange width of 0 the export of each of the eight, Benchy 2 x 3, jersey 3 x 3 and x8 at 3 pours writes the same STL bytes (cover, base, funnel, model and the fit test, 20 files each) as 71aff82, and with 4 mm set the same as 71aff82 at its default: only the notes' words differ. M9's byte check (`test/unit/export.test.ts`) passes, and at 2 mm the fit test's covers are M9's again, as its vent stays where M3 put it.
- **Docs.** README.md and `docs/WORKFLOW.md` say 2 mm, and that up to 1 mm still fits a v0.1.0 cover at the default Seat clearance, the notice giving the width for the project's own settings; WORKFLOW's fit-test step says a Seat clearance past about 0.26 mm keeps the vents a little further from the flange, so the cover is printed again. README.txt stated no width or gap; since the review it states the flange's width (below). FL's and VF's text above keep their history, with VN's figures beside the defaults they stated.
- **After the review** (three reviewers, of correctness, of the tests and of a maker's use; each fix has a test):
  - *The note with more than one pour per body.* Where the pours go first, the width search kept the body's other pours where the width set put them, and left out the floor `placeVentsBeside` holds the vent to, while a plan at a narrower width spaces those pours again: on x8 at 3 pours, t = 1 and a 0.8 mm nozzle, at 4 mm, it named 1 mm for the broken barrier's vent, where a plan at 1 mm has no vent beside that pour at all. So there the note names only the widest flange the land keeps clear, which brings back the order of before FL, and weighs the vent against where that plan puts it, before the body's other pours (the vent placed again among the first pours, the pins and the vents beside earlier bodies' pours). With one pour per body nothing changes.
  - *The note's words.* Its figure is the highest ceiling under each vent, so it says so: "where the highest ceiling under it is X mm lower than under a vent 9.8 mm out, so the air above that height leaves only up the pour: pour slowly. A Flange width of at most W mm under Pour & funnel keeps that drop within 1.5 mm, and one of at most 1 mm lets the vent stand 9.8 mm out."
  - *The funnel warning's advice.* Worked out from where the pours stood, it did not hold once followed, as a narrower flange moves the pours placement spreads: the chick's 2 pours, 16.1 mm apart at 2 mm, stand 15.0 apart at the 0.8 mm that distance asked for, and collide again; Benchy's 16 mm and 1.6 and jersey's 15 and 0.8 did the same, as at FL. `followedAdvice` (`placement.ts`) now makes the plan again at the width the advice names, wherever a pour could move (one not pinned and not a first pour at its body's own top, or a body short of pours), and works the advice out from where the pours then stand, until the flange fits there, with the widest top that fits there; every width up to `widestFlangeAsNone` (the land's 1 mm at defaults, within the top's edge) gives the plan of no flange, made once, and the widest of them that fits is named; after 4 plans at wider widths it takes that one. The one-funnel-at-a-time width likewise. Where the pours then stand elsewhere, the advice says how far apart ("with the pours then 15.0 mm apart"). `funnelCollision` is split into `closestPours`, `funnelsCollide`, `funnelFit` and `flangeOffSeat` for it, and takes the worked-out advice. The advice keeps 1 mm between flanges, as between rims (`FUNNEL_GAP`), where the maker's review asked for none: two funnels, each with its play in its seat and its flange's edge printed half a line wide, close 0.63 mm of it at defaults. The warning itself still counts flanges that only touch as clear, as before.
  - *The placing order and the seat clearance.* The pours went first wherever the flange reached past the land by the derived gap, so at a 1 mm flange a seat clearance of 0.3 put jersey's second pour 15.2 mm from its first, where 0.25 put it 19.7. `flangeOrdersPours` (`validate.ts`) decides the order, and whether a spread pour keeps off the other seats, on the fixed 0.5 mm gap, which at defaults is the derived gap to the bit, so a clearance moves only the vents, by what the gap adds (0.046 mm at 0.3).
  - *The format 5 notice* takes the seat clearance, the half angle, the nozzle and the land as the project opens with them, brought into their ranges or at the schema's defaults (`opensWith`, `src/app/projectFile.ts`), so no default is written out twice, and a hand-edited half angle of 80 degrees, which opens at 45, says 0.8 mm with a clearance of 0.3, where it said that any flange moves the vents.
  - *README.txt* gives the funnel's flange width and says to use funnels printed for the mold, as one with a wider flange, such as a funnel printed at the beta's 4 mm, rests on the vent beside each pour (README.md and WORKFLOW say it too), and its Vaseline step says to keep the film a little short of the flange's outer edge and wipe away any that squeezes out by a vent. `fit_test/README.txt` says, for the seat and nozzle set, past what Seat clearance the vents keep further off the flange, 0.26 mm at defaults and 0.21 in a 45 degree seat, and up to what a cover printed before still takes the funnel with each vent's bore clear, 0.52 and 0.42; WORKFLOW's fit-test step says the same at the defaults, and that the pours stay. README.md's outline sentence says the gap is 0.5 mm at the default clearance and nozzle only. Comments: the pours-first one states 10.8 mm at 2 mm beside FL's 12.8, and `funnel.test.ts`'s ring 111 and 233 mm2 by one measure.
  - *Accepted, not changed:* at a 4 mm flange with a 0.5 mm clearance in a 45 degree seat and a 0.8 mm nozzle, the fit test's vent stops at the box tool's edge 0.021 mm inside the worst case (its mouth 1.086 mm off the flange against a gap of 1.107), as with the fixed gap past about 4.6 mm; its bore stays clear of the flange, and the mold's own vents keep the whole gap.

### CS: Reset, the marks on changed settings, and the defaults file

Not one of the plan's milestones: two items of 0.2.0 the user asked for on 2026-11-09 (`docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, section 8: a global Reset button, and marks on changed settings after PrusaSlicer), designed first by a read-only designer, with the user's answers of 2026-11-16 to the design's questions: "Settings only (Recommended)" and "Accent blue (Recommended)" (section 2); and the user's note of the same day, "I wonder if it would be good to put all default values in some json file, so I could fine tune it later", which the builder answered with a JSON file of values only, read when the app is built (R40, D22). Where the design's references and the code disagreed, the code was trusted.

Checks:
- **At its default** (`src/core/params/defaults.ts`, `src/app/stages.ts`). `isDefault(def, value)`: an auto setting while it is null, every other one while it is the schema's default, by `===`. `PARAM_PATHS` lists the 75 settings in the schema's order, `paramDef(path)` gives a setting's definition (`ui/layout`'s `defOf` is now it), and `changedPaths(params, paths)` the paths off their default, from a table split once, as the sidebar asks on every draw.
- **The store** (`src/app/store.ts`). `resetParam(path)` sets one setting back, null for an auto one, labelled "resetting <Label>" in the control's own history group, so within 400 ms of a wheel step on the same control it joins that step, and a step that ends where it began goes, as a slider's double-click did; at its default it does nothing at all, so the pipeline is not woken. `resetParams(paths, label)` sets the changed ones back in one `setParams`, one step that never joins another, nothing during a drag, as undo; `resetAll()` is every setting. Plane, pins and removed vents are not touched (the user's answer). Every double-click on a row's name or slider now calls `resetParam`.
- **The marks** (`src/ui/ResetMark.tsx`, `SliderRow.tsx`, `ParamRow.tsx`, `style.css`). One per row, in all four kinds: a `span.mark.dot`, a 6 px circle in the track's grey, not focusable, titled "At its default, 3 mm." or "At its default: automatic."; once changed, the row gets `data-changed`, its name (not its unit or tag) takes `--changed`, which is `var(--accent)` on `:root` so it follows the theme, and the dot becomes `button.mark.reset` with the new `ResetArrow` icon, named "Reset <Label>" without the default, so `getByRole('button', { name: 'M3' })` still finds the option alone, and titled "Changed from its default, 3 mm. Click to reset it." or, for an auto one, "Set by you; its default is automatic. Click to make it automatic again." The grid is `112px minmax(0, 1fr) 16px 56px`: label, slider, mark, box in DOM order, so Tab goes slider, arrow, box; a checkbox's mark stands in the sliders' mark column; Mode, Bolt size and Bolt length span columns 2 to 4 with 24 px kept free, and their mark sits in that room at the row's right end (in column 3, "Merged (B)" goes onto two lines: the negative control below). The arrow moves focus to the row's control before it goes: the slider, or the box where the slider will be off (an auto row with no model), the checkbox, the default's option, or the list, with `preventScroll`. A draft or an error in the box goes with a reset. The two hints that said "Double-click to follow again" now name the arrow too.
- **Reset in the header** (`src/ui/Header.tsx`): left of Undo, `icon-btn labelled` with the arrow and "Reset", named "Reset every setting", off with "Every setting is at its default." and otherwise titled with how many are changed and the first four of them, and what it keeps; no shortcut. **A section's Reset** (`src/ui/Sidebar/Section.tsx`): on Base, Silicone, Cover & joint, Pour & funnel and Grid, at the top right on the heading's line, absolutely placed so nothing moves, shown while one of the section's settings is changed, the bolts' while hidden among them, labelled "resetting the <Title> settings"; Model, with one setting, has none. **Advanced** (`ParamSections.tsx`): "N changed" in its summary as text, as a button there would be a control in a control, and inside a first line "N settings changed in here." with a small Reset.
- **The defaults file.** `src/core/params/defaults.json` holds the 75 values, grouped and ordered as `PARAM_SCHEMA`, null for the 9 automatic ones; `schema.ts` passes each to its row's `num`, `auto`, `flag` or `choice` and keeps everything else, the comments that give a value's reason among it, where one is recorded; `PARAM_SCHEMA`'s order stays that of the params and of project.json. It is imported `with { type: 'json' }`, which Node needs where Playwright's tests load the schema, and the static test's import walk takes a JSON import as data. `src/app/defaultsCheck.ts` is the check: `checkDefaults(json)` gives one sentence per problem, naming the setting by its label and path, for a setting missing (with what it takes), unknown or misspelt (with the nearest name, in its group first), a group missing or not an object, a wrong type, a number outside the range its control takes (the schema's, with `ENTRY_FLOORS` where it has none, so an analysis cell of 0 is refused) or not whole where it must be, a choice that is none of its options, and an automatic setting that is not null; once every value passes, `crossParameterErrors` on the defaults, each named by the row its note shows under (`DIAGNOSTIC_PARAM`). `checkDefaultsText(text)` adds text that is no JSON and a setting given twice, which JSON.parse would take the last of without a word.
- **Where the check runs, and why there.** A Vite plugin, `checkDefaultsFile` in `vite.config.ts`, in its `config` hook, so as Vite reads its config, before it builds a file or the dev server listens; a preview, which serves a build already made, skips it. That one hook covers `scripts/serve.sh` (its `npx vite build` exits 1, and `set -e` stops it before `vite preview`), the GitHub Pages build (`scripts/build-pages.sh`, which builds each ref with that ref's own config, `--configLoader runner` included), the test servers' builds and `npm run dev` as it starts (an edit made while it runs is checked when it starts again, as README.md says), with neither script changed: a check in `serve.sh` alone would not reach the Pages build, and the requirements test holds `serve.sh` to its two commands. `vite.config.ts` still imports none of the app's code, so Vitest and Playwright load it as before: the hook loads the check through Vite's own module runner (`runnerImport`), about 0.25 s, and says a file that is no JSON itself, as the check's module could not load then. Its output names the file, each problem on a line, and README.md's "Tuning the defaults". `scripts/ci.sh` runs the same check in `test/unit/defaultsFile.test.ts`.
- **Literal defaults in `src/`.** Routed through the schema: README.txt's "The 3 mm silicone thickness, which is the default," (`src/core/report/readme.ts`), which tested `t === 3`, now `t === PARAM_SCHEMA.silicone.t.default`, the same bytes. Left, on purpose: the migrations of `src/app/projectFile.ts` (`v2ToV3`'s bolts, `v4ToV5`'s slope of 2, `v5ToV6`'s flange thickness of 1.2), which write out the values each version started with "so a later change of the defaults never changes what an old file opens to"; the silicone advice's 3 mm in `src/app/derived.ts` ("under the 3 mm this mold is designed around"), the plan's threshold for its warning, which a tuned default should not move; the two bolt hints in `src/ui/layout.ts` ("0.2 suits a 0.4 mm nozzle", "0.15 lets a nut slide in by hand on a 0.4 mm nozzle"), facts measured at M10 for that nozzle; `GROOVE_OVER_TAPER` (0.15) in `fitTest.ts`, a rule between two clearances, whose other uses read `defaultParams()` already; and the comments that say "at defaults". No `?? <default>` fallback was found. A slider's step follows its default's size where `layout.ts` gives none (`stepOf`), as before, so a default tuned past 1 or 20 changes that row's step.
- **Departures from the design.** The ids are R40 and D22, as R39, D20 and D21 were taken. The widths measured are 163 to 139 px and 227 to 203, where the design estimated 153 to 129 and 217 to 193: the rows are 347 px wide, as the sidebar's scrollbar is overlaid. Advanced's own Reset reads "Reset", with the arrow, so its words are within its name, "Reset the Advanced settings". M6's checks are left as M6 checked them. The planeTol row shows under Model with a model only, so with no model 74 rows have marks.
- **Tests.** `test/unit/params.test.ts` "a setting is at its default while it holds the schema's default, and an auto one only while it is null, whatever its rule gives", with its own negative control, the pocket judged by the value its rule resolves to. `test/unit/history.test.ts` "a row's reset is one step back to its default, ...", with setting the same value again as the control that the store's tick does move, and "Reset sets every setting back as one step, keeps the base face, moved openings and removed vents, and builds the defaults' mold". `test/unit/defaultsFile.test.ts`: the file against the schema, key by key and in order, and `JSON.stringify(defaultParams())` equal to it; the schema read again with the file mocked, so a value changed there changes that default and no label, range or order; each kind of mistake once with its message (17 cases, 19 since the review, and the file that is no object, no JSON or gives a setting twice); and the plugin's hook on a written copy of the file, which throws the problems in the build's words, or goes on with the real file, or with a preview. `e2e/view.spec.ts` "@smoke marks on changed settings: ..." on the build in Chromium and Firefox, (a) to (h) as designed. `e2e/app.spec.ts`'s layout smoke test adds a pass with every setting changed, the bolts on and Advanced open, empty and with jersey, at both sizes, then Reset and a pass with every dot; it waits two frames before those checks, as Diagnostics counts its rows below on the resize event. `e2e/shots.spec.ts`: `jersey-marks`, `jersey-marks-focus` and `jersey-marks-advanced`. `test/unit/static.test.ts` reads a JSON import as data, `test/unit/release.test.ts` expects the plugin, and the requirement map R01 to R40 and D1 to D22.
- **Negative controls** (each in a copy of the tree under the scratch folder, restored and its SHA-256 checked against the working tree's): `PARAM_PATHS` without one path fails the marks' unit test (74 against 75); an auto setting at its default whatever its value fails it (`base.pocketDepth at 0.05`); `resetParam` with group null fails the row reset test (6 steps against 4), with `setParam`'s label its label, and without the at-default guard its tick (22 against 20); `resetParams` as a loop of `setParam` fails the Reset test (20 steps against 11), skipping auto settings its params, also clearing pins and removed vents its kept plane, pins and vents, and going on during a drag its t (3 against 4); the schema writing thickness 4 itself fails "is where the schema takes every default from"; the check with no range fails 4 tests, letting an automatic setting hold a number 1, skipping the rules 2, missing a repeated key 1; the hook checking nothing fails the plugin's test, and leaving the plugin out the release test; in the browser (Chromium), the mark after the box fails (b)'s order (307 against 267), the dot as a disabled button (a)'s count, no colour rule (b)'s colour, the default in the arrow's name its name, focus not moved (c)'s focus, a section's Reset in the flow (f)'s heading, Mode's mark in the third column (e)'s one line (12 px over, both options), and Reset as a loop of single resets (g)'s depth (16 against 13); the layout test fails on an arrow drawn wider than its cell and on a section's Reset too narrow for its word. A 60 px mark column, the design's control, did not fail it: the slider column took the room.
- **Bytes.** Nothing that exports changes: `JSON.stringify(defaultParams())`, `JSON.stringify(PARAM_SCHEMA)`, `GROUP_TITLES`, `SCHEMA_VERSION` (6) and the schema's exports are as at 3310268, compared by loading both trees; the ZIP of jersey, Benchy and the barrel at defaults, and of jersey with t 4, bolts on with M4, Mode B, a 2 x 1 grid, a pocket of 0.4, a 4 mm flange and 0.2 infill, gives every file the same SHA-256 as 3310268 (each STL, the fit test's, README.txt, project.json, and report.json past its timings), with the build's stamp held fixed; and the export tests (`test/unit/export.test.ts`, M9's byte check and FL's among them) pass.
- **Docs.** README.md: Reset, Undo and Redo in the header, the marks, and "Tuning the defaults". `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`: the user's three answers and the note, verbatim (section 2), R40, D22, section 8's rows built, `model_oriented.stl` kept as the user's file for the simplify and the repair to come, and U2. This section, the parameters' note and the GUI's grid and State above.
- **After the review** (three reviewers: behaviour, tests, and look and wording):
  - A group the file lacks or holds as no object, such as `silcone` for `silicone`, a missing `reports`, `"grid": null`, or a file that is a list or null, stopped the build on a TypeError in `schema.ts`, which the check's module loads, before the check could run. The schema now reads such a group as empty (a Proxy over the file), so each gives the check's own sentences, through `vite build`, `scripts/serve.sh` and `--configLoader runner` in a scratch copy of the tree, as before with nothing built and nothing served.
  - A layer height the automatic pocket cannot follow (`pocket-range`) said "set the pocket depth yourself", which the file refuses: it now names Layer height and the range it must keep. Infill, a fraction, is held to at most 1 in the file, with why: 5 for 5 percent passed and gave "500 percent infill" in Diagnostics. A byte-order mark at the file's start ("UTF-8 with BOM"), which Vite and Node pass over, is passed over by the check and the plugin too, where it was refused with the invisible character in the message.
  - After a section's Reset, focus went to the option pressed before it (Merged (B), then unpressed): `rowControl` now picks the default's option, which `ChoiceRow` marks `data-default`, so Mode's arrow and Base's Reset both leave focus on Glue in (A). A row's reset now waits while an opening is dragged, as the section's and the header's do.
  - README.md's "Tuning the defaults": stop the running server before `scripts/serve.sh` (it rebuilds `dist/` and then wants port 5173), where the problems show (its terminal, or `serve.log` under `nohup`), how a sidebar name maps to the file's group and key, that values are in the sidebar's units and Infill is a fraction, that the dev server checks the file as it starts, that README.md and this plan's table quote some defaults, and that a value's reason is in a comment or here only where one is recorded (schema.ts has three such comments), as this plan's note, D22 and schema.ts now say too. "What a new model starts with" became what the app starts with when the page is opened, as settings carry over to the next file; Model, with one setting, has no Reset; and the header's line reads more simply.
  - Tests: `test/unit/defaultsFile.test.ts` changes every one of the 75 values in the mocked file and holds each default to it (it changed 3, so a row that wrote its own default equal to the file's passed), loads the check with a group missing, misspelt, null, a list or null as the build does, has rows for the layer and the infill, the byte-order mark, and the plugin with Vite's runner mocked to fail, which shows text that is no JSON is said without it; `test/unit/history.test.ts` has a section's and the header's Reset straight after a change of the same control and after each other, each its own step, and a row's reset during a drag; `e2e/view.spec.ts` (c) a double-click on the name as one step labelled "resetting Silicone thickness" and none at the default, and the arrow clearing a refused entry and its error, (f) Mode in Base's Reset with focus on Glue in (A), Advanced's Reset as one step with focus on its first row, and Cover & joint's Reset for Bolt size while the bolts are off; `e2e/app.spec.ts`'s layout test counts the sections' Resets (Base, Silicone, Cover & joint, Pour & funnel and Grid, not Model) and Advanced's with every setting changed, and none after Reset; `test/unit/requirements.test.ts` holds "Tuning the defaults" to its facts.
  - Negative controls, each in a scratch copy, restored and checked by SHA-256: the schema reading the file as it was fails the load test (TypeError, reading 't'); `silicone.t` or `print.infill` written in the schema fails the every-value test; the check or the plugin keeping the byte-order mark, the plugin without its own JSON.parse, the pocket's rule worded as in the app and no ceiling on Infill each fail their row; a row's reset during a drag (3 against 4), the section's Reset in a constant group (24 against 25) or the group of its first setting (20 against 22) fail the Reset test; README without "Stop the running server" fails the docs test; in Chromium, focus on the pressed option (focus not on Glue in (A)), the name's double-click as `setParam` (Undo's title "the change to Silicone thickness"), a reset keeping the refused entry (25 against 3), Cover & joint's Reset counting the shown bolts only and Advanced without its Reset fail the marks test, and Grid without its Reset the layout test.
  - Not taken: a watcher that checks the file while the dev server runs (README.md says to restart it; `scripts/serve.sh`, the Pages build and the test servers each start fresh), and moving focus to Undo after the header's Reset, which the design left on the page, as Undo and Redo do when they turn themselves off.

Measured at CS (before at 3310268, after; at the 380 px sidebar, Chromium and Firefox the same where one figure is given):
- **Slider track:** 163 to 139 px, over the design's floor of 120. **Mode, Bolt size and Bolt length:** 227 to 203 px; every option on one line (scrollHeight equal to clientHeight), and the length list 24 px tall, "Auto, 12 mm" on one line. A row's parts within it: slider at 120, 139 px; mark at 267, 16 x 20; box at 291, 56 px; the wide rows' mark at 331, 64 px right of the others.
- **Layout:** `checkLayout` passes 48 times in each browser: at 1366 x 768 and 1920 x 1080, in both themes, with Advanced closed and open, empty at the defaults and with the bolts on, and with jersey at the defaults, with the bolts on, with every setting changed and after Reset (40 at 3310268, without the last). In Chromium at 1920 x 1080 with every setting changed, Diagnostics' "rows below" line still counts the 1366 window's rows when the check comes before the resize event reaches the page, at 3310268 as now; two frames later it passes, which the layout test now waits for.
- **No shift:** the slider, mark and box of Silicone thickness, its section's heading and first row stand to 0.01 px where they stood at the default once it is changed, and the section's Reset appears at its top right (313.3, 12; 53.7 x 20) with nothing moving.
- **Contrast,** from the computed styles: the changed name 6.61:1 on the panel in the dark theme and 5.90:1 in the light, the arrow the same, and the dot 4.07:1 and 3.53:1, against 4.5, 3 and 3.
- **Undo:** a row's reset 1 step, or 0 when it joins a change of the same control within 400 ms that it takes back; a section's or the header's Reset 1; with nothing changed 0, and the store's tick does not move, where setting the same value again does.
- **Cost:** `changedPaths` over the 75 settings, in Node on this server, 48 to 52 microseconds a call as first written (each path split twice) and 3.6 to 5.4 with the paths split once into a table, where the design asked under 50; the header, the five sections' Reset and Advanced's two ask it 8 times a draw. `vite build`: 0.91 to 0.94 s before, 1.16 to 1.19 s now, the check's module loaded through the runner.
- **Test time:** the layout smoke test 5.5, 3.8 and 2.9 s (dev, preview, Firefox) before, 8.9, 7.3 and 5.0 s now (7.6 and 5.0 s in Chromium's preview and Firefox with the review's counts); the new smoke test 8.7 s in Chromium and 7.6 s in Firefox, 8.6 and 7.9 s with the review's checks; the unit tests added about 3 s: about 30 s more for `scripts/ci.sh`, within the design's 60.

### MM1: the memory meter

Not one of the plan's milestones: the first half of an item of 0.2.0 the user asked for on 2026-11-09, "realtime ram ussage metter in the bottom bar + warning if user attempt to load a large model + recomendation to simplify it" (`docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, section 2 and section 8; R41, D23). It was designed first by a read-only designer, with the meter as part A of three; this run builds the meter only. The warning before loading and the one-click simplify are MM2, which finds its seams ready: the ledger's `simplify` source and `MEMORY_WARN_BYTES`.

Checks:
- **What it counts** (`src/app/memory.ts`, pure TS with no DOM, loaded by the workers too). `arrayBytes(roots, seen)` adds up the ArrayBuffers that the roots reach, each buffer once and at its full length, so a subarray counts the buffer it keeps alive, two views of one buffer count it once, and a detached buffer, as a transfer leaves it, counts 0; one `seen` set goes on from one walk to the next, so the view's arrays that are the pipeline's count once. A root's own fields are walked whatever its class; below the roots only plain objects and arrays are, so a Manifold handle, a three.js object or a WebAssembly module is passed over, and so is an array that can hold no typed array: one of numbers by its first item, or a list of points, an array whose first item is an array of numbers. Cycles are walked once, and past 200,000 objects a walk stops and reads low. `MemoryLedger` keeps each source's latest figure (`page`, `field`, `solid`, `reports`, `export`, `simplify`), drops one as its worker goes, adds them up, and tells its listeners whether a change was a drop.
- **Where the figures come from.** Main: `Pipeline.heldArrays()`, an explicit list: the file, the import's input (its mesh when it changed the file's, and the rims of the model's holes), `parsed`, `oriented`, `imported`, `displayProxy`, `joined`, `footprint`, `field`, `placement`, the cover, the base, the funnel, the reports, the wall check, and the last export's files, which main keeps for the single parts while its geometry is current (the design's list, with the import's input and the last export's files added). The store adds a file waiting on the question whether to replace unsaved work, its bytes or the opened project, which can be the whole file and which nothing else holds until it is answered. The view: `PreviewScene.heldArrays()`, every geometry in the scene, those the section keeps for its count, the height field and its texture's data, and the picker's tree, whose own fields are walked (`_roots` and its indirect buffer). Each worker, in every message it already sends to a JobGate or an export (`ready`, `stage`, `result`, `miss`, `error`, `progress`; an optional `memory: { wasm, arrays }`, the only change on the wire): its WebAssembly heap at its full size (`memory.buffer.byteLength`, 0 in field.worker) and the arrays its caches keep (`FieldStages`, `ReportStages` and `SolidStages.heldArrays()`, the last the import's mesh, holes and joined parts and the footprint's mesh, as the solids are in the heap). A solid worker's walk starts with the heap's own buffer as seen (`solidMemory`), so a view into it never counts twice; `bootManifold` now also gives that buffer (`heap()`), and `solidHost` takes it in place of `heapBytes`. An export's worker reports, as each step begins, its inputs and what `runExport` has put in its `holding` list so far: the oriented mesh from the import on, the export field as soon as the field step ends, the whole build from the clearance on, and the parts' files from the zip step. The build has one place in the list, which a raise of the margin takes over once its build is done (both count while it runs), so the list keeps nothing alive that the export has let go; it changes nothing the export makes. meshoptimizer's own small heap is not exposed and not counted. Nothing asks a worker for a figure, and the test-only heap probe is never used.
- **When a figure goes.** `JobGate` passes on `message.memory` from its current worker, whichever job the message answers (`onMemory`), and calls `onMemory(null)` as it terminates the worker, in a respawn after a crash or a boot failure, a recycle, an overrun ended for a newer request, and `dispose()`; the pipeline's ledger drops the gate's source. The export's worker reports while it is the export's (`ready` and `progress`), and `endExportWorker` drops it, so the figure goes when the export answers, fails, crashes into its retry or is cancelled.
- **When it reads** (`src/app/store.ts`). `store.memory`, a signal of `{ total, parts }`, set only when a figure changed, so the strip's item draws only then. `startMemoryMeter(page)`, which main.tsx calls with `document.hidden` and `visibilitychange`, reads at once, then after each change the pipeline makes and each figure a worker sends, at most every 500 ms (`MEMORY_THROTTLE_MS`), and at once when a figure falls, which takes a waiting reading's place, so a figure that fell never shows late. While the page is hidden nothing is read, and a waiting reading is dropped; it reads again as the page shows. `setViewRoots` hands it the scene's arrays. `measureMemory()` is one reading: one walk of main's arrays, the view's and a file waiting on the replace question as `page`, then the ledger's total. Kept out at that question, the file leaves the meter at once. A reading runs inside the handlers of the workers' messages and of the pipeline's changes, so it never throws: a walk that fails leaves the page's last figure. The gate's `onMemory` and the export's report are caught as well, and a worker whose figure cannot be worked out sends its message without one (`figureOrNone`), so the meter can never stop a result, an error or an export.
- **The strip** (`src/ui/StatusStrip.tsx`, `src/ui/format.ts`). `MemoryItem`, after the toggles and before the controls hint, reads `store.memory` alone, so a reading draws nothing else of the strip: "App memory 110 MB", in decimal units, to 10 MB below 1 GB and to 0.1 GB from there (`formatMemory`, `memoryText`), and "App memory …" until the first worker has said what it holds, as main's share alone would read low; in the strip's grey, amber (`warn`) while the reading is `MEMORY_WARN_BYTES` or more. Its native title (`memoryTitle`) gives "Memory this app holds:" and the total to 1 MB or 0.01 GB; past the warning figure, right after it, "That is a lot for one web page: close other browser tabs to free memory, or open a model with fewer triangles." (the design's line pointed at the Model section's simplify, which is MM2's); then each part that holds half a megabyte or more, by name ("This page (model, parts, view)", "Preview worker", "Geometry engine", "Reports worker", "Simplify" for MM2), as one that would read 0 MB says nothing: the reports' worker keeps plain objects, which are not counted, so its line shows only if it ever keeps arrays. "Export" always has its line, which reads "not running" between exports. Last, what is not counted, the same sentence in both browsers: "Not counted: the rest of the browser, with your other tabs and its extensions (on the test computer, 0.5 to 0.6 GB with only this page open and no model); the app's plain JavaScript data; copies of the parts on the graphics card; and the memory a step uses while it runs, of which the meter sees only what is left when the step ends (opening a large model uses the most). So a system monitor shows more: on the test computer, up to 2.6 GB more with a 3 million triangle model. No web page can see how much memory the computer has free."
- **The warning figure.** `MEMORY_WARN_BYTES` is 0.8 GB: the meter is amber while it reads that or more. It is set from the meter's own readings, which are the same in Chromium and Firefox, as both run the same arrays and the same heap: the 3 million triangle test model reads 0.84 GB once its preview stands and 1.18 GB in merged mode; every one of the 15 example files, opened, set to merged mode (glue for the oil barrel, where merged is blocked) and exported with the fit test, peaks at 0.735 GB at most, doomedmarine's (285,260 triangles), with the export's worker beside the preview's solid worker, then Benchy at 0.506 and chick at 0.490 GB. As first built it was 0.7 GB, from glue-mode exports only, which review found turned doomedmarine amber during a merged-mode export (0.735 GB); the design's provisional 1.5 GB would have left the 3 million triangle preview grey. The margins are thin on both sides (0.735 and 0.84 GB), so a test holds each: the soak's amber once the preview stands, and doomedmarine's merged-mode export with the fit test grey from start to end. The amber shows the reading as it stands, not the model: review suggested keeping it once a model had reached the figure, but a meter whose colour does not match its number reads as wrong, and warning about the model itself before it is read is MM2's. So with the 3 million triangle model it is grey while the model opens (the import's memory shows only as it ends), amber once the preview stands, grey as the export starts and recycles the preview's solid worker, amber again as the export's worker grows, and grey once it ends.
- **What the browser offers, left out.** `performance.memory`, Chromium's alone, covers main's JavaScript heap only, and refreshes at most every 20 minutes and in rounded steps unless Chrome is started with a flag (`kTwentyMinutes` in Blink's `memory_info.cc`); `performance.measureUserAgentSpecificMemory()` needs cross-origin isolation; `navigator.deviceMemory` is rough, Chromium's alone and needs a secure context. `test/unit/static.test.ts` now refuses the last two in `src` with the secure-context APIs; outside `src/diag`, any use of `performance` but its clock, `performance.now()`, so the first cannot be read through a cast, brackets or another name either, nor by its fields' names; and any call of the heap probe in the meter's own files and in every `heldArrays()`, `solidMemory` and the hosts' figures.
- **Found on the way: a race the meter's clock tipped.** As designed, the store also read once a second. With that clock, in Firefox, the 3 million triangle model's first field job (S1 to S7 in one job, 8.1 s on this server) was ended at its 8 s budget in every run (5 of 5, and in each variant that kept the clock), and run again from the file, while the solid worker's whole import stood, which took the preview's PSS peak from 2,728 to 2,766 MiB to 2,966 to 3,036 and over the soak's 3 GB (2,861 MiB). The job log showed it: "field terminated job 1 after 8072 ms", where HEAD's job ended at 8117 ms unended. After the field reaches main (the job's `stage` message), the pipeline asks the field gate for the placement alone, a target with another id though the running job is building it under the same keys, so the overrun check finds "a newer request" waiting; whether that check or the job's result comes first, once main is free again after drawing the whole mesh, decided the rest, and a timer due at that moment put the check first. Bisected in scratch builds: without the meter started, 2,734; with only the clock and no reading at all, 2,973 and the job ended; with the meter but no clock, 2,735 and the job unended. So the meter has no clock: it reads only on the events above, which also means it does no work at all while nothing changes. The race itself is HEAD's and untouched here: a field job past 8 s on a model past G1.8's limit, as on a slower computer, can still be ended for its own placement. It is reported for a fix of its own (the field gate taking a request it is already building as no newer one).
- **Review.** Three reviewers read the run: what it counts, its tests, and how it looks and reads to the user in Firefox. Fixed from it: the export's build in one place in `holding`, and its field from the step after it (a raise had kept the first build alive, and the field counted only from the clearance); the meter never stopping a job (above); the rims of the model's holes and a file waiting on the replace question counted; the warning figure raised to 0.8 GB, with every example measured in merged mode with the fit test; the soak's share taken at the export's own peak (below); the tooltip's wording, its advice first, and no part that reads 0 MB; "App memory …" before the first worker's figure; two timing faults in the new smoke test (it polled for the export's heap, which its `ready` already has, and then asserted its arrays; and it waited out the Cancel guard from before the click, which the click's own checks could eat: now a flat half second once the step says clearance, as `e2e/export.spec.ts`'s cancel test, which had the same fault from before MM1, now does too); and the tests each fix needed, with the gaps they found: the hosts' `miss` and `error`, every `heldArrays()` against a walk of everything its owner holds, the view's arrays and the one walk, `holding`, an old worker's late message, the static rule's gaps, the meter in the full suite's loop over the examples, and pins of the throttle and of the warning figure against the docs. Left as built: the strip at 1920 x 1080 wraps the controls hint to a second line for most models (below); the reviewer's fixes, a hint that hides or shrinks, or moves into the view, are a change to the strip the user has not asked for, and a shorter label alone does not make the room. And the amber stays the reading as it stands (above).
- **Tests.** `test/unit/memory.test.ts` (3 s): arrayBytes' buffers, views, subarrays, detached buffers and cycles; the WebAssembly heap seeded as seen, and `solidMemory`; classes walked as roots only; the walk's limit and the list of points; the ledger; the strip's text and the tooltip's lines; the store's readings with a hand-moved clock and a page that hides and shows; every `ready`, `stage`, `result`, `miss`, `error` and `progress` of the gates' and an export's workers carrying `memory`, field.worker's at least the welded mesh, solid.worker's heap equal to `heapBytes`, the solid figure gone on `recycleSolid()` until the fresh worker says ready, the export's while it runs, at least its inputs, and gone after; the export's figure at each step: the export field from the step after it, the parts' files from the zip step, and after a raise of the margin one build, not two; the hosts' `miss` and `error` replies with the worker's figure, and a worker whose figure throws still sending its result and its error without one; a failing figure on main never stopping the export's steps; the view's arrays counted once with the pipeline's, and a file waiting on the replace question counted until it is kept out, which reads at once; a walk that throws leaving the page's last figure; each `heldArrays()` of the pipeline and of the field, reports and solid caches against a walk of everything its owner holds, through any class, Map and Set, for jersey after an export, a box with a hole and an inside-out box; main's share holding the file, the welded mesh, the cover, the base and the last export's files, each once (jersey, where the design named Benchy: the same checks in about a second rather than 32 s); and the throttle at 500 ms and the warning figure as README.md, `docs/DEPLOY.md`, `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` and this file give it. `test/unit/jobGate.test.ts`: the figure passed on from any message, dropped on recycle, crash, overrun and dispose, an old worker's late message not counted, and a meter that throws not stopping a result or a respawn. `test/unit/static.test.ts`: the two words, and "reads no memory figure the browser rounds or delays, and the memory meter never probes a heap". `e2e/app.spec.ts`: the layout test now checks the meter on the Wireframe label's line within 2 px, on one line and inside the strip, empty and with jersey at both sizes, and with `CFG_FULL` with each of the eight examples of the regression set; the new smoke test, in Chromium (development server and build) and Firefox: the text and title are `memoryText` and `memoryTitle` of the store's reading, the total, the parts that hold something and what is not counted, Benchy's file and meshes in the page's share and its welded mesh in the preview worker's, the view's arrays adding to the page's share and the pipeline's counted once when the view holds them too, no amber, an export held at its clearance step showing its heap, inputs and build and dropping them at Cancel, and one that ends dropping them with main then holding its files; and with `CFG_FULL`, in the build and Firefox, doomedmarine opened, set to merged mode and exported with the fit test without the meter ever turning amber, its highest reading logged. `e2e/hardening.spec.ts`'s 3 million triangle soak now logs every reading of the meter, with the export's share, by the page's clock, and holds it at or under the PSS sampled every 200 ms at every sample, amber once the preview stands, a reading under 2 ms on main; it logs the meter's share at the export's own PSS peak, from its worker's first figure to the reading that dropped it, and in Firefox its share of the growth outside the main process, and writes the samples to `memory-samples.json` in its output folder. `test/unit/requirements.test.ts`: R01 to R41, D1 to D23, U1 to U9 and section 8's row.
- **Negative controls** (each in a scratch copy of the tree, the file restored and its SHA-256 checked against the working tree's): a view counted at its own length (1,020 against 1,000, and the seeded heap and the share), no `seen` set (2,000 against 1,000; 801,799 against 771,827), classes walked at any depth (800 against 0), point lists walked point by point (0 against 100), no heap seeded in `solidMemory` (4,120 against 24), a dropped figure kept by the ledger, the strip's text to 1 MB, no throttle (21 readings against 1), a reading while hidden (both of the schedule's checks out: 6 against 5; one alone is caught by the other), a falling figure while hidden, none as the page shows, a falling figure waiting for the throttle (3 against 4), field.worker's result without `memory`, `parsed` out of `heldArrays` (the design's control), the last export's files out of it, the export's figure kept after it ends, the export without its inputs, JobGate without the drop on respawn or on dispose, `deviceMemory`, `measureUserAgentSpecificMemory`, `performance.memory` plain and through a cast, and a heap probe from the store, planted in `src`, each fail. A late message of an ended worker counted before the gate's own check was not caught: the gate clears the old worker's handler first, so none arrives. In the browser: the meter first in the strip fails the layout test (17.4 px off the toggles' line); the export's figure kept after Cancel, the strip at 1 MB, and an export reporting nothing fail the smoke test in Chromium, and a warning figure of 0.1 GB (Benchy amber) and an empty page share fail it in Firefox; in the 3 million triangle soak (Chromium), the page's figure 3 GB high fails, listing the samples over PSS, and a warning figure of 0.9 GB fails its amber. After review, each of these fails as well, checked the same way: the export's field put in `holding` only after the build, a raise's build kept beside the first, the export's progress without `holding`, the import's mesh alone where its input belongs (the holes' rims missed), `SolidStages.heldArrays()` empty, `FieldStages.heldArrays()` without S6 and S7, the store's walk without its try/catch, the view and the pipeline walked apart, the waiting file left out, a kept-out file waiting for the throttle, the gate's and the export's meter calls without their try/catch, JobGate taking an old worker's late message, field.worker's figure without `figureOrNone`, a field miss, a reports error, a solid miss and a solid error each without `memory`, the tooltip listing parts at 0 MB or giving its advice last, the strip at 0 MB before a worker's word, the warning figure back at 0.7 GB against the docs, a throttle of 400 ms, and, planted in `src`, `performance.memory` through another name and through brackets, and a heap probe in `Pipeline.heldArrays()` and in solid.worker's figure. In the browser: the view and the pipeline walked apart, and no view arrays handed to the store, fail the smoke test; and a warning figure of 0.7 GB fails doomedmarine's.
- **Bytes.** Nothing that exports changes: the ZIP of jersey, Benchy, the barrel and the chick at defaults, of jersey with eight settings changed, and, after review, of jersey with its margin raised and Benchy in merged mode, with the fit test, gives every file the same SHA-256 as at c6a405b (each STL, the fit test's, README.txt, project.json, and report.json past its timings), with the build's stamp held fixed; M9's and FL's byte checks pass, and SCHEMA_VERSION stays 6.
- **Docs.** README.md (the strip and a big file), `docs/DEPLOY.md` (memory in the browser), `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` (R41, D23, U9, section 8's row), this section, the Workers memory rule, the GUI's status strip and the static checks above.

Measured at MM1 (on this server, the 3 million triangle fixture of 3,011,040 triangles, PSS from /proc every 200 ms over the browser's tree, one browser at a time):
- **The meter against PSS:** never above it: in the soak test, 262 samples in Chromium and 270 in Firefox as first built, and after review 261 and 298 in Chromium and 275, 270 and 270 in Firefox; and in every sample of fourteen scratch runs (278 to 291 samples each) and of the look review's own run (269). The least room left between them, 476 to 578 MiB, is the browser's own. At the export's own PSS peak, between its worker's first figure and the reading that dropped it, the meter showed 58 and 59% of what the browser had grown by since the empty app in Chromium (2,154 MiB against 510 empty in the first of those runs; 64% in the first build's soak, taken the same way), and 46 to 48% in Firefox (2,684 and 2,613 MiB against 578 and 574 in the first two), 48 to 52% in the first build's runs and the look review's: about half, where the design asked at least 50%. As first built the soak took the export phase's highest sample, its first, where the solid worker of the merged preview still stood, and read 53 to 58% in Firefox there. Firefox's main process held 0.4 to 0.5 GB of that growth (368 to 478 MiB), and of the rest the meter showed 59%. The export's last step, the reports and the ZIP, 10 to 12 s with this model, sends nothing until it is done, and the browser grows by about 0.25 GB in it. The meter's highest reading is 1.18 GB, in merged mode, against PSS peaks of 2,183 to 2,195 MiB (Chromium) and 2,764 to 2,873 (Firefox). The browser held up to 2.0 GB (Chromium) and 2.6 GB (Firefox) more than the meter showed, at the preview's first import, whose heap the meter sees only for a moment, as the worker answers and is recycled; 0.8 and 1.4 GB once all is done.
- **Firefox's main process:** 402 to 421 MiB PSS with the app empty, growing by 530 to 708 MiB at most as it reads and holds the 150 MB file, and 368 to 478 MiB above its empty size at the export's own peak. No page can see it. It varies by about 0.1 GB from run to run, at HEAD too, and that is most of the spread of Firefox's PSS peak in the soak: HEAD's soak peaked at 2,769, 2,842 and 2,805 MiB in three runs (the main process 866 to 954 MiB at that moment, the content process 1,815 to 1,820), and this tree's at 2,873 (979 and 1,820) and 2,805 (900 and 1,820), against the soak's bound of 2,861: the bound is as close at HEAD.
- **The empty app:** 509 to 514 MiB PSS in Chromium and 574 to 593 in Firefox, the meter reading 17 MB (the solid worker's first heap).
- **A reading on main** with the 3 million triangle model loaded, over 20: 0.045 to 0.09 ms in Chromium and 0.10 to 0.15 ms in Firefox, against the design's 2 ms. In the workers each message's walk is of their caches' entries alone.
- **The examples**, the same in both browsers to the byte: all 15 example files, each opened, set to merged mode (glue for the oil barrel, where merged is blocked) and exported with the fit test, read 18 to 194 MB once open; doomedmarine 194 MB, 370 MB in merged mode and 735 MB at most, during its export; Benchy 110, 235 and 506 MB; chick 152, 249 and 490 MB; every other one 298 MB at most; none turned amber. As first built, measured with glue-mode exports, the highest were 377 MB (Benchy) and 558 MB (doomedmarine).
- **Layout:** every example's file name, at 1366 x 768 and 1920 x 1080, in Chromium and Firefox, passes `checkLayout`, with the meter on one line (15.4 px tall) on the Wireframe label's line to 0 px. At 1366 the strip was already two lines with a model, and its height does not change; at 1920 the meter takes the room the controls hint had, so for 10 of the 15 examples the hint now goes to a second line and the strip is 42 px where it was 28 (empty, it stays one line at both sizes).
- **Firefox's preview peak with and without the meter's clock** (see above): HEAD 2,728 to 2,766 MiB; with a one-second clock 2,966 to 3,036; as built 2,560 to 2,782, and 2,680 in the soak test.
- **Test time:** the new smoke test 9.0 s (development server), 7.4 s (build) and 7.1 s (Firefox) after review; the layout test unchanged (8.1 to 9.5, 7.3 to 7.6 and 5.0 to 5.9 s); `test/unit/memory.test.ts` 3 s: about 27 s more for `scripts/ci.sh`, within the design's 30. With `CFG_FULL`, doomedmarine's test takes about 22 s in the build and in Firefox. The soak test takes 55 to 60 s per browser.

### MM2: the large-model prompt and simplify

Not one of the plan's milestones: the rest of the item of 0.2.0 the user asked for on 2026-11-09, "realtime ram ussage metter in the bottom bar + warning if user attempt to load a large model + recomendation to simplify it" (`docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, sections 2 and 8; R42, D24). MM1 built the meter. This run builds the warning before a large model is read and the one-click simplify, with the user's answers of 2026-11-16: `model_oriented.stl` stays the user's own file; the copy's count is editable, from 10,000 to 1,000,000, 300,000 by default, its 0.05 mm bound fixed; the warning point is set from a measured curve; and a model loaded anyway has Simplify…. It also fixes the race MM1 found. It was designed first by a read-only designer (the addendum to MM1's design, with probes in Node), with the memory curve measured on 92724e8 before any change.

Checks:
- **Found at MM1, fixed here: a job and the request it is already building.** After the field reaches main (the job's `stage` message), the pipeline asks the field gate for the placement alone, a target with another id though the running job builds it under the same keys, so `checkOverrun` took it for a newer request and could end the job past its budget: MM1's 3 million triangle first field job, 8.1 s in Firefox, ended at 8 s and ran again from the file. The curve's runs found the same on the solid gate: the first job imports (S1, S2), and once the field is in the pipeline asks for S1, S2, S13, S15 and S16 under the same keys, which ended the 3 million triangle import at 8,001 and 8,084 ms in the repeat runs and imported it again, taking Firefox's preview to 3,100 MiB. Now a `GateTarget` says what it builds (`builds`, one entry per stage under its key, the field's with the rim key it is taken on), and `JobGate` takes a request whose every entry the running job builds as no newer one (it waits for nothing: the job builds it), and a waiting request that wants everything the running job builds and more as no reason to end that job (it runs next). Any other request is newer, and ends an overrun job as before; reports' targets and targets without `builds` keep the id rule. No output changes.
- **The warning point** (`src/app/largeModel.ts`): 500,000 triangles or 125 MB, from the curve below. `minBytesToLook` is 25,000,084 bytes, so no file below 25 MB is looked at first, and nothing new happens for any example.
- **Counting before reading** (`src/core/stl/estimate.ts`, `fileKindOfHead`): from the first 64 kB and the size, in parseStl's order: a binary file of the size its count gives is exact; a text file is counted by its `endfacet`s and scaled by its size (within 3% on the examples), exact when the head holds it whole, or size over 250 with no facet in the head; any other file is binary with its count clamped as parseStl clamps it. The question gives a binary file's count to the triangle, as the file has it (collapsed triangles are dropped only once parsed), and a text file's as "about" to three figures.
- **The question** (`Store.openFile`, `answerLarge`; `ModelSection`'s `large-prompt`). An STL at the point is asked about before `file.arrayBuffer()`, so nothing is read or started; a `.moldproj` in project.worker once opened (`OpenJob.large`), which answers its counts and not its model, as before the question main holds nothing of it. It says the file's count and size and what a model that large took in our tests, the running maximum of the curve's peaks at or below its count, and offers Simplify with a number box, Load anyway and Cancel; for Simplify… on a model on screen it has no Load anyway and says the undo history is cleared. Load anyway reads the file as any file is read (a `.moldproj` is opened again, 1 to 3 s at 3 million triangles); Cancel leaves the page; a newer file drops the question; the replace question about unsaved work comes after, as for any file.
- **The copy** (`simplifyModel` in `src/core/mesh/simplify.ts`, `src/core/mesh/deviation.ts`, `src/workers/prepareJob.ts`, `src/app/modelSimplifier.ts`). A fresh solid.worker per job runs `runPrepareJob`: read the file with FileReaderSync (a `.moldproj`'s `model.stl`), SHA-256, parse with the reader's words, weld, audit; then the lock loop: each round simplifies the welded file afresh (`simplifyWithAttributes`, `ErrorAbsolute`, `LockBorder`, no `Prune`, meshoptimizer's limit at 0.05 mm) and proves, of each of the file's triangles, that every point of it lies within 0.05 mm of the copy (`SurfaceIndex.bound`, below: as built first, the loop measured only the file's dropped vertices, centroids and edge midpoints, which the review found is no bound); the corners of every triangle it cannot are locked, and the next round asks for two more triangles for each vertex newly locked, four after a round that did not halve the triangles past the bound, eight after another; it ends when every triangle is proved within the bound, a round locks nothing, or after 12 rounds, and where triangles are still past it the figure is proved with a reach that grows until it holds. **The bound** (`SurfaceIndex.bound` in `deviation.ts`): the copy's triangles in a hashed grid of 0.5 mm cells, each in every cell its box grown by the reach covers, and each triangle's neighbours across its edges. The distance to one triangle is convex, so over a convex piece of the file's surface it is largest at a corner: a piece whose corners all lie within the goal of one triangle of the copy lies within it everywhere. Each of the file's triangles is tried whole against the triangle that proved the one before it and that triangle's neighbours (a file's triangles mostly follow on from each other), then against the triangles in its centroid's cell, the nearest last; where none proves it, it is cut by the three planes standing on the edges of the triangle nearest its centroid, square to it, into the part over that triangle and the parts beside each edge, each tried again, and a part that cutting does not shrink is halved across its longest extent, at most 256 parts a triangle. The goal is the largest distance measured exactly at a point of the surface so far (its vertices, and each part's centroid) plus 0.0005 mm, at most the limit: so the bound comes within 0.0005 mm of the largest distance found, and is a bound wherever a triangle's parts run out, as its least proved figure stands. Polygons are plain arrays, as millions of small typed arrays cost twice the time to make. Then Manifold must take the copy as a solid where it took the file (the audit's non-manifold edges are no reason: Benchy's copy has 3 that Manifold pairs up), and the copy is written with the app's writer and hashed. The job reports `read`, `parse`, `simplify` and `measure` with the round, the two halves of each round, `check` and `write`, each with `memory` from one `holding` slot each step replaces, which the meter shows as "Simplify": in each half round the welded file, the locks, the bound's distance at each vertex, the copy, its buckets and neighbours, so the meter follows the lock loop (as built first it held the welded file alone through the loop, a tenth of what the step took: the review's finding); meshoptimizer's own working memory, in its own WebAssembly memory, which it does not expose, is not counted, and the tooltip says so; Cancel, a newer file or the answer ends the worker and drops the figure. Before Simplify… a heavy preview solid worker goes first (`recycleForPrepare`, the export's rule).
- **What the mold is made from, and the margin.** The copy loads as the model (`Pipeline.load`/`loadProject` with a `ModelOrigin`), named after the file, its hash the file's; every stage builds on it. `fieldSettings(…, extraMarginMm)` adds the copy's proved error to G4's print margin, for the preview's field (`FieldInputs.modelErrorMm`), the wall check, the pitch and spacing main shows, and the export (`printMargin + modelErrorMm`, and the raise on top): 0, and so the same bytes, for a model loaded as it is. The file id is in every key from S1 down, so no key needs the error.
- **`model_oriented.stl`** (`readOriginal` in `exportJob.ts`). For a glue-mode Export ZIP of a copy, `ExportInputs.original` hands the export's worker the file (a File or Blob, by reference) with the SHA-256 and facts the prepare job took; the worker reads it, checks the hash (a file moved or changed stops the ZIP: "could not be read again for model_oriented.stl, so nothing was saved…"), parses, welds, turns it by the plan's transform, reversed where the copy's import flipped, and writes it; its volume is its signed volume less what the copy's parts share. With a trim (the plane from the copy needs one), or where the copy's import flipped an inside-out separate body (`shell-inverted`, which only the whole solid can do again in the file, `ExportInputs.original.wholeSolid`: the review's finding, as the file was written with that body still inside out and its volume taken off), the file is imported whole in a scratch arena, trimmed if need be and written as for a model loaded anyway, in the step `original` right after the import, so the build reuses that heap; otherwise `original` comes after the clearance. Single parts and merged mode never read the file.
- **The clearance**, glue mode: the build is the copy's; minGap is measured on the copy decimated to 60,000 triangles as G4 does (or the copy's own solid under it), less the decimation's error proved over the copy's whole surface (`measuredError`, `SurfaceIndex.bound` with a reach from 0.5 mm that doubles until it holds; not meshoptimizer's figure, which is 1.7 to 6.7 times too small), less the copy's own proved error (`Clearance.simplify`): a bound on the file's gap by the triangle inequality. The review asked whether the copy could be measured whole instead, as the 3 million triangle block's decimation error comes to about 0.25 mm and its export then raises the margin; that was left as G4 has it, as minGap pairs every model triangle with every cover triangle within its 8 mm search, so its cost grows with the model's triangles, which is why G4 decimates to 60,000; the one raise covers the shortfall, at the cost of a mold about 0.1 mm larger than the preview. The one raise covers a shortfall. Merged mode measures against the copy, which the base carries, with the decimation's error measured too.
- **Files.** report.json's `model` keeps its keys, the file's counts and the copy's bodies and volume, and gains `simplified` (the record); `clearance` gains `simplifyErrorMm` in glue mode, with `minGap` the sum; `parts['model_oriented.stl']` is the file's. README.txt's `model_oriented.stl` entry names the file and its triangles, or where the plane trims it the triangles written, and says the silicone thickness was checked to hold for it, measured on the copy less the copy's error; merged mode's `base.stl` entry says the base carries the copy; "This mold" gives the copy's count, the count asked and its error; the clearance line says which errors were taken off; the project files' paragraph says Save project keeps the copy and how to make it again. project.json gains `simplified` after `basePlane`. Each only when the model was simplified.
- **The project format.** A `.moldproj` of a copy holds `project.json`, `model.stl` (the file, which `ProjectFiles.pack` has project.worker read from the File and check against `modelSha256`) and `simplified.stl` (the copy main holds). Opening checks `model.stl` against `modelSha256` as before and `simplified.stl` against the record; the copy then loads with no question, and any mismatch opens the file as it is with a warning. `readFields` checks the record (whole counts, a target under the file's and at most 1,000,000, finite errors, a SHA-256) and drops one that fails with the same warning. SCHEMA_VERSION stays 6. Settings from an export ZIP or a project.json made on the copy, opened onto the file as it is (or the other way), say so in an info note: their hashes match, as both are the file's.
- **The Model section and the strip.** The file row reads "statue.stl, 752,814 triangles (simplified)", the strip "752,814 triangles (simplified)". The note under the file row (`simplified-note`) gives the copy's count, its error, "Not 300,000…" where it kept more, and the true figure where it missed the bound, with Use the original (the file again, with the project carried, no question, history cleared; its plane found again by `checkPlane`, worded for the copy) and Save simplified STL (`<name>-simplified.stl`). Simplify… (`simplify-loaded`) shows for a model past the point that is no copy. While the copy is made, the busy line says it can take a minute or more, and the step and the round of at most 12, with Cancel. The question quotes, for the file, the curve's highest peak at or below its count, and past 2 million triangles the 3 million triangle model's own; for Simplify, what the whole simplified flow of that model took against it loaded as it is (`simplifyQuote`), as the curve at the count asked for overstated the saving 2.5 times (the review's finding). The diagnostics row `model-simplified` says what the mold and `model_oriented.stl` are. App memory's advice past its amber figure points at Simplify… where it shows (`MEMORY_ADVICE_SIMPLIFY`).
- **Bytes.** Before any change, every file of the Export ZIP with the fit test (each STL, README.txt, project.json, report.json past its timings) and the `.moldproj` were hashed at 92724e8 for jersey, Benchy, the barrel and the chick at defaults, jersey with eight settings changed, jersey with its margin raised and Benchy in merged mode; the working tree gives the same 186 hashes, with the build's stamp held fixed. After the review's fixes, a store-level harness (the review's) gives the same 525 hashes as 92724e8: every file of the ZIP, every single part, each `.moldproj`, and each `.moldproj` reopened, exported and saved again, for jersey at defaults, with its grid, bolts and t changed, merged and with its margin raised, Benchy at defaults and merged, the barrel, the chick at defaults and merged, and a synthetic model the plane trims. A unit test pins jersey's two cases and Benchy at defaults, the version masked and README.txt by its words.
- **Tests.** `test/unit/jobGate.test.ts` (the race: a field job past its budget not ended for its own placement, a newer placement still ending it, a field under another rim newer, a solid import not ended for a request that wants it and more, another model's request newer; the rim key in the pipeline's field entry is also implied by the placement's, as S7 depends on S10, so no pipeline test can tell it is there); `test/unit/pipeline.test.ts` (the same on the real field worker, its result held back past a 300 ms budget, and a rim it is not building still ending it); `test/unit/deviation.test.ts` (exact distances against dense sampling, the check against brute force on 3,000 points, a point beyond the reach, the samples of a cube; the bound: a hole between a triangle's samples, which they read as 0 and it proves at 0.5 mm, random triangles never under dense sampling and within the slack of it, every triangle with a point past the limit past, nothing within the reach); `test/unit/stlEstimate.test.ts` (every example's count from its head, header counts as parseStl reads them, text estimates, `fileKindOfHead` against `fileKind`, the warning point and its boundaries, the counts and quotes); `test/unit/simplifyModel.test.ts` (Benchy to 50,000 with its pinned SHA-256, the test block's ripple where round 1 breaks the bound and the loop does not, the recorded figure never under dense sampling and within the slack of it, Goddess Statue to 12,000 proved within 0.05 mm where its samples alone had left 0.068, war-barricade proved within it, a loop that cannot keep to the bound recording the figure proved, the prepare job's steps, facts and record, a `.moldproj` source, unreadable files, a copy with a hole refused and Benchy's non-manifold edges not, a count at or over the file's); `test/unit/largeModel.test.ts` (the store's flow with in-process workers: one look at the head and none at the whole, Cancel, Load anyway's bytes, doomedmarine read at once at the real point, the copy and its record, its meter figure and worker gone, Cancel mid-job, a newer file, Use the original, Save simplified STL, Simplify… with the project carried, the `.moldproj` of a copy, a copy that does not match, a `.moldproj` past the point, settings across copy and file;
