after the review: an edit made while the copy is made or the file read again is kept, the base face, moved pour and removed vent carried by Simplify…, by a `.moldproj`'s question and by Use the original, a picked face, Use the original after reopening a simplified `.moldproj`, a newer file during the copy, Save project of a changed file, a refused copy's question); `test/unit/simplifiedExport.test.ts` (Benchy's glue ZIP against the file welded and turned, every file's words and keys, merged mode reading nothing and its wider box, the reassembled file at least t with a copy 0.20 mm off, and the measured clearance the copy's own gap less its error; a file lying 0.2 mm outside its copy, nearer the cover, which still keeps t and whose gap report.json does not overstate, where the copy's own gap is 0.15 mm wider and taking no error off fails; an inside-out separate body flipped in `model_oriented.stl`; the decimation's error proved against dense sampling; a changed file; a trim of the file and its words); `test/unit/export.test.ts` (the pinned bytes, Benchy at defaults added after the review for G4's decimated clearance); `test/unit/memory.test.ts` (the prepare worker's figures, the lock loop's arrays among them while it measures, the export's original from its step on, the advice); `test/unit/project.test.ts` (M11's test answers the question for its 671,089 triangle file); `e2e/large.spec.ts` (the question, Load anyway's geometry, Benchy's copy in the browser with Node's SHA-256, model_oriented.stl, Save simplified STL, Use the original, Simplify… and the amber tooltip's advice, Cancel during the copy, the `.moldproj` round trip with `model_oriented.stl` the file turned, a text file's estimate); `e2e/hardening.spec.ts` (the 3 million triangle soak answers Load anyway and asserts no job ended at its budget; a new soak simplifies it on opening, with bars on the copy step and the flow after it and a floor on the meter's Simplify part); `e2e/shots.spec.ts`'s heavy shot answers the question.

- **After the review** (three read-only reviewers: the guarantee and data, the tests, and the look and truth as the user on Firefox). Fixed:
  - *The copy's error was not a bound* (found in Node by sampling each copy again on a 6 to 16 step grid per triangle: Goddess Statue at 12,000 recorded 0.0499 mm where its surface lay 0.068 mm off, war-barricade 0.057 and 0.077, barricate_type_2 0.050 and 0.067), as the lock loop pushes exactly its samples under the bound. Now `SurfaceIndex.bound` proves it (above), and the loop locks on what it proves. War-barricade at 10,000 now keeps 42,442 triangles within 0.050 mm, Goddess at 12,000 15,548, doomedmarine at 50,000 50,072; Benchy's copy is the same, its figure 0.0367 mm for 0.0362 found. The export's decimation error is proved the same way, in about 1 s for a 750,000 triangle copy against its 60,000 triangle decimation (sampling took 4.2 s). The 3 million triangle block's copy now keeps 752,814 triangles in 10 rounds (it kept 501,230 at a sampled 0.050 mm, which was no bound), as its ripple needs them at a proved 0.05 mm, so its flow saves less memory than first measured (below), its export little.
  - *The meter showed a tenth of the copy step*: the lock loop's arrays now go to the meter in each half round (`simplifyModel`'s `hold`, the step `measure`), a quarter of the step's growth on the 3 million triangle block; meshoptimizer's own memory, in a WebAssembly memory its module keeps to itself, cannot be read, which the tooltip and U10 now say (the review's suggestion to count its buffer cannot be taken without changing meshoptimizer's own code).
  - *The prompt overstated the saving about 2.5 times*: it quoted the curve at the count asked for, where the copy keeps more and the export reads the file again; now it quotes the whole flow measured (`simplifyQuote`), and for a model past 2 million triangles the 3 million triangle model's own peak (2.9 GB), not 2 million's 3.4.
  - *Edits made while the copy was made were lost*: the project is now carried as it stands when the copy (or Use the original's file) arrives.
  - *An inside-out separate body stayed inside out in `model_oriented.stl`*, its volume taken off: the file now takes the whole-solid path there.
  - *Words*: a trimmed file is no longer "with all its N triangles" (README.txt gives the count written, the row says it is trimmed); README.txt and README.md say the thickness was checked to hold for the file, measured on the copy less its error, not "measured against this file"; the clearance line names the lighter copy and both errors; the prompt no longer promises 0.05 mm at the count asked ("or more where its shape needs them") nor compares with a grid finer than 0.1 mm; the view's row says the mold is built from all of the copy's triangles; the busy line says it can take a minute or more, and the round of at most 12; Save project of a changed file gives the reason, not "Try again"; README.md and DEPLOY.md agree on 2.9 to 3.0 GB for the 3 million triangle model in Firefox.
  - *Tests*: the reassembly test could not fail for the copy's error being taken off; now a file lying 0.2 mm outside its copy, nearer the cover, makes it fail, and the measured clearance is pinned to the copy's gap less its error. New tests for the carried base face, pour and vent (Simplify…, a `.moldproj`'s question, Use the original, a picked face), Use the original after reopening a simplified `.moldproj`, a newer file during the copy, Save project of a changed file, a refused copy's question, a field under another rim in the gate (the pipeline's rim key in the field's entry is also implied by the placement's key, so its control cannot fail), Cancel and Simplify… in the browser and the amber tooltip's advice; two assertions that could never fail now compare `model_oriented.stl` with the file welded, turned and written; the opt-in heavy screenshot answers the question; the soak asserts bars from the measured runs; the byte test says README.txt is compared by its words and pins Benchy too.
  - Left as it was: measuring minGap on the whole copy rather than its 60,000 triangle decimation, which the review offered for the 3 million triangle block's raise of about 0.08 mm: minGap pairs each model triangle with every cover triangle within its 8 mm search, so G4 decimates for its cost, and the one raise covers the shortfall.

Measured at MM2 (on this server, one browser at a time, PSS from /proc every 200 ms over the browser's tree):
- **The memory curve** on 92724e8, the 3 million triangle block at eleven sizes, 31 runs (20 Firefox, 11 Chromium), the whole browser from opening through glue, merged and an export with the meter paired with every sample: Firefox's PSS peaks were 1.22 to 1.27 GB at 299,520 triangles, 1.48 to 1.53 at 399,600, 1.50 to 1.57 at 449,334, 1.59 to 1.67 at 499,982, 1.88 to 1.89 at 600,590, 2.10 at 749,070, 2.55 to 2.60 at 999,680, 2.47 to 2.61 at 1,499,894, 3.25 to 3.39 at 1,999,982, 2.53 at 2,007,990 and 2.89 to 3.25 at 3,011,040 (decimal GB); Chromium's 1.18, 1.59, 1.77, 1.93, 2.40, 2.26, 2.94, 1.95 and 2.49 to 2.51 at the same sizes from 299,520 (less 399,600 and 449,334). The bar, 1.5 GB in Firefox, is first passed between 0.40 and 0.45 million: rounded to 0.25 million, 500,000. Every peak below 2 million is in the export, but 1.5 million's, in merged mode. The meter never read above PSS (3,079 samples in Firefox, 2,079 in Chromium). 1,999,982 triangles peaks above 3,011,040 in both browsers, as merged mode unions the whole 2 million triangle solid (its heap to 1.64 GB) and the export's whole import stands beside the recycled preview's: `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, section 8, for a run of its own.
- **meshoptimizer's figure and the true deviation**, in Node: Benchy to 50,000 reported 0.0073 mm where the surface moved 0.0362; the 1 million triangle block to 300,000, 0.0488 and 0.176. G4's 60,000 triangle copy: Benchy 0.0066 against 0.0362, doomedmarine 0.019 against 0.058, chick 0.0050 against 0.030, barricate_type_2 0.017 against 0.113, the 1 million block 0.146 against 0.247.
- **The copy, in Node, as first built (sampled):** Benchy to 50,000 in 1 round, 49,998 triangles, 0.0362 mm; doomedmarine to 50,000 in 2 rounds, 50,018, 0.0494 mm; war-barricade to 10,000 in 3, 34,976, 0.057 mm at one sliver; the 1 million triangle block to 300,000 in 8, 370,712; the 3 million triangle block to 300,000 in 9 rounds, 501,230 triangles at a sampled 0.0500 mm, 65 s. With the count held at the target, as first built, that block ran 8 rounds to 625,500 triangles and 0.091 mm; raising the count with the locks brought it within the samples.
- **The copy's figure against dense sampling** (the review, in Node, each copy sampled again on a grid of 6 or 16 steps per triangle of the file): Goddess Statue at 12,000 recorded 0.0499 mm and lay 0.0684 mm off; at 20,000, 0.0500 and 0.0548; war-barricade 0.0570 and 0.0772; barricate_type_2 0.0500 and 0.0666; doomedmarine at 20,000 0.0547 and 0.0553; Benchy and the chick equal. So the samples were no bound.
- **The copy, in Node, proved** (after the review): Benchy to 50,000 in 1 round, the same 49,998 triangles and bytes, proved within 0.0367 mm, the largest distance found 0.0362 (the check 0.42 s); doomedmarine to 50,000 in 2 rounds, 50,072, 0.0499 mm; the chick to 30,000 in 1, 30,000, 0.0407; Goddess Statue to 12,000 in 4, 15,548, 0.0500; war-barricade to 10,000 in 3, 42,442, 0.0496; barricate_type_2 to 10,000 in 4, 35,146, 0.0500. The 3 million triangle block to 300,000: 752,814 triangles in 10 rounds at a proved 0.0500 mm, the loop 81 s (each round about 1.7 s to simplify and 5.6 to 6.4 s to prove), peak RSS 0.76 GB. Its rounds with the count raised by two triangles a lock throughout did not converge in 12 (737,638 triangles, 0.063 mm, the triangles past the bound rising again in rounds 5, 8 and 11); four a lock throughout converged in 4 rounds at 961,930; the 1-ring of each triangle past it in 7 at 1,140,346; so the raise doubles after a round that does not halve them. Proving a 750,000 triangle copy against its 60,000 triangle decimation, as the export's clearance does, takes 1.0 s (0.2456 mm; sampled, 0.2451 in 4.2 s). Profiled, the bound first spent most of its time making small typed arrays for the cut polygons: plain arrays halved it.
- **The 3 million triangle block simplified on opening** (the new soak), as first built: the copy took 69.8 s in Firefox and 67.8 s in Chromium, 501,230 triangles; PSS peaks, Firefox then Chromium: the question 575 and 512 MiB, the copy being made 1,561 and 1,392, the preview 1,166 and 939, merged mode 1,405 and 1,171, the export 1,969 and 1,911. The meter's Simplify part read 84 MB from 8 s to 68 s of the step, while PSS grew 635 to 888 MiB over the empty app (the review).
- **The same, proved** (after the review, one browser at a time): the copy takes 95.7 s in Firefox and 87.2 s in Chromium, 752,814 triangles in both, proved within 0.0500 mm. PSS peaks, Firefox then Chromium: the question 579 and 511 MiB (the empty app, nothing read); the copy being made 1,467 and 1,546; the copy's preview 1,427 and 1,121; merged mode 1,689 and 1,387; the export, which reads the 150 MB file for `model_oriented.stl` beside the build, 2,504 and 2,410 (2,525 and 2,427 in the final soak runs), where the file loaded as it is peaks at 2,769 to 2,774 and 2,190 to 2,405 MiB. So on this block, whose ripple needs a quarter of its triangles at a proved 0.05 mm, simplifying saves 1.3 GiB in Firefox's preview (1,427 against 2,748 MiB loaded as it is), but in the export only 0.26 GiB in Firefox and nothing in Chromium, as its reading of the file stands beside the build: reading it in a worker of its own once the build is done would take that off (`docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, section 8). The meter's Simplify part now reads 115 MiB through the rounds (median of 20 readings), up to 169 in the measuring halves and 405 in Manifold's check of the copy, about a quarter of PSS's growth over the empty app; the rest is meshoptimizer's own memory, which it does not expose, the browser's own and its garbage. The meter was at or under PSS at every sample (644 in Firefox, 592 in Chromium). The soak now asserts the copy step under 1,900 MiB, the flow after it under 2,800, and the Simplify part's median through the rounds over 95.
- **Loaded anyway, with the race fixed** (the 3 million soak): PSS 2,769 MiB in Firefox (preview 2,748; MM1's runs with the race 2,966 to 3,036) and 2,405 in Chromium, and 2,774 and 2,190 in the final soak runs, no job ended at its budget in either.
- **A trim of the file**, once, in Firefox, with the copy as first built: the block simplified on opening, then stood on a face picked on its ripple, so the plane trims it, and a glue-mode ZIP: `model_oriented.stl` has 1,681,084 triangles, the export's worker reached a 2.09 GB heap in its `original` step (12.8 s) and the browser 3,787 MiB PSS, against 1,969 untrimmed. It works, and is the costliest case of the user's answer; HEAD's export of the file loaded anyway with a trim takes the same whole-solid path, and was never measured at 3 million triangles.
- **Determinism:** Benchy's copy at 50,000 has the same SHA-256 in Node, Chromium and Firefox (995c7b10…), and the 3 million triangle block's copy the same count in both browsers. The format does not depend on it (the copy is stored); README.txt's "which gives the same copy" does.
- **The file under the plane:** where the copy needs no trim, the file's points could lie up to the copy's error below the plane, untrimmed; on Benchy none does: `model_oriented.stl`'s lowest point is at 0.
- **Test time:** the new unit tests take about 17 s and the MM2 tests in the old files about 4 s when run alone with 2 workers, which in the whole suite is about 15 s more; the browser smoke tests 6.6 s in Chromium (build) and 4.6 s in Firefox: about 27 s more for `scripts/ci.sh`, within the design's 30. Benchy's copy in the browser, the `.moldproj` round trip and the text file's estimate run in the full suite (19, 5 and 6 s in Chromium). After the review's tests: the new unit files take 21 s alone and the MM2 tests in the old files 3 s, as Benchy's byte pin (15 s) runs only with CFG_FULL=1, as does the browser test of Cancel during the copy.

### GC: the clearance's error proved

Not one of the plan's milestones: the item MM2's design found at its HEAD and left for a run of its own (`docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`, section 8, the row of 2026-11-17; R08, D25). G4 measured the silicone at export on the model decimated to 60,000 triangles and took off meshoptimizer's own figure for that decimation, which on the examples is 1.7 to 6.7 times under the surface's true move, so README.txt's measured clearance of a model loaded as it is could read up to about 0.1 mm more than the silicone is, and the one raise of the margin could miss a mold that needed it. MM2 had proved that error for a simplified model's decimation alone. HEAD's exports were captured before any change.

Checks:
- **The clearance** (`decimate` and `measuredError` in `src/workers/exportJob.ts`). Every decimation G4 makes gives up its error proved over every point of the surface the gap is for (`SurfaceIndex.bound`, with a reach from 0.5 mm that doubles until it holds), in glue and merged mode, for a model loaded as it is as for a simplified one. That surface is the mesh, or past G1.8's limit in merged mode the copy of 1 million triangles the base carries, so a merged mold's figure is the silicone round what is printed (at HEAD it was taken from the whole mesh's decimation, which says nothing of the copy). The copy's mesh comes with `ManifoldPath.copy` and `importFromCopy`'s `copy`, turned as the model stands, and the export's `holding` counts it; the preview's solid worker, which imports from the copy alone past the limit, drops it, as it never reads it (the review: as first built it held it, about 18 MB on the 3 million triangle block, and the memory meter read 0.86 GB for its preview where HEAD's read 0.84). Where the model's own solid is measured (60,000 triangles or fewer, or a decimated copy that is no solid) the error is 0, as that solid is the model; past G1.8's limit it is the copy, which is the base's own in merged mode (0), and in glue mode how far the whole mesh lies from it is proved the same way. README.txt names the copy in merged mode past the limit: "between the simplified copy the base carries and the cover, measured on a lighter copy of the model, less 0.397 mm for how far the base's copy lies from it" (the review's words, as the figure is not the lighter copy's own error), or with nothing taken off where the copy itself is measured.
- **Every use of a simplifier's reported error at HEAD**, and what became of it:
  1. `decimate`: meshoptimizer's figure for G4's 60,000 triangle copy was the clearance's error for every model loaded as it is. Now proved, as above.
  2. `decimate`'s fallback: G1.8's `ManifoldPath.errorMm`, meshoptimizer's figure for the 1 million triangle copy, was taken off where minGap measured that copy itself (in use only where the decimated copy is no solid, and in the tests at small limits). Now proved in glue mode and 0 in merged mode. `ManifoldPath.errorMm` and `importFromCopy`'s `errorMm` are gone; the copy's mesh comes instead.
  3. The `mode-b-simplified` warning (`simplifyForManifold`) gave that figure as "with up to X mm of error": under Mode, in Diagnostics, and in README.txt's warnings and report.json's diagnostics of a model past 2 million triangles. It now gives no figure, the builder's choice (D25): proving it on the 3 million triangle block took 10.7 to 12.6 s and 140 to 280 MB more in Node (0.096 mm proved where meshoptimizer said 0.020), in the preview's solid worker beside a browser near 2.8 GB in Firefox, and no guarantee needs it, as merged mode's clearance is now measured round the copy. README.md's "with the error that costs said under Mode" now says that.
  4. `SimplifiedMesh.errorMm` is renamed `reportedMm` and kept for the record and the tests, as `ModelSimplified.reportedMm` of MM2's lock loop already was; nothing a guarantee or a printed figure depends on reads either. `displayProxy` drops `simplifySloppy`'s figure, as it did.
  MM2's own errors were already proved: `simplifyModel`'s `errorMm` (the copy's record, the widened margin, README.txt) and a simplified model's decimation.
- **Bytes.** Before any change, a pipeline harness in Node captured HEAD's exports: all 15 STL files in `examples/` (the two kept only on the server among them), at defaults in glue and merged mode with the fit test, jersey with eight settings changed, and each case's `.moldproj`, 739 hashes with the build's stamp held fixed and report.json less its timings (N Scale Oil Barrel's merged mode is refused at both, as its mesh is open). The working tree gives the same hashes but 16: README.txt and report.json of Benchy, the chick, doomedmarine and barricate_type_2, the four examples loaded as they are of more than 60,000 triangles, in both modes. In each only the clearance changed: README.txt's "Measured clearance" line, its figure and the error taken off, and report.json's `measured` and `decimationError`; `minGap` is `measured` plus the error, so the chick's reads 3.288992409359904 for 3.2889924093599037, its last bit, as (gap - e) + e rounds back differently for the new e. No margin is raised at defaults, so every STL, project.json and `.moldproj` is HEAD's. war-barricade (51,384 triangles), the stand (38,408) and every other example are not decimated, and are byte for byte HEAD's. The unit test's pins for Benchy, README.txt and report.json, were measured again (CFG_FULL): the old ones are HEAD's, the new ones the captures'. The 3 million triangle block in glue mode at defaults, in Node: HEAD took off 0.136 mm and measured 3.09; now 0.397 is taken off, so the first build measures 2.83, under t, and the margin is raised once, by 0.22 mm: cover.stl and base.stl change, the box grows from 129.0 x 129.0 x 33.4 to 129.4 x 129.5 x 33.6 mm, the silicone from 78 to 83 ml, the plastic from 155 to 156 g and the cover's lift from 4.5 to 4.6 N, the auto spacing and edge in README.txt's settings follow the raised build, and README.txt says the margin was raised and gives the warning without a figure; model_oriented.stl, funnel.stl and project.json are HEAD's. In merged mode the same, with 0.3965251 mm proved for the copy the base carries (0.3965260 for the whole mesh) and README.txt naming that copy. The review's changes (the preview's held copy, README.txt's words past G1.8's limit, the tests) change no example's bytes, as none is past 2 million triangles: captured again, all 739 hashes are those of the tree before them. Past the limit they change README.txt's clearance line alone: the 3 million triangle block in merged mode, exported again in Node, gives the same STLs, project.json and report.json.
- **Tests.** `test/unit/simplifiedExport.test.ts` (a rippled block of 183,600 triangles at a print margin of 0, whose decimation meshoptimizer says moves 0.082 mm where it moves 0.185: report.json, README.txt and the export's clearance give the proved figure, its first build's gap is t or more less meshoptimizer's figure and under t less the proved one, so the one raise fires, by the shortfall plus 0.05, said in the warning and README.txt, and the whole model reassembled with the cover keeps t; then in merged mode, under G1.8's limit, the same proved error, the same raise and the model's own gap, not a copy's, as the review asked); `test/regression/heavyPath.test.ts` (at limits of 4,000 and 2,000 triangles: glue mode's copy measured itself gives up the whole mesh's distance from it, proved, 0.846 mm where meshoptimizer said 0.431, in report.json and README.txt; merged mode's copy measured itself takes off nothing and README.txt names the copy, whose own gap with the cover is the one measured; a smooth block of 66,960 triangles, past 60,000, its floor 5 mm up so the plan moves it to stand (the review: every block stood as it was, so a copy left where the import has it passed), where glue mode takes off the whole mesh's error from the decimation, 0.0072 mm (meshoptimizer said 0), and merged mode the copy's, 0.0754, each never more than the gap it is for; the same block with the export's decimation made open, so Manifold refuses it and minGap measures G1.8's copy itself, where glue mode takes off the whole mesh's distance from the copy, 0.0742 mm (meshoptimizer said 0.0279), and merged mode nothing, the route that past 2 million triangles is the only one to that figure (the review)); `test/regression/heavy.test.ts` (the warning gives no figure whatever meshoptimizer reports, and the copy's mesh comes with its solid; the 3 million triangle soak proves the copy's figure and logs it); `test/unit/shells.test.ts` (the figure is kept for the record and left out of the warning); `test/unit/export.test.ts` (Benchy's new pins); `test/unit/solidStages.test.ts` (imported from the copy alone, the preview holds the import's mesh and none of the copy's arrays, which MM1's meter would count). Negative controls, each made in a copy of the tree and restored to the working tree's SHA-256: meshoptimizer's figure back for the decimation fails the rippled block, the smooth block and Benchy's pins, and with the rippled block's error checks left out, its raise (0 where more than 0.05 was due); nothing taken off where glue mode measures the copy fails the hollow block; merged mode bounding the whole mesh, or taking the whole mesh's error off the copy measured itself, fails the two merged tests; README.txt's old words fail both; the warning's figure back fails the three tests of its words. The review's, the same way: meshoptimizer's figure back in merged mode alone fails the rippled block (0.0825 for 0.1853); G1.8's copy left in the import's coordinates fails both smooth-block tests (13.1 mm for 0.0754, 5.02 for 0.0742); nothing taken off, or in merged mode the whole mesh's distance taken off, where the decimation is no solid fails the open-decimation test; the preview keeping the copy's mesh fails its held arrays; README.txt's earlier words past G1.8's limit fail the smooth block.

Measured at GC (Node on this server, nothing else running, unless said):
- **meshoptimizer's figure against the error proved**, G4's 60,000 triangle copy: Benchy 0.0066 and 0.0367 mm, doomedmarine 0.0191 and 0.0586, the chick 0.0050 and 0.0307, barricate_type_2 0.0168 and 0.1134, the 3 million triangle block 0.136 and 0.397, its 1 million triangle copy against the decimation 0.397; G1.8's copy of that block against the whole mesh 0.020 and 0.096.
- **The proof's own time** (median of 5, the model as it stands): Benchy 0.97 s, doomedmarine 1.33, the chick 0.73, barricate_type_2 0.35; the 3 million triangle block 2.3 s, its copy against the decimation 1.2 s. war-barricade is not decimated.

The 3 million triangle block once: 10.1 to 15.0 s (the clearance 3.1 to 6.4, and the field, tool and cover twice, for the raise), merged mode 12.9 to 18.8 s.
- **The 3 million triangle soak in Firefox**, loaded anyway, HEAD twice and GC once, one browser at a time: the export's worker 13.7 s at HEAD and 19.8 s now. PSS less Firefox's main process, HEAD then GC: the preview 1,806 and 1,804 MiB, merged mode 1,920 and 1,913, the export 1,800 and 1,939: 139 MiB more in the export, as the raise holds the first build while it makes the second (MM1's rule). The browser's peak stays merged mode's: 2,735 and 2,771 MiB at HEAD, 2,852 now, where Firefox's main process alone moved by up to 99 MiB from run to run; under the soak's 2,861 MiB, by 9 MiB in GC's run. That run's preview still held the copy's mesh, so its meter read 0.86 GB for the preview where HEAD's read 0.84, and 1.19 GB at its peak for 1.18 (the review). Once more after the review's changes: the preview 1,807 MiB, merged mode 1,923, the export 1,856 and its worker 19.9 s; the browser's peak 2,695 MiB, in merged mode; the meter 0.84 GB for the preview and 1.18 GB at its peak, HEAD's figures.
